# TODO
This document is the canonical source that aggregates incomplete tasks managed across the whole repository.

**How a finished item is recorded.** Do not delete a completed item. Keep it in place and append a
`- **DONE (<what closed it>, <date>).**` sub-bullet stating the evidence, so that the reasoning which
closed it stays readable next to the problem it closed. A top-level item is removed only when it turns
out never to have been a task (withdrawn or duplicated), not when it is finished. Measured over the
file's history on 2026-08-13: of the 71 commits that touch it, 42 add a `**DONE` line and 6 remove a
top-level item.

**How to read an absolute `ruff` figure in this file.** Many entries below record
`ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` as "83 errors with an identical histogram". That number is a
measurement of `origin/main` AT THE DATE OF THE ENTRY, not a live baseline: `ruff` has no
in-tree configuration here, so those runs inherited whichever default set the installed build
had, and the vendor's default went from 59 rules to 413 in one minor release (0.16.0). The same
command on the same unchanged tree reports over a thousand findings today, most of them
stylistic. Read every such figure as of its date. The convention that still has a fixed referent
is the PER-FILE `ruff check --statistics` DELTA against `origin/main` (the form used in the
entries dated from 2026-08 onward); prefer it when adding a new entry. Driving this repository's
own findings to zero is deliberately not a task — the rewrite would itself be the second defended
class of `AGENTS.md` §Development premises, and the delta convention already catches the finding
classes that matter. This paragraph is the residue of issue #120, whose measurement surfaced it;
the linter PRESET of the same name is a different subject entirely.

## TODO list

- **Issue #143 downgraded one `Generate.verify` finding class from `major` to `minor`, and the reason now lives only here** (2026-09-03, informational — not work, a record). `skills/workflow-generate-verify/SKILL.md:42` (the inert-dependency-call item: an invented tolerance, a validity flag folded into an output, a branch on a dependency result) carried an inline `` (`major`) `` until issue #143 removed it. That is a real routing change: in `dev`, `major` terminalizes the run and `minor` warm-repairs the producer. It was removed because the defect is one `Generate.generate` can fix from the same `spec.ir.yaml` and `tests.md`, so the hand-assignment contradicted the repair-route rubric the same line now points at (`docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` §2-2). The reason was written into a test docstring that a later round replaced, and round 3's disclosure axis found it stated nowhere else. **If an inert-call violation is ever seen exhausting `MAX_ATTEMPTS_PER_PHASE` on repeated warm repairs, the answer is not to restore `(major)`** — it is that the rubric's lead question was answered wrong for that class, which is a rubric change, not a hand-assignment.

- **`Tune.verify` has no `issue_severity` rubric** (2026-09-03, low, MEASURED at `7144404` — enumerated while fixing issue #143; issue #148 closed the `Compile.verify` half and left this one). `skills/workflow-tune-verify/SKILL.md:22` states only the `dev` routing consequence of `major|critical` and assigns no value by hand. The conductor does not drive `Tune` (`tools/workflow_conductor.py` names only `compile` and `generate` in its same-phase repair branch), so the repair-route axis the two core phases use (`docs/workflow/phases/phase_01_compile.md` §1-2, `phase_02_generate.md` §2-2) has no `minor` route to name there yet; extending it requires stating first which repair a `Tune.verify` `minor` calls for.

- **The diagnostician's severity rubric is a twin with no coupling test** (2026-09-03, low, MEASURED at `7144404` — enumerated while fixing issue #143, out of its scope because the two rubrics are not the same rule). `skills/workflow-escalate/SKILL.md:52-60` and `tools/workflow_conductor.py:847-871` (`_DIRECTIVE_SCHEMA` / `_ESCALATE_PERSONA_FALLBACK`, whose own comment says "Keep this fallback and the SKILL's persona in lockstep") state the same three values in prose, twice. **They do NOT grade on the same axis as the `Generate.verify` rubric, and an earlier version of this entry said they did** — measured at `0caaf52`: the escalate SKILL says "Grade how compromised the existing artifacts are" and `_DIRECTIVE_SCHEMA` says "severity grades how disruptive the defect is", which is the weight-of-consequence axis `docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` §2-2 explicitly refuses ("the value does not grade the consequence the defect would have at `Validate`"). Their `major` rows point opposite ways: the verify rubric's means no producer re-run reaches the defect, the diagnostician's means reuse by default. So the follow-up is TWO questions, not one — whether the twin needs a coupling test, and whether the two personas should grade on one axis at all — and only the first is cheap. `test_diagnosis_prompt_renders_escalate_skill` (`tools/tests/test_workflow_conductor.py:7123-7136`) pins a substring only, so a rewritten SKILL rubric that disagrees with the schema ships green. Not folded into issue #143's coupling check because the diagnostician's rubric ties severity to `repair_strategy`, which a `verify` verdict does not carry: one check would have to assert two different things.

- **`audit_orchestration`'s report-wide loaders have no "must not swallow" pin, unlike the dangling-launch path** (2026-09-02, low — found by the issue #130 review loop, round 5 security axis; NOT reachable today). `tools/audit_orchestration.py:72-95` `_load_jsonl_with_errors` and `:102-106` `_load_json_if_dict` produce every count in the report. MEASURED at the round-5 HEAD: wrapping `_load_jsonl_with_errors`'s `read_text` in `try/except Exception: return [], []` leaves both test files green (164 passed) while producing a completely clean report — all counts 0, `parse_errors` 0, `diagnostic_failures` empty, `orchestration_found` True, exit 0. **The shipped code does not swallow there** (a read error propagates out of `audit()` as a traceback, exit 1 — fail-loud), so this is unpinned-but-correct behaviour, not a hole. What it records is that issue #130's invariant is pinned on the dangling-launch path only, and the report's own numbers are the wider surface: a regression there makes the WHOLE report a false record, not one section. The fix is one pin of the same shape as `CollectorsDoNotSwallowTests` — inject at `Path.read_text` and require it to reach `audit()`'s caller.

- **`skills/workflow-audit-claude/SKILL.md` Step 2 enumerates AGENTIC leaves only, and says nothing about it** (2026-09-02, low, MEASURED — surfaced while fixing issue #137; the fix for it was attempted on that branch and REVERTED, see the sub-bullet). Step 2 builds its session-id set from `payload_summary.session_id` in `native_hook_events.jsonl`. A pure `claude_cli` leaf runs under `--safe-mode` with no settings layer, fires no hook, and writes no row there, so its session is never looked up. MEASURED over `workspace/orchestrations/` with `os.walk` (`workspace*/` is gitignored, so a `grep` honouring ignore files misses all of it), taking "pure" as `tools/pure_leaf.py`'s own predicate (`leaf_mode == "pure"` in `launches/<arid>.request.json`): **23 orchestrations hold pure leaves, 64 pure leaves in total, and 0 of their `agent_run_id`s appear in ANY `native_hook_events.jsonl`**, while agentic ids do (239 agentic launches, 91 matched). So a "found N sessions" result from Step 2 is a count of the run's agentic leaves, not of its leaves — on `orch_20260827T105454Z_99f63b7e`, 2 sessions found out of 8 pure + 2 agentic claude leaves. Two independent reviewers re-derived 23/64/0, and so did the author separately.
  - **Why the branch that measured this did not fix it, which is the part worth keeping.** Issue #137 wrote the limitation into Step 2 with an instruction for reaching a pure leaf anyway (take the arid from the launch record; the conductor pins `--session-id <agent_run_id>`). The next round measured that note wrong in three ways, and the whole edit was reverted. (a) The mechanism is `claude_cli`-only: of the 64 pure leaves, **48 are `claude` and 16 are `openai_compatible`** — an HTTP provider that launches no CLI, for which `--safe-mode` is not the reason, no transcript exists anywhere, and the recovery instruction can never succeed. A pure CODEX leaf is a third shape and is launched with `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, i.e. hooks ON. (b) `launches/<arid>.request.json` carries no backend key at all (it is in the sibling `.response.json`), so the note named an artifact that cannot tell the shapes apart. (c) "the transcript exists" is false for most of the corpus: **8 of the 64 are present under `~/.claude/projects` and 56 are gone**, all 8 from the newest run — the CLI expires sessions, which the note did not hedge. Three rounds running, that branch's edits to this document were measured false, each written from evidence about the AUDIT TOOL rather than about this SKILL's procedure. **What closes this is measuring Step 2 against all three pure shapes and the retention window, which nobody has done** — not another sentence written from adjacent evidence.

Making a STRONG claim would need the rule stated once over the whole module rather than over a function list — e.g. deriving the function set from the call graph under `build_launch_incident` and the primitive set from what those functions actually call, so both lists stop being hand-maintained. Within `tools/ skills/ docs/` the only other occurrence is `tools/tests/test_audit_orchestration.py:1180`, which FABRICATES the key in a fixture — which is what makes the format string look produced.

- **`_load_json_if_dict` degrades a corrupt `orchestration_meta.json` to `{}` in silence** (2026-09-02, low — found by the issue #130 review loop, security axis, which correctly declined to raise it as a finding on that branch; pre-existing and out of that diff). `tools/audit_orchestration.py:102-106` swallows `OSError` / `JSONDecodeError` and the caller does `meta = ... or {}`. That failure reaches NEITHER of the two integrity channels: `data_integrity_warning` counts jsonl parse errors only, and `diagnostic_failures` (added by issue #130) records only what the three best-effort handlers catch. MEASURED at that branch's HEAD by writing `{ not json` into a fixture's meta: exit 0, no warning section, and the token-cost and pure-leaf sections silently degrade to "unavailable". **This is issue #130's own class — a clean negative over something not measured — reached by a third route**, which is why it is recorded rather than dropped; it was left out of that branch because the branch had already grown one scope increase (the missing-orchestration route) and a second would have been the "fixes calling for fixes" shape. **Severity**: an operator reads a degraded section as a measured one; a leaf gains nothing.
  - **DOWNGRADED by measurement (2026-09-02, after PR #135 merged).** The reachability this entry left open was "a crash mid-write truncates the file" — the scenario the dangling-launch audit exists to diagnose, which would have made it the most reachable of #130's residues. **It is not reachable that way.** MEASURED at `ff3d7a9`: all **15** writers touching `meta_path` in `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` go through `_write_json` (`:3151-3152`), which is `_atomic_write_text` — `tempfile.mkstemp` in the same directory then `os.replace`, added precisely so "a concurrent reader can transiently observe an empty or truncated file mid-write" cannot happen (`:3109-3118`). Zero non-atomic writers. So a corrupt `orchestration_meta.json` needs real storage corruption or a hand edit, and `AGENTS.md` §Development premises puts a path only the operator can reach out of scope. **What remains in scope is the shape, not this file**: the swallow reaches neither integrity channel, so if a NON-atomic writer of some other `_load_json_if_dict` input is ever added, the same silence returns. Fix it when something makes it reachable, not before.

- **Two siblings still carry the import shapes issue #130 removed** (2026-09-02, low — found by the issue #130 review loop; the security axis located the first, the disclosure axis the second. Neither was verified to have a fail-open route). **Both halves of #130's shape survive elsewhere, and an earlier version of this entry filed only the first, which made the omission arbitrary rather than measured.**
  - **The bare-first shim.** `tools/prune_workflow_homes.py:81-100` is the same `try: from orchestration_runtime import ... except ModuleNotFoundError: from tools.orchestration_runtime import ...` shape that `tools/audit_orchestration.py` had. Two things are unmeasured here and the entry is deliberately not stated more strongly than that: whether any caller's `except` can swallow a deferred `tools.` import in that module's reachable paths, and whether the dual module identity (`orchestration_runtime` and `tools.orchestration_runtime` as two objects) is reachable in the script path. `AGENTS.md` §Development premises and `.claude/skills/atmofab-review-loop` both say a sibling gap whose measurement you do not have is the honest thing to leave: issue #130's evidence covers `audit_orchestration` and `orchestration_diagnostics`, not this file. What would settle it is one run of the same witness that settled #130 — drive the script path with `PYTHONPATH` dropped over a fixture where the answer is not empty, and dump `sys.modules`.
  - **The deferred `tools.` import.** `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` — the very file whose bootstrap #130 copied — has FOUR function-body `tools.` imports, MEASURED 2026-09-02 by an AST census over the file: `:1026` `tools.dependency_graph` in `_dependency_resolution_freshness`, `:15946` `tools.validate_workspace_root` in `post_phase_complete`, `:19136` and `:24195` `tools.llm_config` in `probe_all_providers` and `main`. None sits inside a `try` in its own function, so there is no fail-open at the import site itself; whether any CALLER's `except` can swallow one is exactly what is unmeasured, and is the same honesty standard the bullet above applies. `tools/tests/test_audit_orchestration.py::_deferred_tools_imports` is the scanner that would answer it — it is applied to `tools/orchestration_diagnostics.py` only.
  - **DONE — NOT an instance of the class, measured 2026-09-02 at `ff3d7a9`, after PR #135 merged.** The entry deliberately said what was unmeasured; measuring it retires both halves. It is kept rather than deleted because "this shape exists here" is still true and the next reader will otherwise re-file it.
    - **The shim does not produce a dual identity.** Ran `tools/prune_workflow_homes.py` through `runpy` with `PYTHONPATH` dropped and `sys.path[0]` set to `<repo>/tools` — the exact shape of a direct script run — then dumped `sys.modules`. Result: `orchestration_runtime` is loaded under the BARE name only, 14 `tools.*` modules are loaded, and the set of names loaded under BOTH spellings is **empty**. The reason is structural rather than lucky: the bare `orchestration_runtime` bootstraps the repo root itself at module level, so everything IT imports is already `tools.*`, and nothing re-imports it under the dotted name. Limit of the measurement: the run was `--help`, so only module-level imports executed — which is what decides identity, since they all run before argparse.
    - **The deferred imports cannot fail the way #130's did.** `tools/orchestration_runtime.py:92-97` bootstraps the repo root at MODULE level, so by the time any of the four function bodies runs, `tools` is importable. #130's mechanism was a missing root, and that root is never missing here. A genuinely absent module would still raise, but that is a different and much weaker risk than the one this entry was filed for.
  - **What is left is a style inconsistency, not a defect**: the shim is the spelling `tools/audit_orchestration.py` moved away from, and converting it would make the two agree. Not worth a change on its own.

- **The `cppcheck` lint gate applies a severity its own declaration does not name** (2026-09-01,
  low — documented but NOT measured; found by the issue #120 review loop, witness-census axis,
  by pulling on a smaller finding). `tools/backends/linter/cppcheck/lint.py` declares
  `ENABLED_SEVERITIES = ("warning", "style", "performance")` and the argv passes them to
  `--enable`. The tool's own `--help`, identical on 2.7 / 2.16.0 / 2.17.1, documents `style` as
  enabling "all messages with the severities `style`, `warning`, `performance` **and
  `portability`**". So the severities the gate APPLIES are a superset of the severities it
  DECLARES, which is the one place in that backend where the declaration is known to be narrower
  than the behaviour.
  - **Not measured, and that is why it is here rather than fixed.** No source constructed during
    the review produced a `portability` finding on any supported build, so the difference between
    the two sets has no observation behind it — only the vendor's text. Writing `portability` into
    the constant on the strength of a `--help` sentence would be declaring something nobody has
    seen, which is the failure this backend's own §Requirements is about.
  - **What would settle it**: construct a source that produces a `portability` finding (the
    `--errorlist` entries with that severity are the candidate list), then either add the
    severity to the constant or pass `--enable` a set that excludes it, and record which.
  - Measured and pinned in the same review: `--enable=style` ALONE gives a verdict identical to
    the three-name list on all three builds, so two of the three declared members are redundant.
    They are kept deliberately — a declaration should name what it applies — and
    `test_the_severity_list_is_redundant_and_that_is_measured_not_assumed` fails if a build stops
    subsuming.
  - **DONE (issue #120 review loop round 3, 2026-09-01).** The `portability` gap this entry asked
    someone to settle was settled the round after it was written, by two reviewers independently:
    `sizeof(*p)` and `p + 1` on a `void *` report `sizeofDereferencedVoidPointer` and
    `arithOperationsOnVoidPointer`, severity `portability`, exit 2, under the DECLARED argv on
    2.7 / 2.16.0 / 2.17.1, with `--enable=warning,performance` clean as the negative control.
    `ENABLED_SEVERITIES` now names `portability`; the verdict is unchanged, because `style` was
    already enabling it, and the constant now says what the gate applies.
    `test_a_portability_finding_reaches_the_verdict` witnesses it.
    - **The lesson is about the RECORD, not the tool.** This entry was written the same day, in the
      same review round, saying "no source constructed here produced a `portability` finding on any
      supported build" — which was true of the searcher and read as a property of the tool. Six
      lines of C settled it. An unmeasured claim written into three files and a ledger entry is a
      claim someone will act on; the cost of one more construction attempt was five minutes.
- **`preset=mixed` refuses its own lint gate on a node whose tree holds nothing one sub-preset can
  analyse** (2026-09-01, low — reproduced, unreachable today; found by the issue #120 review loop,
  correctness axis, as a `route not established` item and reproduced here). `mixed` runs
  `fortitude` then `cppcheck`. Measured on Cppcheck 2.7 and 2.17.1: over a directory holding only
  `main.f90`, `cppcheck` exits 1 with `could not find or open any of the paths given`, which its
  `unusable_invocation_reason` correctly classifies as a REFUSAL, so
  `_raise_on_unusable_lint_invocation` raises a transport `fail_closed`. That is reachable on a
  `mixed` node in two places: a `src/` that happens to hold no C, and — always —
  `_attribute_lint_findings`'s HOST-AUTHORED copy, whose basenames on such a node are Fortran.
  - **The previous argv did not avoid this**; it made it quieter and wronger. `--error-exitcode=1`
    made the same exit 1 indistinguishable from findings, so the failure was routed to the leaf as
    defects in its own source. Issue #120 did not introduce the case, it made it legible.
  - **The fix belongs to the composite, not to a backend**: a sub-preset should be skipped over a
    tree holding nothing it can analyse, which is a change to `tool_run_linter`'s composite branch
    or to `_gate_lint_check`. Both are existing machinery issue #120 does not touch.
  - Unreachable today: `_validate_toolchain_backend_supported` refuses `language: mixed` on every
    non-`infrastructure` node and the corpus is `fortran` throughout. Recorded in
    `docs/backends/linter/cppcheck/RULES.md` §Limits.

- **Two ways a `Generate.gate` lint step reports exit 0 over source it did not read, on the
  linter that is actually reachable** (2026-09-01, low — reproduced, pre-existing; found by the issue #120 review loop,
  security axis). Measured on this host: `chmod 000` on a subdirectory of `project_dir` gives
  `fortitude` `warning: Encountered error: Permission denied (os error 13)`, `0 files scanned, 1
  could not be read`, **exit 0**, and `ruff` the same shape. `cppcheck` is the exception (exit 1,
  which its `unusable_invocation_reason` classifies as a refusal). So a lint gate can report a
  pass over a tree it could not read, more quietly than any channel the declared invocations do
  close — the tool says it failed to read something and then reports success.
  - **Not fixed here, and the ground is the gain, not the mechanism.** A leaf that hides a source
    from the linter has hidden it from the compiler too: `_write_makefile` pins its sources by exact
    top-level name — `<spec_id>_model` and `_runner`, plus `_checks` on the nodes whose runner the
    conductor authors, so TWO or three depending on the node, and never a glob (an earlier
    version of this sentence said "the three" unconditionally; the count is wrong and the
    load-bearing half — that the template never globs — is not), so a file the walk
    skips is a file that never builds, and the leaf is no closer to reporting its `substep` done.
    Making the whole `src/` unreadable fails `Build` for the same reason.
  - **What would close it** is a caller-side check rather than a flag: no linter in the supported
    set has one. The gate would have to refuse a run that reports zero files scanned over a
    directory the conductor knows is non-empty. That is a change to `_gate_lint_check`, in
    existing machinery issue #120 does not touch, which is why it is recorded rather than done.
  - **The SECOND way, found a round later and sharing the fix but NOT the ground** (2026-09-01,
    issue #120 review loop round 3, security axis): a symlinked directory whose target lies
    OUTSIDE the walk root is not entered. Measured on all four ruff builds and on `fortitude`:
    `warning: No Python files found` / `0 files scanned`, exit 0; a target INSIDE the root is
    followed, and a symlinked FILE is followed either way. `cppcheck` fails closed.
    **The gain argument above does not transfer**: a `chmod 000` directory is hidden from the
    compiler too, but a symlinked directory COMPILES, so what bounds this one is the leaf's write
    authority — it would need a link target outside `project_dir` — and not the toolchain. That
    makes it the stronger of the two and the reason this entry now names both.
  - Both are recorded in `docs/backends/linter/ruff/RULES.md` §Design Policy as measured
    non-closures, and pinned as measured behaviour by
    `test_an_unreadable_directory_is_a_measured_NON_closure` and
    `test_a_symlinked_directory_outside_the_root_is_a_measured_NON_closure`, so a build that
    starts failing closed is noticed rather than assumed. One caller-side check closes both:
    refuse a run reporting zero files scanned over a directory the conductor knows is non-empty.

- **What issue #102 left unexercised and un-gated** (2026-08-26 — disclosure, not a plan).
  - **No orchestration has ever been run against it.** Twelve commits, five review rounds, 5278 tests and three live-CLI measurements, and not one leaf launched: no conductor loop, no `record-launch`, no gate, no `--resume`, no `bwrap` profile rendered. The change alters the entrypoint every leaf's every tool call goes through and makes it fail closed, so the modality nobody ran is the one carrying the remaining risk. What lowers it: `leaf_config/claude/settings.json` is byte-unchanged, `leaf_config/codex/hooks.json` is byte-identical to the file it moved from, the prepared leaf homes are sha256-identical to `origin/main`'s, and `preflight` returns the same verdict for every check on both backends against both revisions. What it does not cover: anything that only appears once a leaf is running under the sandbox.
  - **No preflight executes the leaf hook chain any more.** The roster probe used to seed the leaf's `hooks`, so a hook that exited non-zero failed that gate; it cannot any more, because the same block removes the `orchestration_id` the refusal keys on (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py`, and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §0-2's roster bullet). `_probe_claude_leaf_config` replaces it structurally only, by string equality on the wrapper command. So an import-time defect in `tools/hooks/cli.py` would switch every policy off for a whole run with no preflight seeing it. The suite catches that class today — `tools/tests/test_hooks_dev_cli.py::DevCliWrapperCommandsExecute` and the two leaf-wrapper tests in `test_hooks_cli.py` execute the committed wrapper strings — but the runtime gate does not. Restoring one means adding a gating preflight check, which is machinery with its own review.
  - **Merging re-prompts for codex hook trust, once.** `.codex/hooks.json` changed content, and the CLI keys its trusted digest on that path (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` §0-3). The prompt is expected; it is not a sign that anything is wrong.
  - **Every `Suite NNNN passed, 0 skipped` line in this branch's commit messages holds only in a checkout two levels below `$HOME`.** Elsewhere the same revisions give three fewer passes: two `ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests` failures and one skip, all issue #84 and all unrelated to the change. The collected totals reproduce anywhere. The condition is stated here because a message cannot be edited after the fact, and the same applies to `c3585f0`'s mutation baseline of 1832.
    - **DONE for the two failures (issue #84, 2026-08-26).** The `ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests` pair no longer resolves `../..` against the checkout, so those revisions' totals now reproduce at any depth. The one SKIP was left in place then, as a declared environment capability (`tools/tests/test_skip_reasons_are_declared.py`) — a test that says out loud it does not apply here, which is the opposite of a failure belonging to no change. It is gone since 2026-09-02: the row it guarded now builds its own `$HOME`, so it applies everywhere and the capability was removed from the declaration table with it (see the depth entry below).

- **The suite no longer answers a question about the machine it runs on** (2026-08-26 to 08-27, issue #84 — measured, closed with residuals). **Every commit this entry pins a figure to predates the `met-dsl` -> `atmofab` rename, `165c26f` included: to re-take any of them, spell the environment names `METDSL_*` at that commit — the current spelling matches nothing there (`docs/GLOSSARY.md` §13).** Two independent couplings, both pre-existing and neither a product defect; what they cost is a reviewer's round, and on PR #81 they cost one — 152 failures reported as a branch regression when 143 were the branch's.
  - **The operator's ambient environment was 20x larger a surface than the issue said.** The issue named `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ID` + `ATMOFAB_CHILD_AGENT_RUN_ID` and 9 failures. Measured on `165c26f` over the whole suite: seventeen `ATMOFAB_*` names, plus `CODEX_HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, give **181 failed / 356s** against a clean **5280 passed / 114s**. (Seventeen is what one grep spelling over one file set returns and it was written down as "the `ATMOFAB_*` names the tree reads"; a reviewer counting differently got 23, a third enumeration gets 25. No count is stated in the code any more — the property it was standing in for is checked instead, today by `test_every_environment_name_the_tree_reads_is_stripped_or_declared`. Two later attempts to state a count were ALSO wrong; see the round-3 bullet. Of the five figures this branch has quoted, only two carry their method and are reproducible: `_environment_names_read_by` returns **27**, and **21** with its constant resolution removed. **17** was `grep -rhoE 'environ(\.get)?[(\[]"[A-Z_]+"' tools mcp_servers` — which includes `tools/tests/` and reads only a literal argument. **23** and **25** were reviewers' enumerations whose method was not recorded when their reports were consumed, so they cannot be re-taken; that omission is the reason no count appears in the code.) Attributed on `test_orchestration_runtime.py` alone (1242 tests, 20s clean): `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ENFORCE_LIVE_PREFLIGHT=1` **84 failed and 482s** — the tests then run the real probes — `CODEX_HOME` 10, `ATMOFAB_HOME` 3, `ATMOFAB_ENFORCE_REPLY_BUDGET` 1, the other ten 0. So the pair in the issue was 5% of the surface and did not include the expensive member. `tools/tests/conftest.py`'s `pytest_configure` now removes those names before collection (before collection, because a module body that reads the environment at import runs earlier than any fixture), BY PREFIX from `orchestration_runtime.LEAF_ENV_ALLOWED_PREFIXES` so a knob added later is covered without anyone remembering the file. After: **5287 passed / 111s** under the same poisoning, identical to the clean run.
  - **The path-depth coupling passed in the wrong place as well as failing in it.** `test_blocks_bash_only_tilde_prefixes` spelled the escape `~+/../../.claude.json`, which is `$HOME` only in a checkout two levels below it; in `/tmp/wt84` it failed. `test_directory_options_anchor_like_cd`'s `../..` case did NOT fail there — it was blocked on a different policy than the one it means to exercise. Both now derive the route with `os.path.relpath(home, cwd)`. The witness moves the process to two temporary checkouts (depth 1 and 6, outside `$HOME`) rather than using a synthetic `repo_root`: `~+` is `$PWD` and `tools/hooks/common.py` expands it from `os.getcwd()`, so a synthetic root leaves that anchor pointing wherever pytest was started — the first version of the witness failed in a worktree for exactly that reason and was testing nothing about depth.
  - **The CPU budget was load-sensitive even though it already used CPU time.** `time.process_time()` was chosen over wall clock for this, and it is not enough: CPU time itself inflates under contention. Measured on `165c26f`, 22 cores: the two blocks cost 1.13-1.21s / 0.78-0.82s solo, 1.40-1.48s / 0.92-1.01s under three concurrent pytest runs, and 2.30-2.41s / 1.85-1.91s under 44 spinners — 2.1-2.5x of movement against a 5.0s bound (a separate prototype run reached 4.29s; issue #84 reports 9.36s, their measurement). The bound is now relative to a calibration workload measured around the block. **The reason first given for that — a compensation of "1.4x of spread instead of 2.5x" — was refuted in round 1 and deleted; read the round-1 bullet below before this sentence, not after it.** What justifies the bound is a bracket with both ends measured: above every figure ever observed for the block (34.3 units) and below the smallest regression the family ever caught (8x a ~22-unit baseline = ~176 units). Bounds are 110 and 80 units.
  - **The four verdicts, taken**: primary checkout clean **5287 passed**; primary checkout fully poisoned **5287 passed**; a `/tmp/wt84` worktree and a `/tmp/a/b/c/deep` one **5286 passed, 1 skipped** each; that worktree poisoned as well **5286 passed, 1 skipped**. The single difference between the checkouts is the declared `"needs the checkout under $HOME"` skip, which is a test saying out loud it does not apply — the opposite of the failure this closes. Before: the same worktree gave **1 failed, 5278 passed, 1 skipped**, reproduced three times on `origin/main`.
  - **Round 1 (two axes, 2026-08-27) found four things worth the round, all fixed.** (1) HIGH, and it was the branch's own witness: `tools/tests` has no `__init__.py`, so pytest imports the conftest as module `conftest` while `from tools.tests.conftest import ...` re-executes the same file as a SECOND module object. The hook populated one copy's record and the witness read the other's — empty on every host, including the poisoned one it was written for — so its primary assertion iterated nothing, and a mutant that records a name's value without stripping it survived. The rule, the table and the record now live in `tools/tests/suite_env_guard.py`, a plain module reached only by its dotted name; `CONFIGURED` makes a reader that somehow gets a second copy skip loudly instead of asserting over an empty record. (2) The strip was SILENT, which is a check recorded as run and not run: `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ENFORCE_LIVE_PREFLIGHT=1` was 84 failures and 482s of real probing on `165c26f` and became 1242 passed in 49s with nothing probed. Reported in the session header now, with `--keep-operator-env` to mean it. (3) The two calibration self-tests flaked under contention at a TIGHTER margin than the bound they were introduced beside — 2/200 and 9/200 violations under 44 spinners. Split by property: one pins the SCALE with bounds ~3x outside the measured union spread, the other pins PROPORTIONALITY as a paired ratio in the same process; 0 violations in 150 solo + 80 loaded samples after. (4) `_CpuUnits.__exit__` returning True — one word — makes all eight bracketed blocks unconditional passes, including the assertions inside them. It survived both sweeps; it has a witness now.
  - **This branch shipped a mutant for two commits, and the witness that caught it was nearly silenced.** A mutation applied in a `git worktree` — the reviewer's own "record the name but do not strip it, for a `SUITE_OWNED_ENV` name" — was not reverted, because the file was UNTRACKED there and the `git checkout` meant to undo it did nothing and said nothing. A later `cp` of that worktree's files into the checkout carried it in, and it was committed at `4c2b588`. It then survived a whole-suite run, a poisoned whole-suite run and a 24-mutant sweep, because the sweep's own re-application of it reported `PATCH-FAIL (count=0)` — the tell was there and was read as an anchor typo. What did fire was the poisoned run's `test_no_ambient_operator_name_survives_into_a_running_test`, correctly; it was misread as a value collision (the poison value happened to equal the suite's own `setdefault` value) and the first "fix" was an exemption that would have silenced it for good. Two rules paid for here: `git checkout -- <path>` is banned for reverting a mutation for a second reason beyond discarding uncommitted work — on an untracked file it is a silent no-op — and when a test fails, read WHY before deciding it is the test's fault. What stands in place of the discipline now is `strip_operator_env` verifying removal at the point of the strip and raising, so the shape cannot be present and quiet; the exemption that remains is narrowed to the one case that is genuinely undecidable by value, and only when the values actually collide.
  - **Round 1 also refuted the reason this change gave for its own budget, and the fix was to delete the reason.** The claim was that a calibrated quotient compensates for load, "1.4x of spread instead of 2.5x". A reviewer re-measured in FRESH processes and got the opposite — within one heavy batch, seconds moved 1.36x, the calibration denominator 3.02x and the quotient 2.36x. Both are true and they are different comparisons: ACROSS load levels the quotient does help (mine: seconds 5.5x, units 2.1x; theirs: 5.0x and 3.0x, agreeing on direction), WITHIN one load level it costs, because a ~0.1s denominator does not average out the way a 1-10s numerator does. Measured further: that is not sampling noise and more samples do not fix it — mean-of-2, median-of-5 and median-of-9 estimators all spread 1.8-2.0x across fresh processes, quiet and loaded alike. The bound is therefore justified now by a BRACKET with both ends measured — above every figure ever observed (34.3 units) and below the smallest regression the family ever caught (8x a ~22-unit baseline = ~176) — and not by a mechanism. The absolute bound it replaces had neither end: the reviewer measured that block at 12.21s against its own 5.0s bound.
  - **And it found the same defect live in six siblings, one of them dead.** Six more CPU budgets in the same file carried the identical rationale comment and were left absolute. `test_brace_expansion_is_bounded_no_dos` computed `time.process_time() - t0` with `t0 = time.time()` — a wall-clock epoch — so its difference was about -1.79e9 and no input could ever fail it. Pre-existing, at `origin/main` too. All eight are on the calibrated bound now; the six small ones measure 0.0-1.0 units, which is also not the "~1s of CPU for these shapes" their old comments claimed.
  - **A reviewer profiled the calibrator's justification and it was false.** "The SAME KIND of work the hook does — compiled-regex scanning" — `cProfile` puts `re.findall` at 2.2% and 3.9% of the two blocks, and every one of those calls is the calibrator itself; one block is syscall-bound (114,647 `lstat`) and the other is hand-written character loops. The docstring says what the quotient was measured to buy instead of arguing from a resemblance.
  - **Round 2 (three axes — blank-slate, previous-round, and a sonnet recomputation pass — 2026-08-27).** The recomputation axis returned ZERO mismatches over every re-runnable number in the branch. The previous-round axis found no second carried-over mutant, no test weaker than its `origin/main` counterpart and no `def`/`class` lost in the hand reapplication, which is what that axis existed to check. Four defects between the other two, all this change's own:
    - **The `--keep-operator-env` flag added in round 1 failed one test on a CLEAN host** — `1 failed, 5293 passed, 1 skipped` with no knob set at all. `pytest_configure` returned early before setting `CONFIGURED`, and the witness reads that flag to detect "the hook never ran". So the escape hatch added to stop a silent strip re-introduced a failure belonging to no change, which is this branch's whole subject. `decline_strip()` now sets `CONFIGURED` (a fact about the hook running) separately from `DECLINED` (a fact about policy); with the flag AND a knob it is the ratchet that fails, which does belong to the knob.
    - **The check that replaced the deleted count was TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION.** It harvested `ATMOFAB_*` literals with a regex and asked whether a `ATMOFAB_` prefix rule covers them. Both other axes found it independently: adding `GEMINI_CONFIG_DIR` or `ATMOFAB2_NEW_KNOB` to the tree left it green, and its own docstring claimed it could not go stale. What can go stale is the HAND-WRITTEN half — `CODEX_HOME` / `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` carry no prefix, so a third spelling is covered by nothing. The question asked now is the one that can fail: every name the tree READS must be stripped or declared in `MUST_BE_INHERITED`. Read by AST including reads through a module constant (six names in this tree are spelled that way, and a literal-only reader misses all six — one of the reasons three hand-counts disagreed). Verified to fail on three uncovered names and to pass on two controls.
    - **The module-scope-read detector wrongly refused legitimate code.** A `def` nested inside a module-level `try: … except ImportError:` was reported as a module-scope environment read; both files under test already have such blocks, so it was one ordinary edit away. Its self-test had exercised only a top-level `def`, which is the shape that cannot catch this. The reader now excludes function and class bodies at any depth and self-tests both directions, nesting included.
    - **`CONFIGURED` is process-global and a sibling test left it up.** The synthetic drive of `strip_operator_env` set it as a side effect and restored only the record, so the cross-module-split detection could be satisfied by another test rather than by the hook, depending on execution order. Restored now.
  - **Round 2 also corrected the record.** `tools/tests/conftest.py` still stated "the 17 `ATMOFAB_*` names the tree reads" four commits after the branch claimed to have removed that count from every document, and `TODO.md`'s own first CPU bullet still carried the compensation reason that a later bullet in the same commit says was refuted and deleted. A reader stopping at the first bullet got the retracted claim. Both fixed; the commit message that asserted the removal cannot be, and is wrong on that point.
  - **Recorded, not fixed** (both measured by reviewers, neither a branch regression): `test_multi_wildcard_glob_no_dos`'s 40-unit bound catches the regression it names — removing the lexical fast-path, measured at 0.123s CPU per pattern and ~75 units for the block — with only 1.9x of margin, and on a host with a smaller `$HOME` it would not catch it at all; `origin/main`'s 5.0s bound was worse. And the narrowed `SUITE_OWNED_ENV` exemption in the witness is an EQUIVALENT variant of the unconditional one — verified over four poisonings, identical verdicts in every case, because the assertion it skips passes exactly when the exemption does not fire. It is kept for what it states, and no test can distinguish the two.
  - **Round 3 (two axes — the previous round's fixes, and blank-slate — 2026-08-27). Both found the same thing independently, and it is the third consecutive round in which one mechanism was wrong.** The module-scope-read detector was added in round 1 (excluded only top-level `def`/`class`, so a fallback `def` inside a module-level `try:` was refused), corrected in round 2 (excluded those NODES, and thereby stopped seeing a class body, a decorator argument, a default argument and a class base — all of which execute at import — while still refusing a module-level `lambda` body, which does not), and measured wrong in **five of eight shapes** at `3052aa3`. The two files it scanned hold 29 class-body assignments, 10 decorators and 20 lambdas, so neither error was hypothetical; a reviewer drove the miss end to end and got the operator's `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ID` cached at import with the guard's own witness reporting the tree clean. It also scanned 2 files while the hook's import pulls in 15, so an ordinary module-level constant in any of the other 13 was invisible.
    - **The answer was to change the question, not to write a third reader.** `.claude/skills/atmofab-review-loop` says at three rounds on one mechanism to suspect the question cannot be answered at that level of analysis. Deciding "what runs at import" from source is that question. The check now IMPORTS the module in a fresh interpreter with `os.environ` replaced by a recording mapping and asserts nothing of ours was read. It needs no grammar, covers all 15 modules for free, and sees two things no static reader can: a name assembled at runtime, and a read another module makes on this one's behalf. Verified against every shape the AST readers got wrong — class body, decorator argument, default argument, plain read in a previously unscanned module — and against the two that must NOT be refused, the module-scope lambda and the `def` inside a `try:`.

That is round 1's defect, live in a second form, and the escape hatch added for it unreachable. `pytest.ini` gains `testpaths = tools/tests`. `pytest .` still bypasses it, because an explicit argument overrides `testpaths`; recorded, not defended.

    - **The calibrator — this change's central mechanism — could be replaced by the number it usually returns.** `elapsed = 0.05` passes every bracket; only an implausible constant like `1.0` was caught, while the record listed "a calibrator returning a constant" among the killed.
