Pinned by COUNT — two at default verbosity, one under `-q` — and the header is not redundant: it is the only producer that survives `-p no:terminal`.
    - **The citation ratchet added in round 4 refused this repository's own vocabulary.** `test_id` (42 files), `test_profile_version` (30), `test_predicates` (27) are spec/IR FIELD names; the ratchet reported them as tests that do not exist and told the maintainer to rename something with no other name. Over-refusal is this author's recorded default error direction and it arrived in a new shape on the ratchet's first round. A citation is dangling now only if it is not a test, not a test module, and not part of the tree's vocabulary; the two names this branch actually deleted are still reported.
    - **The breadth block did not pin the root it names.** All four anchors are also read under `tools/`, so dropping `mcp_servers/` from the walk survived. `PYTHONPATH` is the anchor for that root now, being one of only two names read solely there.
    - **The depth witness was one-sided, and blind exactly where sweeps run.** `..` clamps at `/`, so a route anchored on the repo root instead of the process's directory still lands on `$HOME` and blocks; the mutant survives in a `/tmp` worktree and fails only in the primary checkout — the branch's own subject, inside the branch's own depth witness. The route VALUE is asserted now, not only the policy.
    - **`isolated_record` did not restore `DECLINED`**, latent because nothing calls `decline_strip`; now restored and witnessed.
  - **Round 5 also found a refusal whose remedy did nothing.** The import spy subtracted a hand-written allowlist, so ANY import-time read was refused — including `HOME`, which the guard never strips and about which nothing can be cached wrongly — and the message told the reader to declare it in `MUST_BE_INHERITED`, where it already was. The rule now asks the guard whether it would strip the name, which removes the over-refusal, the misleading instruction, and the allowlist itself.
  - **Two records were wrong in the other direction.** `README.md` carried "nineteen such names" — a sixth uncheckable count, in the most-read document, added by the commit whose subject is that uncheckable counts rot. And residual (e), added one round earlier, was wrong in BOTH halves: `testpaths` does not capture a bare `pytest` from a subdirectory (measured: collection is byte-identical with and without it), and `pytest .` is no longer silent, because round 4's terminal-summary line fires from a non-initial conftest. Two reviewers in the same round measured that entry the opposite way; the version that reproduces is now recorded.
  - **And round 5's own fix broke a round-2 witness, found by the sweep that followed it.** Adding the `DECLINED` block to `test_driving_the_strip_leaves_no_global_state_behind` put a second `isolated_record()` after the first, and `__enter__` clears the record — so dropping the record RESTORE became invisible: the later `__enter__` cleared the residue the closing assertion would have seen, and the mutant that had been killed since round 2 came back green. A sentinel is written into the outer block's record now, so an outer state of `{}` is no longer indistinguishable from a missing restore. **This is the sixth consecutive occasion on which a fix broke or failed to observe something the previous round had closed**, and it is the reason the budget ends the search rather than a claim that nothing is left.
  - **Seventh, same shape, from the next sweep.** The rule round 5 introduced to fix the import spy's over-refusal — "only a name the guard would strip can be cached wrongly" — lived as an expression inside the test that consumes it, where on this tree the answer is the empty set and the assertion is green whatever the rule computes. Making it return nothing survived. It is `suite_env_guard.import_reads_that_would_be_stripped` now, with a witness driven in both directions: a stripped name must be reported, and a name in `MUST_BE_INHERITED` must not, which is the over-refusal the rule replaced.
  - **FINAL STATE at `1212e62`, measured in the primary checkout** unless said otherwise. Suite **5304 passed, 0 skipped**, clean and under a poisoning of every `ATMOFAB_*` name plus `CODEX_HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` alike; **5303 passed, 1 skipped** in `git worktree`s at depth 1 and depth 6 outside `$HOME`, the one skip being the declared `needs the checkout under $HOME`. `origin/main` in the same worktree gives 2 failed / 5277 passed / 1 skipped. `ruff check --statistics` per touched file is identical to `origin/main` (3 F401 in `test_hooks_common.py`, 1 E741 in `test_hooks_cli.py`, both pre-existing; the two new files are clean). Mutation, 37 mechanism mutants over `suite_env_guard.py` / `conftest.py` / `pytest.ini` / the two test files: **33 killed, 4 survivors, 0 unresolved**. The four are the standing kinds and each is annotated where it sits — the two budget constants, which cannot pin themselves; the second depth of the route witness, measured redundancy rather than a second pin; and the breadth assertions on the environment-name walk, where the regress stops because an assertion inside a test is not a mechanism.
  - **THE LOOP STOPPED AT ITS BUDGET, NOT AT CONVERGENCE.** Round 0 plus five, which is the cap. What was met: class descent (HIGH -> MEDIUM -> one mechanism wrong three rounds running -> a disclosure round with no functional defect), a disclosure round spent BEFORE deciding to stop rather than after, and two independent measurements that nothing this branch touches reaches a leaf. What was NOT met: the security axis never produced nothing for two consecutive rounds, and no blank-slate reviewer has returned zero functional defects even once — round 5's returned five unwitnessed mechanisms. **Seven consecutive times, a fix either arrived without a witness of its own or broke something a previous round had closed**, the last of them found by the sweep run after the final commit. That is the reason the budget ends the search; it is not a claim that nothing is left.
  - **Still open at the cap, recorded rather than fixed**: `-p no:terminal` gives a silent strip (0 disclosures; no documented invocation uses it, and it is the reason the header was kept rather than folded into the summary); `_Spy.copy()` does not record, so a module-scope `os.environ.copy()` would be invisible to the import spy — 20 occurrences in the tree, all inside function bodies, none at import scope; and `_last_import_reads` keys by name, so a name read at two sites reports one location.
  - **One HIGH finding of this branch's own reached no commit message**, and a commit message cannot be fixed afterwards. `_CpuUnits.__exit__` returning True — one word — makes all eight bracketed CPU-budget blocks unconditional passes, every assertion inside them included, and it survived two sweeps before being witnessed. It landed inside `85b4c8d`, whose subject line is about six sibling assertions and a dead one, so a reader of `git log` alone never learns a suppress-everything fail-open was found on this branch. It is recorded here because this file is the place that can still hold it.
  - **Mutation, round 0**: hunk mutation does not apply — the whole change lives in test files and a conftest, where reverting an added hunk deletes an assertion. Mechanism mutants instead, run with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 -p no:cacheprovider` (a same-byte-length rewrite inside one second otherwise reuses a stale `.pyc` and scores the PREVIOUS mutant). At `5bbf925` 15 mutants gave 9 killed and 6 survivors; three of the survivors were real gaps and were closed — the two-sided calibration averaging, the `SUITE_OWNED_ENV` ratchet (which lived as an expression inside the witness, so mutating it mutated an assertion; it moved into `tools/tests/suite_env_guard.py` beside the rule), and the unverified half of the depth claim. 16 mutants gave **12 killed, 4 survivors** at that point. **That figure is superseded — read the per-round sweep line below for the current one.** The sweeps since: at `3052aa3`, 33 mutants / 28 killed / 5 survivors, the survivor list having CHANGED (the four breadth assertions on the environment-name walk entered it; the end-to-end control's own assertion left, having been given a witness). The survivors have been the same four kinds throughout: the two budget constants, which cannot pin themselves; the second depth of the route witness, redundancy over route length rather than a second pin (measured: either depth alone kills the `../..` mutant); and assertions inside a test, which are not mechanisms — the regress stops there.
  - **Superseded, kept for the reasoning**: the first sweep was seven mutants, all caught — the route reverted to `../..`, a calibrator returning a constant, a calibrator ignoring its repeat count, an inverted quotient, `pytest_configure` made a no-op, the backend-home names dropped, and the prefix hard-coded instead of read from the constant. `tools/tests/test_suite_environment_isolation.py` carries the environment half, including a three-way end-to-end control (poisoned under pytest passes; the same test poisoned under plain `unittest`, which loads no conftest, must FAIL, or the first result proves nothing).
  - **RESIDUAL — five things this does not do.** (a) `env -i` is NOT covered. `PATH`, `HOME` and the locale family are still inherited, and the claim measured here is about the named surface only. (b) A budget CONSTANT cannot pin itself: widening `110` to `100000` survives the suite, exactly as widening `5.0` did. (c) Four test modules — `test_orchestration_runtime.py` and the three `test_pure_leaf_*` — set `ATMOFAB_DEP_READINESS_ALLOW_PERSISTED_FALLBACK=1` with a module-level `os.environ.setdefault`, which is process-global and therefore visible to every test collected afterwards. Harmless today (each module sets it itself, so nothing depends on another module having done it) and left alone, but now DECLARED: `SUITE_OWNED_ENV` in `tools/tests/suite_env_guard.py` is a ratchet that fails if a new process-global environment dependence appears without someone naming who sets it. It also survives `restore_operator_env`, which puts back only what it stripped — so an in-process pytest run leaves that one name behind in its caller's environment. (d) `tools/tests/test_hooks_cli.py`'s two id-popping helpers are now the second wall under pytest and were kept, not deleted — they also run under plain `unittest`, where no conftest is loaded. (e) `pytest.ini`'s `testpaths = tools/tests` is honoured ONLY when pytest is invoked from the rootdir. `pytest .` passes an explicit argument and bypasses it, so `--keep-operator-env` is still an argparse error there — though the strip is no longer silent, since round 4's terminal-summary line fires from a non-initial conftest. A bare `pytest` from a SUBDIRECTORY is unaffected: measured from `workspace/`, collection is byte-identical with and without the `testpaths` lines. **Both halves of this entry were wrong when first written** — it claimed the subdirectory case was captured and that `pytest .` was silent — and two reviewers measured it the opposite way in the same round; the version above is the one that reproduces.

- **Two rough edges the issue-#102 review named and did not fix** (2026-08-26, review round 1 — measured).
  - **The isolated codex home's SHA-256 re-verification cannot fail.** `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` re-hashes the hook file it has just written and raises on a mismatch, but the bytes compared are the bytes the same call wrote from the same in-process buffer, so no reachable input reaches the branch. Neutering the check to `if False:` survives the suite (measured, 2554 passed, 0 new failures). It is defence in depth against a future path that writes and re-reads separately; it is NOT a covered check, and the docstring does not say so. Pre-existing, untouched by #102, and a leaf gains nothing from it either way — the file is the one the host just certified.
  - **The DEV codex hook file has no shape check any more.** `codex_project_hooks_validated` moved to `leaf_config/codex/hooks.json` with the leaf, so nothing validates `.codex/hooks.json`'s structure beyond `HookLayerSeparationTests` asserting it names `tools/hooks/dev_cli.py`. That is deliberate — the file governs the operator's own session and `AGENTS.md` §Development premises puts a defense against the operator out of scope — but it means an operator who breaks that file learns it from their own session rather than from preflight.
  - **Operational, stated rather than fixed**: a claude leaf that has somehow lost `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ID` gets rc 2 on `Stop` as well, which blocks the stop rather than ending the leaf. That is the fail-closed direction and the conductor's own timeout is what ends such a leaf, but it is the one refusal whose shape an operator reading a stuck run should recognise.

- **The DEV layer's hooks and the LEAF layer's hooks are to be fully separated** (2026-08-26, issue #102 — decision taken, not started). Only the settings FILE is separate today: measured on `7560edc`, `.claude/settings.json` and `leaf_config/claude/settings.json` register 9 hook entries each, the two sets are identical down to the sha1 of every command, and all four distinct commands run `python3 -m tools.hooks.cli` out of the checkout. What separates a leaf from an operator is the environment the hook reads, not the file that registered it. Four statements landed by issue #82 rest on that sharing and become false the day the separation lands — `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` §39's third case, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and the ambient branch's comment in `tools/hooks/cli.py`; issue #102 lists them with the acceptance criteria.
  - **DONE (issue #102, 2026-08-26).** Both layers separated to the entrypoint. DEV (`.claude/settings.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`) names `tools/hooks/dev_cli.py`, which imports the standard library and its rule modules and nothing else (`operator_safety.py` at the time this was written; `dev_session_hygiene.py` joined it later — `docs/HOOKS.md` is canonical for the current set); LEAF (`leaf_config/claude/settings.json`, the new `leaf_config/codex/hooks.json`) names `tools/hooks/cli.py`, which now fails closed on a missing `orchestration_id` with no policy variable — `ATMOFAB_MISSING_ORCHESTRATION_ID_POLICY` and its writer are deleted. The codex half was not optional: `.codex/hooks.json` was BOTH the leaf's source and the project hook layer an operator's own codex session loads (the trust digests in `~/.codex/config.toml` are keyed on its absolute path), so an unconditional refusal would have refused that session. The four sites this entry names are revised, and the import boundary is pinned by reading the source rather than by observing a loaded module.

- **The isolated backend homes are durable, and their retention is manual** (2026-08-20, issue #64 — measured). Both preparers created the home with `tempfile.mkdtemp(dir="/tmp")`, where it holds the leaf's ONLY conversation record (the claude transcript, the codex rollout) and a host restart took it — a billed run stopped being auditable the moment the machine rebooted. The homes are now `<homes-root>/<orchestration_id>/<backend>`, rooted at `operator_secret_root()/homes` so the Bash read guard's unconditional refusal of `~/.atmofab` covers every orchestration's home whether or not the per-orchestration resolver can name it. `tools/prune_workflow_homes.py` is the only thing that removes one; nothing automatic exists or will (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` §"The operator-private root" is canonical, and `test_nothing_is_wired_to_invoke_this_tool_automatically` samples for a caller appearing later).
  - **Follow-up, deliberately not done here: no preflight check that the homes root is creatable.** A `workflow_homes_root_creatable` probe would surface an unusable `~/.atmofab` at launch instead of at the first leaf, which is the ordering this repository prefers (`REQUIRED_CLI_TOOLS` exists for exactly that reason). It was skipped because it ripples through roughly 30 launchable-preflight fixtures for a case the first launch already fails closed on, by name and with the path in the message — `_create_workflow_backend_home` raises a `ValueError` naming the ancestor it could not create or could not verify. The cost of the current behaviour is that the failure lands after preflight rather than at it, on a run that has not yet spent anything.
  - **A new failure mode, stated rather than hidden.** The leaf directory is created EXCLUSIVELY, so a crash between that `mkdir` and the metadata write leaves an unrecorded directory that refuses the next launch. That is the fail-closed direction — adopting the directory would hand a new orchestration an earlier run's transcripts — and the refusal names the path and the remedy — which is NOT one command: `--allow-unverifiable --delete` for a run whose metadata is gone, but a terminalize (the `set-status` block under `docs/RUNBOOK.md`
    #launch-incomplete-recovery) then a plain `--delete` for the crash case described here, whose `owner.json` is present and valid so the entry is refused as non-terminal instead. An earlier version of this sentence, and of the message itself, prescribed the first for both.
  - **One latent hook defect surfaced with the move and was fixed.** The unresolved-expansion fallback in `_command_reads_protected_host_path` called `_blank_persisted_tool_results` without an orchestration id, so it resolved only the operator's `~/.claude/projects` and did not recognize a persisted tool-result inside the ISOLATED home — refusing the leaf the reading of its own gate output. Latent since issue #63 and reachable then only through `$(…)`, because a `/tmp` home has no `${HOME}` spelling to trigger the branch; ordinary once the home moved under `$HOME`. Measured across all four spellings, with the credential file, another leaf's transcript and a sibling orchestration's home as controls.

- **The leaf's configuration surface AND its environment surface are closed** (2026-08-19; environment face 2026-08-20, medium — measured). Issue #63's final form closed the configuration face: an agentic claude leaf launches with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<private per-orchestration home> --setting-sources user`, whose only settings layer is a SHA-256-pinned, read-only-remounted copy of the committed `leaf_config/claude/settings.json`. What that did NOT close, and what the 2026-08-20 work below closes, is the process environment the conductor passed through from the operator's shell: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` and the `CLAUDE_CODE_*` family reached the leaf and could redirect its traffic or change which model runs. Naming the face precisely matters — the 2026-08-19 work closed the *configuration-file* surface, not "the leaf's surface".
  - Completion criterion: `_child_env` reconstructs the child environment from a declared allowlist rather than inheriting, with the inventory (`PYTHONPATH` / `PATH` / locale / proxy variables included) written down and each entry justified; until then `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` keeps naming this as the open face.
  - **DONE (environment face, 2026-08-20).** `_child_env` now reconstructs from `orchestration_runtime.LEAF_ENV_ALLOWLIST` — a dict whose VALUE is the per-entry justification, so an entry cannot be added without writing one — plus `ATMOFAB_*` by prefix minus `ATMOFAB_HOME`. Two justifications for that prefix were written and review falsified both — "every member has an in-tree reader" (`ATMOFAB_MISSING_ORCHESTRATION_ID_POLICY` is seeded into every leaf and read by nothing) and "the namespace is repo-owned so nothing an operator set lands inside it" (nothing enforces that; `leaf_env_from({"ATMOFAB_ANYTHING": "x"})` forwards it). What holds is the weaker claim: the names known to redirect a leaf are outside the prefix by construction. The residue is accepted and named in the code — an operator-exported `ATMOFAB_*` value is forwarded and persisted into the leaf-readable profile, with the one recognisable credential case (a declared `api_key_env`) dropped first. It absorbs the former `_safe_host_env_for_child` verbatim, including its `/usr/bin:/bin` PATH default. bwrap enforces it structurally: `--clearenv` in the fixed head, then one `--setenv` per declared name, so `profile["env"]` — persisted at `sandbox_profiles/<arid>.json` and referenced by `sandbox_profile_ref` — became the TRUE record instead of the 7-recorded/2-applied gap it was. No new launch-record field (`feedback_no_redundant_persistence`): the profile is the one record, threaded to `record-launch` over stdin rather than argv so the values are not readable in `ps`.
    - **The proxy / TLS families are EXCLUDED by name, and that is the decision the criterion's "proxy variables included" asks the inventory to cover** — not an omission. `HTTP(S)_PROXY` / `NO_PROXY` / `ALL_PROXY` (both cases), `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` are listed in `LEAF_ENV_NAMED_EXCLUSIONS` with the reason next to them: a proxy URL routinely carries credentials and this list's values are persisted verbatim into the profile and the rendered argv, and their absence fails LOUDLY at egress rather than silently redirecting. An operator on a proxied host adds them there with a justification. Named consequence: `llm_http_leaf._env_proxies` now finds nothing, so an HTTP leaf does not honour a host proxy either — and, found in review, the HTTP **preflight** reachability probe calls `urllib.request.urlopen` bare, so it still reads the process-global environment and still goes through the operator's proxy. On a proxied host preflight therefore passes while the leaf call it vouches for cannot reach the same endpoint. Recorded in `tools/llm_http_leaf.py`'s module docstring rather than fixed: making preflight bypass the proxy too would make it probe an endpoint no leaf uses, and making the leaf honour it means putting a credential-bearing proxy URL back on the persisted list. The decision belongs with whoever needs a proxied host.
    - **Measured, not inferred.** (a) CLI 2.1.235 through an unbilled capture harness: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` redirects 100% of a leaf's traffic; `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` decides the model **only when the launch passes no `--model`** — with `--model opus` on the argv the request carried `claude-opus-5` and the variable was ignored. The prior note said it "overrides the stamp", which is wrong; the exposed case is the repo's default unpinned launch, which is the common one. (b) The CLI runs under the stripped inventory: `claude -p` completed, a `sh -lc` hook fired and found `git` via PATH, and `build_runtime_server.py` came up and answered a workflow-mode refusal byte-identically to a full-environment control. (c) bubblewrap 0.11.0: `--clearenv` wipes what EARLIER `--setenv`s set, so its position in the fixed head is load-bearing; and bwrap adds `PWD` itself, the one name in the child the profile does not name. A live micro-witness runs the rendered argv against `/usr/bin/env` under a poisoned parent and compares the child's real environment to `profile["env"]`.
    - **Defect this change found in itself.** `ATMOFAB_*` passes by prefix, so an operator whose HTTP `api_key_env` was named `ATMOFAB_..._KEY` would have had the credential forwarded to every leaf and, for a CLI leaf, written verbatim into the persisted profile. Every name the configuration DECLARES as an `api_key_env` is now dropped before the per-kind deltas, and only the launching HTTP entry's own is put back. The first version of that test used a name outside the prefix and passed while the hole was open.
  - **Found while reviewing this, NOT fixed here (pre-existing); BOTH HALVES ARE GONE SINCE — see the DONE entries below.** `ATMOFAB_MISSING_ORCHESTRATION_ID_POLICY` WAS set by `tools/run_workflow.py` into every node's environment while `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` §39 described it as a live mechanism, and **nothing in the tree read it** (measured by an `os.walk` scan, since the interactive `grep` here respects `.gitignore`). Either the hook lost its reader or the document describes a mechanism that never existed. It is out of scope for this PR — the allowlist forwards it either way, and the environment face is what this PR closes — but the document should not keep asserting a live policy that no code applies.
    - **DONE (issue #82, 2026-08-26).** Neither branch of the either/or: the mechanism existed and was removed. `785c717` (2026-04-25 12:17) created the `strict` reader, the writer and §39 together; `ec52a09`, four hours later, dropped the reader and replaced the missing-id branch with an unconditional ALLOW returned before any policy ran, leaving the writer and §39 in place. The reader was restored for the workflow path (`strict` -> refuse; workflow mode without the policy -> evaluate as `_global`; a call outside a run allowed without evaluation, because an operator's session ran the same entrypoint). **That three-way contract lasted one commit**: issue #102 below separated the layers, so the operator no longer reaches this entrypoint, the refusal became unconditional and the policy variable was deleted with its writer. Measured with the dev layer's own argv, controls both ways: the four mutants that restore the removed behaviour are all killed. §39 was made canonical for that contract (it now states the unconditional one), and the two sites that claimed the sync test makes an operator's session enforce the leaf's policies (`CLAUDE.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`) say what it does and does not mean instead.
  - **Still open, named rather than fixed.** The deterministic substep's `merged_env = os.environ.copy()` (`mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`, reached in-process via `_run_deterministic_substep`); the `sh -lc` login shell inside the sandbox sourcing the operator profile and re-exporting names (measured on this host: only `PATH` comes back, rewritten by `/etc/profile` + `~/.profile`, so a hook's PATH is the profile's rather than the allowlisted one); `run_workflow.py`'s own `base_env = dict(os.environ)`, which is the DRIVER's environment and is now filtered on the way to a leaf but not at its source; and the conductor's host-side `env=self.env` subprocesses, which are the host's own tools, not leaves.
  - **DONE (configuration face only, issue #63 final form, 2026-08-19).** Measured on CLI 2.1.235 with an unbilled capture harness (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` pointed at a local server that saves request bodies): hooks in the private home's `user`-tier settings fire (both `UserPromptSubmit` and `PreToolUse` sentinels observed); `--setting-sources user` also stops this checkout's `CLAUDE.md` — and the `AGENTS.md` it `@`-imports — from reaching the leaf's first message (control: `project` and the CLI default inject it, 96,852 B, versus 96,430 B under `user`, identical prompts; and, launched with the production argv in the real checkout, the leaf's request carried zero bytes of either against a `project` control that carried both); the build-runtime grant is honoured with NO trust seed present (`tool_dispatch_end … outcome=ok`) in a session where a control command was refused, so the permission layer was demonstrably alive; and `--resume` finds this home's sessions while failing against a different home.
  - ~~**Not fixed here, deliberately: the suite leaves a private home per claude launch test under `/tmp`.**~~ **DONE (issue #64).** The homes moved to `~/.atmofab/homes/<orchestration_id>/<backend>`, and `tools/tests/conftest.py` now points `ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_HOMES_ROOT` at each test's `tmp_path` (with a session guard that raises if a prepared home lands in the operator's real secret root), so the suite leaves nothing behind anywhere. The measurement this entry recorded — 2026-08-19: ~1,000 `atmofab-claude-*` per full-suite run against ~5,700 pre-existing `atmofab-codex-*`, 22 MB total — is kept as the record of what the leak was, not as a current figure.
  - **Two consequences worth keeping in view.** (a) A leaf's transcript moved out of `~/.claude` into the private home, so every consumer that locates one now goes through the canonical `tools/hooks/common.py::claude_leaf_projects_roots` — including `_claude_session_resumable`, where a stale spelling would have silently degraded every reuse-repair to a cold launch with no error anywhere. (b) A vanished home rotates and bumps `claude_workflow_home_generation` rather than bricking `--resume`. **Issue #64 has since made the home durable** (`~/.atmofab/homes/<orchestration_id>/claude`, with the codex twin beside it) and the rotation path survives unchanged: the re-created home is now at the SAME path, so what distinguishes the generations is the integer, never the location. The trigger is an operator prune or a lost filesystem rather than a `/tmp` sweep. Retention is indefinite and manual — `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §"The operator-private root".

- **Migrate the backend knowledge that is still inlined in the neutral core** (2026-08-14, high — measured, structural). The rule is now written and mechanically bounded (`docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md`, `tools/backends/registry.py`, `tools/tests/test_backend_boundary.py`); what remains is paying the debt the rule made visible. **The previous claim that language-specific code was already separated was wrong**: the only declared backend covered signature parse/render, four other concerns were hard-coded in neutral-named modules, and the `language` axis was the only one anyone had named at all.
  - **DONE (structure + rule + ratchet, 2026-08-14).** `tools/backends/<axis>/<backend_id>/` with a per-axis registry whose declared axes `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Definitions lists (not repeated here: the list has one owner, compared against `registry.AXES` by test); `tools/backends/language/fortran/{lines,structure,structure_differential,signatures}.py` moved out of `tools/` — behaviour-preserving for every caller (same module alias, no usage site changed), with four code edits inside the moved files themselves: `structure_differential.py`'s `REPO_ROOT` depth and its own import of the relocated `structure` module, one error-message path in `structure.py`, and `signatures.py`'s import of the relocated `lines` module (this count has been corrected twice — 2, then 3, then 4 — each time by a reviewer diffing the blobs rather than by me re-reading them). The two infrastructure signature gates in `validate_pipeline_semantics.py` take their refusal clause from the registry instead of spelling the implemented set themselves. `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` canonical + `docs/GLOSSARY.md` §11 + `AGENTS.md` §"Backend boundary rules". Measured in the primary checkout: suite 4693 passed / 0 skipped (4651 collected before the branch); `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 errors on both sides, unchanged, identical histogram. NOTE on the suite figure: a checkout outside `$HOME` shows 2 pre-existing environment-dependent failures (`test_hooks_common.py::ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests`) and 1 skip on `origin/main` and on this branch alike; the collected totals match either way.
  - **DISCLOSURE — defects this change introduced and review caught.** (a) Routing the two signature gates through `registry.unsupported_reason` created a latent fail-open: that function answers `None` for a member declared with `module=None`, which is the state most members are in, so registering a second `language` member would have stopped the gates refusing while the renderer under them was still a hard-coded Fortran import. Reproduced by registering `Backend("language", "cpp", None)`. Fixed by splitting membership (`unsupported_reason`) from usability (`unavailable_reason`) and having the gates ask the second. (b) The first ratchet scanned `docs/*.md` non-recursively, so moving a document into any new `docs/<subdir>/` lowered the debt figure without anything reaching a backend — demonstrated with `CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md`. Fixed by making every glob recursive and widening the scope to `mcp_servers/` declarations and the root documents. (c) The direct-import reader recorded imported SYMBOLS as modules, so importing one more symbol from an already-allowlisted module failed the pin with a message about modules. Fixed by collapsing each dotted name to the longest prefix that is a real module. (d) `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` claimed no neutral gate needs editing when a backend is added, while `_validate_toolchain_backend_supported` 140 lines below the gate it described still spells `(make, fortran)`. Fixed by scoping the claim and listing the gates it does not hold for (area below). (e) The `parallel` axis declared a closed vocabulary against a knob whose schema states it is deliberately not a whitelist, and the `linter` axis listed one preset where the live gate accepts four. (f) The fix for (a) closed only half the hole: `unavailable_reason` proves a backend package exists, not that the §5.1 helpers will USE it, so a second language backend with a real module still fell through to the hard-coded Fortran renderer. Two independent round-2 reviewers found this. The gates now go through `_signature_backend_refusal`, which refuses on two separate grounds with separate messages, and `_SIGNATURE_HELPERS_BACKEND_ID` is pinned by set identity against the language backends the validator actually imports. (g) `registry.get` raised an EMPTY message for an open-vocabulary value with no record, and classified it as `UnsupportedBackend` while `require_available` called the same input `BackendNotExtracted`. (h) The direct-import reader resolved module names with `importlib.util.find_spec`, which IMPORTED 20 neutral-core modules as a side effect and turned any module-level failure anywhere in the neutral core into a boundary-pin error; a reader documented as static now resolves by filesystem path. (i) A per-alternative probe over the token classes found that `\bg\+\+\b` and `\bclang\+\+\b` could NEVER match — a word boundary needs a word character on one side and `g++ ` has none — so two `compiler-driver` alternatives were dead; five more alternatives and three `\s+` flexibilities were unobserved, killed only by whatever the corpus happened to contain. (j) The registry ground of `_signature_backend_refusal` was unpinned: deleting it left the suite green and made a NON-MEMBER language receive the other ground's message, which asserts that language has an extracted backend. (k) The refusal compared an un-normalized `language` against the constant while asking the registry a normalized question, so a caller passing `Fortran` would have produced a false `Compile fail`. (l) The axis list was restated in three documents with nothing comparing them to `registry.AXES`; a sixth axis left all three stale silently. One owner now, compared by test. (m) `--write-baseline` rewrote the PINNED direct-import allowlist as well as the sampled counts, and the sampled check's own failure message tells the maintainer to run it — so the prescribed remedy for the sample laundered the pin. Measured: 14 of the 60 preceding commits shed a sampled token and would have asked for a regeneration. The allowlist now lives in its own file that no command writes. (n) `skills/<skill>/backends/<axis>/<id>.md` is the placement table's fourth backend location and was scanned as neutral core, so performing this ledger's own `skills` migration was reported as GROWTH with a message saying to move the file where it already was — and that area's "counts drop" acceptance was unreachable, since the 191 occurrences never left the scanned set. (o) The "Adding an axis" procedure omitted "register at least one `Backend`", so following it verbatim left the suite red. (p) The claim that the canonical §Definitions sentence is the only restatement of the axis list was false in four places, two of them added by this branch (`README.md`, `docs/README.md`), because the guard read two named files for one comma spelling. It now enumerates every markdown file in the tree and keys on the names, not the separators. (q) The direct-import pin was NOT a set identity: `_module_prefix` collapsed a name whose directory does not exist yet — `tools.backends.build_system.make` — all the way to `tools.backends`, which is a registry module and therefore filtered out, so a bypass into any of the FOUR UNEXTRACTED AXES, exactly the set this ledger is about, was invisible. (r) Relative imports were skipped on the stated ground that they cannot leave a package; `tools/` is a namespace package containing `tools/backends/`, so `from .backends.build_system.make import RULE` crosses the boundary without leaving it, and it was executed to confirm. (s) An unparseable neutral module returned the empty set from the reader, so a file that does not parse — where an unread import would sit — left the pin silently. It raises now. (t) `_is_backend_location` tested prefixes, not the placement table's `<axis>/<backend_id>` SHAPE, so `tools/backends/scratch.py`, `docs/backends/notes.md` and `skills/x/examples/backends/y.md` were excused; moving a 76-token contract to a flat path under a backend root took the total 2584 -> 2508 and stayed green with no backend created. (u) The scanned file set and the token-class list were observable only through the regenerable baseline, whose failure message says to regenerate — so narrowing the scope (89 occurrences, or 13 files) or deleting a class with its probe failed once and passed forever. Both are hand-pinned now. (v) `\\bclang\\+\\+` was subsumed by `\\bclang\\b` and deletable for free, and the test that promised a probe per alternative was counting them. (w) The three headline guards of the previous round — the `_module_prefix` axis floor, relative-import resolution, and the unparseable-module raise — each REVERTED with the whole suite green: the fixes worked and nothing observed them. Measured over the whole instrument: of the 20 decisions that round added, 15 were unwitnessed, while the decisions it inherited improved from 20 unwitnessed to 11. The class of defect was not in the rule's form but in writing new code without its controls. (x) Pinning token-class NAMES left the alternative level open: dropping `\\bgcc\\b` from `compiler-driver` together with its probe shed 13 occurrences, failed once with the regenerate-me message, and passed forever after. The pinned file now carries the RULES — globs, exclusions, backend roots, class patterns — instead of the file list they produce; pinning the produced list had also made adding an ordinary new module a scope failure, a false rejection on routine work. (y) `importlib.import_module(name=...)` required positional args and was unread, and `__import__(\"literal\")` — a builtin, announced by no import statement — was not read at all, while the documents called the pin complete over four spellings. Both are read now; an importer obtained indirectly and a computed name are pinned as limits instead of implied to be covered. (z) `direct_backend_imports(root)` accepted a root and resolved module names against `REPO_ROOT` anyway, and the docstring claiming otherwise was the second version of that claim. (aa) The stale-baseline message said \"no longer in the scanned set\" for a file that was still scanned and had merely shed every sampled token, sending a maintainer after a scope change that had not happened. (bb) Making the document globs recursive had no witness — no `docs/` subdirectory outside the old globs exists, so reverting the fix stayed green. `ScannedSetTests` drives the scanner against a synthetic tree instead of this one.
  - **The measured baseline, 2026-08-14 (re-measured after the gates area closed: 2,575 across the same 48 files; the figure below is the state the ledger was written against)** (`tools/tests/data/backend_boundary_baseline.json`): **2,598 sampled token occurrences across 48 neutral-core files** (the two added files are `tools/backends/registry.py` and `tools/backends/__init__.py`, which are neutral infrastructure inside the backend tree and are now scanned rather than excused by a prefix), and **7 backend-module crossings in 3 modules** (`validate_pipeline_semantics.py` 4, `orchestration_runtime.py` 2, `runner_renderer.py` 1). The token figure is a SAMPLE of an enumerated token list, not a count of violations — see `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Enforcement. Regenerate with `python3 -m tools.tests.test_backend_boundary --write-baseline`; the numbers here are this commit's measurement and go stale as areas migrate, so re-measure before quoting them.
  - Area ledger, largest first. Each area's acceptance is the same: its knowledge lives under `tools/backends/<axis>/<backend_id>/`, the neutral core reaches it through the registry, its baseline counts drop, and its allowlist entry disappears.
    - **Gates that still spell their own implemented set.** `_validate_toolchain_backend_supported` and the two `make`-quality-check gates in `validate_pipeline_semantics.py`, the `make ∧ fortran` authorship conjunction in `workflow_conductor.py:5584`, and `codegen_bundle.LANGUAGES` / `LANGUAGE_EXTENSION_ALLOWLIST` / `COMPILER_SELECTOR_FAMILIES_BY_LANGUAGE`. Until these consult the registry, "add a backend without editing a gate" holds only for the two signature gates. This area is listed first because it is what makes the documented procedure true, and it is cheap relative to the source-reading areas below.
      - **DONE (capability dispatch, 2026-08-14).** The registry gained the question these sites actually have. `unsupported_reason` (membership) and `unavailable_reason` (extraction) were joined by `unimplemented_reason` (declared **and** implemented, wherever the code lives) and `provides(axis, value, capability)` — then defined as *the neutral core does this named job for this value*, a wording the runner-emission area below SUPERSEDES (it is now the union over `core_provides | backend_provides`, i.e. this repository does the job wherever the code lives) — with `CAPABILITIES` declaring `control_file`, `build_execute`, `runner_render`, `syntax_check`, `lint`, `parallel_directives`, checked at import so a typo cannot answer `False` forever. Wired: `_validate_toolchain_backend_supported` (per-axis capability, one clause per axis, refusal text taken from the registry verbatim), the three `make`-quality-check sites via one shared `_make_quality_check_applies` (three sites in two polarities — an earlier version of this sentence said four, the same miscount that was corrected in the code docstring and left here), `_LINT_ALLOWED_PRESETS` (deleted; the gate asks `registry.unimplemented_reason` per value, so the accepted set has one owner instead of two agreeing copies), and the conductor's `_conductor_authors_makefile` / `_conductor_authors_runner` / `_write_makefile` via `_core_authors_control_file`. `codegen_bundle`'s per-language tables moved to `tools/backends/language/fortran/bundle.py` and are read through `registry.load`; the bundle schema keeps its own enum/pattern and `test_codegen_bundle.py` fails on divergence. Measured at the END of the review loop, which is the only figure worth recording: suite 4693 -> 4701 (implementation) -> 4711 (review round 1 fixes) -> the figure in the final commit; `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` 83 errors with an IDENTICAL histogram throughout; sampled baseline 2,598 -> 2,575. An earlier version of this line quoted 4701 and was not updated when the fixes landed — the suite figure moves every round, so it is stated as a progression rather than as one number that goes stale.
      - **A design correction found mid-implementation, recorded so it is not undone.** The first plan routed these sites through "is this value implemented?". That is FAIL-OPEN, and worse than the hard-coded pair it replaced: the conductor's control-file writer and runner renderer each emit one build system's and one language's text, so the predicate would answer True the day a second build system was implemented AS A BACKEND and route its nodes into the existing writer. Hence a capability rather than a state — declaring one asserts the inlined code exists now, and an extracted-but-capability-less backend correctly falls to the leaf-authored path. Both halves have a witness (`test_authorship_follows_the_capability_not_membership`), because neither state exists in the live registry.
      - **NOT closed by this, and deliberately:** `MAKE_QUALITY_CHECK_REQUIRED_LANGUAGES = {fortran, c, cpp, mixed}` stays in the neutral core. It is a POLICY set over language families, not an implemented set — `c` / `cpp` / `mixed` are not registry members, and a physics node naming one is rejected upstream, so what keeps them live is the infrastructure node's language exemption. It is the same kind of set as `mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`'s `FORTRAN_C_FAMILY` and migrates with it in the compiler / linter adapters area below. The `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Operations Rules bullet now states, per gate, what registering a backend is and is not sufficient for.
    - **`language/fortran` source reading — `validate_pipeline_semantics.py` (807 when this area was written; 794 now)**. The stanza parser / normalizer HAS MOVED (sub-item below); what remains is `_fortran_procedure_envelopes`, `_iter_fortran_calls`, `_fortran_source_views`, `_local_fortran_module_map` and the `_FORTRAN_*` regex family. Blocking sub-item (CLOSED 2026-08-15, see below): `backends/language/fortran/signatures.py` USED TO import `_fortran_logical_lines` / `_normalize_fortran_line` / `_parse_interface_stanzas` from the validator. The import DIRECTION is legal (a backend may import the neutral core); what is wrong is that those three are Fortran knowledge sitting in a neutral module, so the backend has to reach back for its own subject matter. They move into the backend first; everything else in this area follows them.
      - **BLOCKING SUB-ITEM DONE (2026-08-15).** The §5.1 line and stanza layer moved: `lines.fortran_logical_line_texts` / `lines.normalize_fortran_line` (the `;`-less §5.1 view of the shared scanner, and the per-line canonicalizer) and `signatures.parse_interface_stanzas` / `.canonicalize_end_line` / `.declaration_atoms` / `.stanza_atoms` / `.stanza_line_set` / `.stanza_line_list` / `.source_atoms`, with the four `_IFACE_*` regexes and `_END_STMT_RE`. **It was not the three named helpers but nine**: `runner_renderer.assert_harness_pin` imported FIVE names from the validator, and `_stanza_atoms` pulls `_declaration_atoms` and `_canonicalize_end_line`/`_END_STMT_RE` behind it — moving only the three would have rebuilt the same backend→neutral-core edge from the other caller. `source_atoms` is new: both callers had spelled the same two-level comprehension over the line scanner, and stating it once is what let `runner_renderer` stop importing `backends.language.fortran.lines` (a module the allowlist did not grant it — the first version of this change added that bypass and the pin caught it). `signatures` now imports NOTHING from the neutral core, so the validator imports it at module level, and `lines.py`'s docstring no longer justifies its own placement with a cycle that no longer exists. Witnessed by `NoImportCycleWithTheValidatorTest`, whose scanned set is COMPUTED as the import closure of two roots — the modules whose subject matter moved, and every backend module the validator imports — so a dependency routed through a sibling is covered and a backend module no root reaches is left free to import the neutral core, which `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` permits. Getting there took six rounds and six wrong shapes: a substring check that missed the alias spelling, then a literal-`importlib` gap, then a prefix filter that excluded `registry.py`, then a name-to-path map that could not see a package `__init__`, then a walk of every backend file that refused the ledger's own next migration area and carried an exemption list that was itself a bypass, and finally the closure's own blindness to a RELATIVE import — its reader stripped the leading dots, so `from . import helper` named nothing the traversal could follow. The closure removes the class of "which files"; the relative-import fix closed the last spelling. **The redesign is itself witnessed now**: the algorithm runs against a SYNTHETIC tree with a known shape, because deleting its transitive step, or reverting wholesale to the previous walk, each left the entire suite green. Two limits stay recorded rather than closed: a dependency routed through a NEUTRAL module (backend -> shim -> validator) is one hop outside the traversal, and a computed module name is out of reach of any static reader — following arbitrary chains through the neutral core is not a question this level of analysis can answer. Nine tests moved to the modules whose code they pin (`test_fortran_lines.py` / `test_fortran_signatures.py`); the gate-level tests that merely consume the atoms stayed. **13 definitions left the validator** (8 functions + 5 constants) and **12 landed**: `_IFACE_TYPE_START` was collapsed onto the pre-existing `_TYPE_HEADER_RE`, which is byte-identical to it, so the destination now holds 3 `_IFACE_*` regexes plus `_END_STMT_RE`, not four plus one. All 12 are AST- or pattern-identical to `origin/main` after normalizing the renamed callee references, and the whole layer was verified behaviourally over all 365 in-tree `*_model.f90` — logical lines, parsed stanzas and source atoms alike. (Both counts in this sentence have now been wrong once: first twelve when 13 left, then thirteen when only 12 landed, the second because the collapse commit preceded the correction commit. They are measured from the tree now, not typed.) Two gaps this leaves, recorded rather than closed. (i) `_TYPE_HEADER_RE` silently drops two legal header shapes — no stanza AND no error, so a gate reports the symbol missing from a source that declares it. Its `[^:()]` class refuses EVERY attribute list containing parentheses (`extends(b)`, `bind(c)`, `public, bind(c)`), not just `extends` as an earlier version of this sentence said. A `module subroutine` header is dropped the same way but by a DIFFERENT pattern — `_IFACE_PROC_START`'s prefix group, which has no `module` alternative; an earlier version of this sentence attributed it to the type pattern, which cannot match it at all. The class does NOT exclude component declarations, which the missing comma already does. All of it is pre-existing and absent from the corpus, and the pattern is now single-point-of-truth for both the splitter and the parser, so it is worth fixing WITH the language area rather than pinning as-is. (ii) The import pin reads direct imports only, so a neutral module can reach the backend through the validator's module attribute (`from tools.validate_pipeline_semantics import fortran_signatures`) and be recorded as a neutral import; a reviewer's probe was caught anyway, but by the token ratchet, not by the pin. A witness census classified every decision this change introduces or relies on, by execution, into witnessed / corpus-dependent / vacuous / caught-only-by-the-ratchet. Its outcomes, which are what survive re-measurement: **nothing is caught only by the sampled ratchet**; the unwitnessed holes it found are closed (a lazy `importlib.import_module` back-import, `tools/backends/registry.py` and the two package `__init__` modules going unscanned, a probe floor an order of magnitude below its real reach); and the guards it could prove observe nothing are LABELLED in the source rather than deleted, because what misleads a reader is taking one for a live guard. One of those labels was wrong and is now a witness instead: `stanza_atoms`' empty-atom filter fires on a line of exotic blanks, which the scanner keeps as content and the normalizer erases. The per-class tallies are deliberately NOT restated here — three successive versions of this paragraph carried a count that was stale or wrong within one commit, so the counts live in the tree and the prose states the outcomes. A forward cost this creates, named here because the ledger does not otherwise say it: a NEW neutral gate can no longer reach the §5.1 layer by importing it — the compliant routes are a registry surface (which exposes no §5.1 API today) or an allowlist entry, which the pin records as a boundary regression. Measured in the primary checkout at the END of the review loop: suite 4723 -> 4744 passed / 0 failed. Nine of the difference are relocations rather than additions; the rest are witnesses this loop found missing. A checkout outside `$HOME` shows the same two pre-existing environment-dependent `test_hooks_common.py::ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests` failures and one skip on `origin/main` as before; `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` 83 errors with a histogram identical to `origin/main`; sampled baseline 2,575 -> 2,564, `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py` 805 -> 794 (`fortran-subroutine` 85 -> 77, `fortran-intent` 43 -> 42, `fortran-standard` 32 -> 31, `fortran-module-procedure` 4 -> 3; the `fortran` class did NOT move — the prose explaining where the layer went replaced the prose that went with it). **The allowlist is unchanged**, which is the mechanical proof that no new bypass was introduced — and the reason it is unchanged is NOT that every pair was already granted. Three of the four module x importer pairs are: the validator holds `...fortran.lines` and `...fortran.signatures`, `runner_renderer` holds `...fortran.signatures` only. `runner_renderer` -> `...fortran.lines` is the pair that does NOT exist, which is exactly why the pin refused the first version and why `source_atoms` was written. An earlier version of this sentence claimed both modules were granted to both importers, which contradicts the sentence three lines above it that describes the pin firing; two reviewers caught it independently.
      - Noted while moving it: `_FORTRAN_NAME_LIMIT` (validator), `bundle.IDENTIFIER_MAX` and the runner emitter's `MAX_IDENTIFIER_LEN` were three copies of the f2008 63-character identifier bound. The runner-emission area below removed the third (the emitter reads `bundle.IDENTIFIER_MAX`). **Two remain** — `_FORTRAN_NAME_LIMIT` in the validator and `bundle.IDENTIFIER_MAX` itself, which is the owner — and the validator's copy migrates with the source-reading area. Do not add a third.
    - **`build_system/make` — `workflow_conductor.py` (334) + the GNU-make sublanguage parser in `validate_pipeline_semantics.py`**. Who authors `src/Makefile`, what its grammar is, which targets are required. The parser is 14 top-level `make`-named functions totalling 784 lines, of which the longest contiguous run is 8 functions at lines 1849–2157; `_makefile_logical_lines` / `_parse_makefile_rules` / `_makefile_full_var_map` are 130 of the 784. Measured with `ast`, not by reading line numbers off a screen — the previous version of this sentence named a span that began inside a Fortran helper, ended mid-function, and contained 65 lines of Fortran knowledge. `_conductor_authors_makefile` / `_conductor_authors_runner` are the seam already: they are a `make ∧ fortran` conjunction, which is a two-axis backend selection written as an `if`.
    - **`docs` — phase and contract documents (532 across 22 files)**. `docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` (170), `docs/workflow/CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md` (76) and `docs/workflow/CODEGEN_BUNDLE_CONTRACT.md` (69) are the bulk; the first mixes Fortran rules into a neutral phase contract, and the latter two are Fortran ABI documents filed as neutral workflow contracts (they move to `docs/backends/language/fortran/` and the neutral contract references them).
    - **`language/fortran` runner emission — DONE (2026-08-15).** `tools/runner_renderer.py` (167 sampled, 1230 lines) is now `tools/backends/language/fortran/runner.py`, reached through the neutral seam `tools/host_render.py`; its allowlist entry is gone and the file left the scanned set. The ledger called it a whole-file move and it was not: the module also held two neutral things, and separating them was its own commit.
      - **The neutral half.** The two node-IDENTITY spec-input gates (`spec_id_length_violation` / `infra_dep_count_violation`) and the case_id safe-token grammar are now `tools/spec_input_gates.py`. They cannot be answered by a backend: the closure builder applies them to `spec_ref`s out of `deps.yaml`, **before an IR exists**, so there is no language to ask the registry about. The spec_id bound is therefore carried as a neutral number and pinned equal to the backend's own bound by a test — the precedent `MAKE_QUALITY_CHECK_REQUIRED_LANGUAGES` already set. The rejected alternative, taking the minimum over every language backend, would put every backend's import on the most failure-sensitive path in the repository AND make registering a backend silently tighten an input gate. Making the grammar public retired three cross-module imports of a private name.
      - **The registry half.** `Backend` gained `backend_provides` beside `core_provides`, and `provides` is now the union. This is what keeps the migration from flipping a dispatch: `provides` answers "is this node's runner the host's job or a leaf's", which is not a question about where the code sits, so the two authorship predicates read exactly as before and no node changed hands. `capability_module` is the reaching half — it refuses a value that has not declared the job and a package that does not carry what its record claims — and `CAPABILITY_MODULE_ATTR` says where a package re-exports each capability. Import-time checks refuse a package capability with no package, with no reach convention, or owned by both sets.
      - **`RenderError` stayed neutral**, in the seam. The neutral core catches it in one place (`workflow_conductor._write_runner`) and a class reached through `registry.load` is not the class that clause names — it would silently degrade to `except Exception`. (An earlier version of this line, and of two source comments, said "three clauses". Measured: there are two `except RenderError` in the tree and the second is inside the backend catching its own raise, where class identity is trivial and this placement argument does not apply. One clause is enough to decide the placement; the count was never checked.)
      - **The hard call site** was the compile gate's module-level `CHECKS_PUBLIC_NAMES` import: module scope has no node, so it has no language to ask about. It is a per-node lookup now, and a refusal is appended as a violation and never raised — inside `_validate_compile_stage_impl` an uncaught exception discards every violation the sibling gates already collected.
      - **Two readers of the node's language were about to become four.** Each side now reads through one set of helpers (`workflow_conductor._toolchain` / `_ir_language` / `_ir_build_system`, and `validate_pipeline_semantics._ir_m3c_language`): a predicate that approved authorship and a seam that then refused the same IR would be a render-kill on a shape that renders fine today. **An earlier version of this line said "exactly one" per side and was wrong** — a reviewer found a THIRD conductor-side reader, `_read_toolchain`, still holding the unguarded dereference the other two had been given guards for, and it is the reader the writers actually use, so the predicate approved authorship and `_write_makefile` raised.
      - **A gap that cannot be closed, recorded because four review rounds were spent trying**: `provides` answers a DECLARATION question (does this repository do this job for this value), while every seam reaching a migrated capability asks whether it can actually dispatch — which additionally requires the record's package to really carry the capability. Those cannot be made identical: `tools/backends/registry.py` must not import a backend at declaration time (its module docstring is the reason), so nothing can check a declaration against the tree until something loads it. **Three declaration rules were written claiming to close it and all three were wrong**, each refusing legitimate work: keyed by capability, the first axis to migrate `control_file` refused the second axis' still-inlined declaration; keyed by axis, migrating one `linter` adapter refused the other three — which are genuinely separate inlined adapters — and that refusal is an `ImportError` for a module the whole tree imports, with `parallel/none`'s own record comment predicting the forbidden state. "Does the neutral core still implement this job for THIS value" is per (axis, value) and no other record carries it. The gap is HANDLED instead, at the three places it lands: the reachability loop in `test_backend_boundary` catches it for the live tree, the seam refuses, the two deterministic gates report the refusal as a violation, and `_write_runner` raises a named build precondition. **A routing residue, not fixed here**: the gate violations carry `compile_static_violation`, which `COMPILE_STATIC_FAILURE_ROUTING` sends to a warm `compile.generate` re-author — and no re-authored IR repairs a registry/package disagreement, so such a run spins its retry budget before escalating. The conductor routes the same fault correctly (transport fail_closed). Closing it needs a distinct failure category, which is a conductor routing-table change rather than a gate change; the gate messages name the fault as a host one meanwhile.
      - **A forward cost this creates, named here because nothing else says it**: the validator's `try` / `except ModuleNotFoundError` bootstrap spells every module-level import TWICE, and the new `DirectExecutionBootstrapTests` pin requires the second copy. So each future migration step that the validator reaches through a backend import costs 2 sampled tokens rather than 1, and the token ratchet fires once on a change that moved knowledge OUT of the neutral core — with a headline sentence ("backend knowledge grew") that is the opposite of what happened. The remedy is the documented `--write-baseline`, and `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Operations already states that a migration is expected to fail the stale-baseline half; the doubled cost and the misleading headline are new and are recorded, not fixed. Closing it properly means the validator not needing a dual-import block at all.
      - **Cross-axis residue, recorded rather than closed**: `MAX_RENDERED_LINE = 100` in the moved module is the `linter` axis's column limit (fortitude S001), not a language fact. It sits in the language backend because its only consumer is the emitter; it migrates with the compiler / linter adapters area, the same treatment `-Werror=ampersand` has.

The second correction claimed the figures now came from a script reading tracked artifacts; three commits later the suite figure was stale again, because a test count is NOT derivable from a tracked artifact and goes stale on the next commit that adds a test. The sampled baseline and the allowlist are derivable and are stated; a suite count is measured at review time and belongs in the review, not in a ledger that outlives it.
    - **`skills` — (191 across 4 files)**. `skills/workflow-generate-generate/SKILL.md` (122) and `skills/workflow-generate-verify/SKILL.md` (58) carry `fortitude` rule ids and Fortran idioms inline; they split into a neutral `SKILL.md` plus `backends/<axis>/<backend_id>.md` fragments.

Also carries `validate_pipeline_semantics.MAKE_QUALITY_CHECK_REQUIRED_LANGUAGES`, the same language-family policy set as `FORTRAN_C_FAMILY` — the gates area above closed the build-system half of that condition and left this half deliberately (see its notes). **Measured while closing that area**: `mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`'s `run_linter` preset dispatch spells the four linter ids a fourth time (plus `mcp_servers/tools/run_linter.json`), so registering a fifth linter is accepted by the validator (which now asks the registry) and refused by the server. Issue #109 moved that dispatch from an if-chain into `_LINT_PRESET_COMMANDS` + `_LINT_PRESET_COMPOSITES` (one row per preset, the composite naming the presets it composes rather than restating their argv) so the launch-time host probe could read argv[0] out of it. That is a smaller surface, NOT a closure: the ids are still spelled in the server, the server still keeps its own accepted set rather than asking the registry, and the declaration file is untouched. The area still owns it. The `tools/` half of that pair is closed by a test — the validator's own inference chain must be able to infer every implemented linter — but the MCP half needs this area.
      - **THE `linter` SIDE IS CLOSED (2026-08-27 issue #111, completed 2026-09-01 issue #120); the `compiler` side is not.** The `fortitude` row's argv left the server: `tools/backends/linter/fortitude/lint.py` declares it, `registry.capability_module("linter", "fortitude", "lint")` is how the server reaches it, and the `lint` capability moved from `core_provides` to `backend_provides` on that one record. What FORCED the move was the rule set: the argv now carries `--select <39 codes>` (the gate had been inheriting whatever the installed build enabled by default, which is how a vendor's 0.9.0 made every Fortran node uncertifiable — issue #110), and a lint rule id is the example `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Design Policy gives of knowledge the neutral core may not hold. Issue #120 did the same for `cppcheck` and `ruff` and DELETED `_INLINE_LINT_PRESET_COMMANDS`, so no linter invocation is spelled in the neutral core any more; `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics._infer_run_linter_preset_from_command` stopped holding its own copy of the three executable names with it. What each of the two declarations carries is in `docs/backends/linter/{ruff,cppcheck}/RULES.md`, and the `cppcheck` one states a WEAKER property than its siblings on purpose: that tool has no `--select`, its `--errorlist` does not enumerate everything it reports (measured: `uninitvar` is absent from it on 2.16.0 and 2.17.1 and still fires), and a check can get broader without being new (`passedByValue` 2 -> 3 from 2.7 to 2.16.0), so the declared version range is the only bound on drift it has. `mixed` keeps `lint` in `core_provides` and that is correct rather than residual: a composite has no invocation of its own, holds no rule id, and naming an axis value is what the neutral core may do — issue #120's acceptance wording ("`core_provides={"lint"}` appears on no `linter` record") was wrong, not the row. STILL OPEN: the preset ids are still spelled in the server and in `mcp_servers/tools/run_linter.json` (naming, not knowing, but a fifth linter still has to be added in two places), and the whole `compiler` half is untouched. The area still owns those.
        - **The sampled ratchet does not measure this migration, in either direction.** The token class counts the NAME, which the neutral core may still use — it is the preset key — while what moved is the KNOWLEDGE, which no token class can see. `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Enforcement already states the sample is a bound on growth and not a detector; this is an instance of it. The PROPERTY, which is what a reader needs: every `linter-*` occurrence these changes add to a `neutral core` file is a path citation, an operator install line, or a preset key. NO FIGURE IS WRITTEN HERE, and that is the rule this bullet exists to enforce: two successive versions of the #111 sentence carried a number that was right only at the commit it was taken at (8, then 3), a review re-took both, and the #120 edit reintroduced a whole paragraph of them before this sentence was re-read. Re-take them instead, in one command: compare the `linter-*` counts per file between `git show origin/main:tools/tests/data/backend_boundary_baseline.json` and the working copy.
          - What #120 changes about how to READ that comparison. Two token classes are NEW (`linter-cppcheck`, `linter-ruff`), so their first baseline is a starting point rather than a fall, and there is nothing on `origin/main` to compare them against. Some `linter-fortitude` counts FALL, and not because knowledge moved — they fall because prose describing the old split was deleted. Some counts in the CITING documents RISE (`docs/RUNBOOK.md`, `mcp_servers/README.md`), which `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Operations Rules names as an expected consequence of a migration rather than a violation. None of the three is evidence about the boundary.
        - **A second launch-time question was added with it**: a required host tool that is PRESENT but of an unmeasured version is refused before the first leaf (`unsupported_required_host_tool_versions`), reading the range and the probe argv off the backend package so `tools/host_prerequisites.py` still spells no technology token. The protocol is mandatory for a package-implemented `lint` capability rather than duck-typed, because an optional one would turn the whole arm off on a rename.
- **Two residual holes in the `Generate.gate` lint file set, and one linter-version boundary** (2026-08-28, medium — reproduced, pre-existing; found by the issue #111 review loop). None is introduced by that change; all three became visible because it claimed the verdict is a function of the source and a declared rule set, and each is a way that claim is narrower than it reads.
  - **Neither `Generate.gate` content check sees SOME subdirectories of `src/`.** (The lead sentence said "a SUBDIRECTORY" without qualification until 2026-09-01; measured on fortitude 0.8.0 / 0.9.0 / 0.9.2 over a tree holding `top.f90`, `build/probe.f90`, `dist/probe.f90`, `sub/probe.f90` and `.hid/probe.f90`, the declared invocation scans three of the five — `top.f90`, `sub/probe.f90` and `.hid/probe.f90` — and skips only the two named in the walker's built-in list. The body already recorded the `.hid/` half as a correction; the lead had not been re-read.) The linter default-excludes `build/` / `dist/` / `venv/` / `_build/` / `site-packages/` (measured, one directory per run: each yields `0 files scanned`, exit 0; an earlier version of this entry added "and hidden directories", which the round-3 review measured to be FALSE under the declared invocation — a `.hid/` subdirectory IS scanned), and both syntax-stage file readers walk with `iterdir()` — non-recursive — (`tools/workflow_conductor.py`'s `_gate_syntax_check` staging and `mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`'s source ordering). A bundle `logical_path` of `build/x_model.f90` passes `tools/codegen_bundle.py`'s `logical_path_violations` (measured: `[]`) and the build graph flattens it to `build__x_model.o`, so a nested path is a designed feature rather than an accident. For a Fortran node the two gates partly cover each other — a top-level file that `use`s the hidden module fails the syntax gate with `Cannot open module file` (measured) — and the reviewer could not demonstrate a fully green gate over hidden violating source. Where it is unguarded is a node whose syntax arm does not run at all (`language != "fortran"`), and no such node exists in the corpus today, so this is a bound on growth. Closing it means declaring the file set instead of inheriting the walker's defaults, or forbidding a `/` in `logical_path`; both are wider than the issue that found it. **THAT COST ESTIMATE IS FALSIFIED (2026-09-01, issue #120 review loop, blank-slate axis, re-measured here across the whole supported range).** A THIRD option exists and it is one flag: `--exclude=` empties the walker's built-in exclusion list. Measured on fortitude 0.8.0, 0.9.0 and 0.9.2 over a tree holding `top.f90`, `build/probe.f90` and `dist/probe.f90`, each carrying the same defects — the declared invocation scans `top.f90` alone and reports 5 findings, and adding `--exclude=` scans all three and reports 15. Issue #120 took exactly that route for `ruff`, where the same channel was found and closed. It is NOT taken for `fortitude` here, deliberately and by the operator's decision: this is the one linter a node can actually reach, so the change alters what a LIVE certification gate scans, an archived tree carrying source under an excluded directory would begin failing — a condition that measures ZERO instances today (enumerated with `os.walk` over all 352 `src/` directories in this tree: no Fortran file under `build/`, `dist/`, `venv/`, `_build/` or `site-packages/` beneath any of them), so it is a bound on growth and not a live risk, and this entry says so rather than leaving a hypothetical stated as a cost, and the gain analysis above still holds — a source the walk skips is not staged by `_gate_syntax_check` (`iterdir()`, non-recursive) and is not built (`_write_makefile` pins three top-level names), so a leaf gets no closer to reporting its `substep` done by hiding one. What has changed is the COST, not the verdict: whoever picks this up should know it is one flag and a witness, not a file-set declaration.
  - **`S001` compares `>=` on 0.8.x and `>` on 0.9.x**, so a line of EXACTLY 100 columns fails on the declared floor build and passes on the ceiling ones — two supported hosts disagreeing on one source (measured on 98 / 99 / 100 / 101 columns across 0.8.0 / 0.9.0 / 0.9.2). The leaf-read contract already requires lines UNDER 100, so a conforming source is unaffected. What is NOT conforming is the renderer's own guard: it rejects `len(ln) > MAX_RENDERED_LINE` rather than `>=` (`tools/backends/language/fortran/runner.py`, whose own comment 550 lines above already records the off-by-one for a different check), so it emits exactly-100 lines. The corpus evidence is 73 such lines across 67 `*_runner.f90` files, and it is weaker than it reads: 66 of the 67 sit in archived `workspace_2026030*` trees and none carries the current host-render header, so what is measured is that the GUARD permits the shape, not that today's renderer produces it on today's IRs. On a 0.8.x host a node whose render hits exactly 100 fails lint. **An earlier version of this entry said "in a file no leaf can edit" and cited the 2026-07-24 harness runner as the recent evidence; the round-3 review corrected both halves** — that node is the infrastructure harness self-test, the one case whose runner a leaf DOES author, so it is the wrong witness for the leaf-unfixable claim. For every other node the runner is host-rendered and the claim holds; the evidence for it is the 73 lines, not that file. Tightening the guard to `>=` is one character and was NOT taken here because it fails CLOSED on a node that renders today: it would refuse the harness node above rather than wrap its line. The fix is wrapping, in the renderer, with the corpus re-measured after it. `docs/backends/linter/fortitude/RULES.md` records the divergence.
  - **The launch version arm asks about the DEFAULT language's linter, whatever the run's own toolchain is.** `resolve_launch_axis_selection` derives the linter from `_ir_language({})` because at a cold start no IR exists to read (its own LIMIT section says so). That was harmless while the probe answered presence only; it now carries a VERSION verdict, so a host with an out-of-range fortitude is refused for a run that would never invoke fortitude. Zero instances in the corpus — every `spec.ir.yaml` declares the same language — so it is a growth bound rather than a live refusal, recorded because the change turned a benign over-broad probe into a launch refusal.
  - **The linter version is checked at launch and never again.** `unsupported_required_host_tool_versions` is a `tools/run_workflow.py` startup gate; `run_linter` does not re-check, so an operator who upgrades mid-run gets a rule set the declaration was not measured against. Operator-reachable only — a leaf gains nothing from it — so it is out of the defended set (`AGENTS.md` §Development premises) and recorded rather than closed.

Already per-language lookup tables, so this area is a mechanical relocation into the registry plus the backends.

The producer half is the same shape and older: it reads `src/<spec_id>_runner.f90`, a file extension the axis value implies, from the neutral core. **Measured: this issue's branch added no debt** — the `backend_boundary_baseline.json` token counts for `tools/prompt_templates/pure_generate_verify.txt`, `tools/workflow_conductor.py` and `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` are byte-identical to `origin/main` — so this is a carrier to migrate WITH the templates above, not a regression to fix there. Five decisions in `tools/tests/test_backend_boundary.py` are of that kind. They are covered by external mutation runs and by review, not by anything inside the suite, and the module docstring says so rather than letting them read as covered.

- Once the Claude backend can obtain `session_id` or `agent_session_id` from the hook payload, abolish the `agent_run_id` resolution that depends on `active_child_agent_run_id.txt`, and unify it to the same session-identifier-based resolution as the Codex backend.
