- Harden `Compile.generate` so it reliably authors full-fidelity `io_contract.test_predicates`, reducing the run-to-run variance where it emits degenerate predicates and forces a full node re-roll. In the degenerate shape every pass-test predicate is a bare `verdict.overall eq pass` (per_case), dropping the per-check thresholds and cross-case scopes that `tests.md` requires the predicate to carry. Because `Generate` is `tests.md`-blind, `test_predicates` are the only channel by which these thresholds reach the leaf-authored `<spec_id>_checks.f90`; when they are dropped the per-test verdict cannot be deterministically reproduced from the IR (defeats R2) and `Compile.verify` (V3) fail-closes `major`, discarding the whole `compile.generate` output.
  - This is `Compile.generate` authoring variance, not a gate bug and not executor-specific: the `compile` phase is `--generate-executor`-independent (the `pure`/Z2 work migrates only the `generate` phase), so the same weakness exists on the legacy arm (historical note: the `--generate-executor` flag and the legacy generate arm were later removed at M-F; the variance statement itself is executor-independent and stands). Observed on `orch_20260717T154247Z_d82d283a` (`compile.verify` attempt 2, `reason_detail=dev_verify_major`, repo `df0b6ad`); the immediately following re-roll authored correct predicates and passed, confirming variance rather than incapacity. Representative losses the degenerate roll dropped for `shallow_water2d`: the nx-dependent `analytic_h_l2_rel` threshold map, the `convergence_order>=0.80` cross-case reductions, the `symmetry_h_l2_rel` cross-case reduction, the test-specific `mass_drift_rel` override, and all remaining per-check thresholds plus the `na_allowed` structure.
  - Fix direction: strengthen the `Compile.generate` prompt/exemplar (see `docs/` and the compile-phase authoring rules) so full-fidelity `test_predicates` — per-check `op`/threshold conditions, `per_case` value maps for nx-dependent thresholds, `case:`-scoped conditions for cross-case reductions, and the matching `io_contract.diagnostics_contract.metrics` addresses — are the default authoring. Consider also a deterministic `Compile.static` check that rejects the all-bare-`verdict.overall eq pass` degenerate shape, so the failure is caught cheaply and deterministically rather than by the semantic verifier after a full generate.
  - **DONE (deterministic gate + prompt, 2026-07-18):** the deterministic `Compile.static` check is implemented — `verdict_evaluator.degenerate_predicate_violations` (a pass set whose every `expected_outcome=pass` predicate asserts only `verdict.*`, with no `checks.<id>` or metric-address condition anywhere in the pass set; `xfail` exempt), wired into `_validate_test_predicates` (`--stage compile`) so it routes back to `compile.generate` as a `compile_static_violation`, with unit + E2E coverage (incl. a real full-fidelity IR negative). The `Compile.generate` prompt was also strengthened (anti-degenerate clause in `skills/workflow-compile-generate/SKILL.md` R2, doc in `phase_01_compile.md`). Remaining = the prompt-side completion criterion below (a billed multi-roll observation), which is out of this change's scope.
  - Scope: keep this separate from the Z2 pure-executor A/B work — do not bundle it into a P-arm measurement run, and do not smuggle a `spec_version` bump through it.
  - Completion criterion: for a representative multi-check node (e.g. `shallow_water2d`), `Compile.generate` authors `test_predicates` carrying the per-check thresholds and cross-case scopes without a V3 `test_predicates`-fidelity `major` fail-close across several consecutive rolls; the deterministic static check rejects the degenerate shape and is wired into the `Compile.static` enforcement path with test coverage (this half DONE — see above).

- Guarantee `Compile.generate` IR **self-sufficiency**: every operation an `algorithm.steps[]` entry declares must be lowered into `spec.ir.yaml` with enough mathematical definition that a `spec/`-blind `Generate` leaf can implement it without reading `controlled_spec.md`. Under phase_02 §2-1 the IR is the sole algorithm carrier for `Generate`, but a thin `compile` roll can lower a step as a name + I/O shape + invariants with the FORMULA missing, and `Compile.verify` currently passes that. Observed on `orch_20260717T160736Z_c6d8b921` (`shallow_water2d`, repo `df0b6ad`): the 564-line IR declared `hydrostatic_reconstruct_p0` / `bottom_topography_source_p0` by name only — no Audusse (2004) reconstruction math (`h* = max(0, eta - max(z_b,i, z_b,i+1))`), no explicit ban on an analytic-gradient source term — both of which live ONLY in `controlled_spec.md` §5. The pure `Generate.generate` leaf, being tool-less and `spec/`-blind, then omitted the reconstruction and substituted the forbidden analytic bed-slope source in all three tries (structural, not stochastic), and `Generate.verify` fail-closed `critical`. Same root/shape as the `test_predicates`-variance item above but a distinct symptom (missing algorithm math, not missing verdict thresholds); file separately. This variance is `--generate-executor`-independent (it hurt the legacy arm too — the 1048-line L-arm roll happened to be dense; density is not guaranteed), so the fix is on the `compile` side.
  - Fix direction: strengthen the `Compile.generate` prompt/exemplar so a declared local operation (`hydrostatic_reconstruct_p0`, `bottom_topography_source_p0`, …) is lowered WITH its defining expression / the constraints that pin it, and design a self-sufficiency check ("every non-dependency `operation_ref` has an implementable definition in the IR"). A purely deterministic check is hard (it is a completeness judgment over free-form math); consider a `Compile.verify` checklist item.
  - **PARTIALLY DONE (prompt + verify checklist, 2026-07-18):** the `Compile.generate` lowering rule (`skills/workflow-compile-generate/SKILL.md`) now requires each non-dependency `operation_ref` to be lowered with its defining math + pinning constraints (incl. forbidden forms), and the `Compile.verify` checklist (`skills/workflow-compile-verify/SKILL.md` + `phase_01_compile.md` V2) makes a name-only local operation a `major` remand. Left deliberately NON-deterministic (free-form-math completeness is not machine-decidable) and NO new IR schema field was invented. The carve-out itself is NOT removed here (removal needs a verification run — see below); this is strengthening only.
  - **This TODO is the removal trigger for the pure-5 `controlled_spec.md` interim carve-out** (`tools/pure_leaf.py` `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION`, `tools/workflow_conductor.py` `_build_pure_context`, `tools/prompt_templates/pure_generate_generate.txt`, `docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` §Generate-executor / §2-1, `docs/workflow/LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md`). **(SUPERSEDED 2026-07-22 — the carve-out CODE was removed at `pure-10` backed by the new deterministic `Compile.static` presence floor; see the "Carve-out code removed (pure-10)" bullet below. The present-tense "is live" / removal-step narration in the rest of THIS bullet and the "Do NOT re-remove" directive in the next describe the pre-pure-10 state and are kept only as history. The TODO itself stays OPEN — the removal TRIGGER still needs the billed run.)** While that carve-out was live, a thin IR was silently completed by `Generate` from the injected controlled_spec — the detection signal (a `Generate.verify` fail on missing math) is suppressed. When IR self-sufficiency is guaranteed, remove the injected `controlled_spec_document` slot + required-key (a future `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION` bump — NOT `pure-6`, which shipped the defect-E distillation below with the carve-out still live), drop it from `PURE_CONTEXT_REQUIRED_KEYS[("generate","generate")]`, and confirm a verification run passes with the slot gone before re-defaulting.
  - **Premature removal attempted and REVERTED (2026-07-18):** a `pure-8` change removed this carve-out on the strength of the `b5b5f94` prompt/checklist strengthening alone. A Codex review flagged it P1: the self-sufficiency check is explicitly NON-deterministic (`skills/workflow-compile-verify/SKILL.md` — "not deterministically gated", a `major` remand), pure is now the ONLY executor (M-F), and the removal trigger requires the verification run to pass with the slot gone FIRST — which has not run. Removing before that run recreates the unrecoverable thin-IR loop (`orch_c6d8b921`). The removal was reverted at that time. (SUPERSEDED 2026-07-22: the code was re-removed at `pure-10`, but only AFTER landing the deterministic `Compile.static` presence floor as the safety net the `pure-8` attempt lacked — the difference the review demanded. The removal TRIGGER is still not satisfied; do NOT declare it satisfied or close this TODO until the billed slot-gone run passes.) `pure-8` retains only the runner-driven per-id checks ABI (Item 4).
  - **Structural floor landed (2026-07-22):** a deterministic `Compile.static` presence floor now catches the *zero-signal* end of this class — `validate_pipeline_semantics._validate_local_operation_lowering` flags a LOCAL `operation_ref` (one not resolved by a `dependency.direct_deps[].operations[]` entry) that carries NO lowering signal at all: no per-step non-structural field (e.g. `description`), no `derived_field_rules` entry keyed by a step input/output, and no mention of the op name (full or bare `<spec_id>__` tail) in any `derived_field_rules` / `invariants` text. It routes back to `compile.generate` through the existing `_compile_static_inproc` → `compile_static_violation` warm-reopen wiring (no new substep). It is presence-only: a present-but-INCOMPLETE formula is untouched and stays the `Compile.verify` V2 `major`. The whole certified fleet (advdiff flux/boundary/euler components + harness + profile + problem) passes; a name-only `p0_interface_reconstruct` shape fails. This is a safety net that de-certifies the worst thin-IR shape but is NOT the removal trigger — a present-but-thin formula still needs the billed run to certify (see completion criterion).
  - **Carve-out code removed (pure-10, 2026-07-22) — this TODO stays OPEN:** the `controlled_spec_document` producer slot + `PURE_CONTEXT_REQUIRED_KEYS[("generate","generate")]` key + `_build_pure_context` read + generate-template scoping paragraph/slot + the exemplar-preamble source reference + docs were removed, and `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION` bumped `pure-9`→`pure-10` (drift pin refreshed). The `("generate","verify")` `controlled_spec_document` is by-design and stays. This landed the CODE removal on the strength of the strengthened lowering rule + the deterministic `Compile.static` presence floor (above) — the structural floor now non-certifies name-only lowering, which the reverted `pure-8` removal lacked. **BUT the removal TRIGGER is not yet satisfied**: the presence floor catches only the zero-signal shape; a present-but-INCOMPLETE formula (the `orch_c6d8b921` Audusse class) is still only a semantic `Compile.verify` `major`, so the true acceptance remains the operator's billed slot-gone `validate` run (completion criterion below — `sw2d` reproducing Audusse). Do NOT declare "carve-out removed (trigger satisfied)" or close this TODO until that billed PASS. If that run regresses, the code removal is what gets reverted.

Evidence for each clause, taken from the second run:
    - *Several consecutive rolls lower the math*: three consecutive `Compile.generate` rolls — `shallow-water2d_20260806_001`, `_20260806_002`, `_20260807_001` — each `attempt_count=1`, `verification_status=pass`, and each lowers both local operations with a defining expression, not a name. The `_20260807_001` IR states the Audusse interface depth (`z_face_x = max(z_b(i,j), z_b(i+1,j))`, `h_star_xL = max(0, h(i,j) + z_b(i,j) - z_face_x)`, and the y-interface twin) and the reconstruction-consistent source `S_hu = (g/(2 dx)) (h_star_xL(i,j)^2 - h_star_xR(i-1,j)^2)`, with the analytic bed-slope form `-g h dz_b/dx` named as forbidden.

- Make the checks-module **check-id ABI runner-driven** so the "emit every `io_contract.diagnostics_contract.checks[].id`" obligation is structurally impossible to violate, retiring the `pure-6` distillation + acceptance gate that currently compensate for it. Today the host-rendered runner calls `checks_compute(case_id, ncheck, chk_ids, chk_status)` and passes NO ids — the checks module invents `chk_ids` from memory, so a pure (tool-less, `spec/`-blind) leaf that drops an id emits a `diagnostics.json` missing that check, caught only phase-late at `Validate.execute` `post_execute` (`_validate_diagnostics_contract_output`). Z2 defect E: `orch_20260718T000925Z_43172c0e` (`shallow_water2d`, repo `pure-5`) dropped `analytic_agreement` / `convergence_order` / `symmetry` and failed 53 min in. Contrast `metric_compute`, which the renderer drives per-address, so the analogous class cannot occur there.
  - Fix direction: change the renderer ABI so the runner passes the IR check ids into `checks_compute` (e.g. `checks_compute(case_id, chk_ids_in, ncheck_in, status_out)` with the runner supplying `chk_ids_in`/`ncheck_in` from the IR, the module returning only `status_out` per id) — the same per-call driving `metric_compute` already uses. This touches `tools/runner_renderer.py` (the ABI + call sites), `docs/workflow/CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md` §1-§3, the `Generate.static` checks-source gate, and — crucially — **legacy** checks-module authoring, so it **invalidates the L-arm baseline** (`d899b6df`) and every certified checks source. Do it in the **M-F period, AFTER the Z2 A/B is complete**, not during it.
  - **This TODO is the removal trigger for the `pure-6` defect-E compensations** ([[feedback-ir-as-sole-carrier]]'s removal-condition set): when the id ABI is runner-driven, delete only clause (A) from the "Checks-module behavioral contract" paragraph of `tools/prompt_templates/pure_generate_generate.txt` while KEEPING clauses B-F and therefore KEEPING the `**Checks-module behavioral contract` entry in `orchestration_runtime.PURE_REPAIR_STATIC_PARAGRAPH_PREFIXES` (A-F are one paragraph under that header; the prefix lifts the whole block into a cold-fallback repair, so dropping the prefix would silently vanish B-F from recovery prompts — the defect-D-in-recovery class the lift exists to prevent). Only remove the prefix if that entire removal empties the paragraph. Also remove `codegen_bundle.m3c_checks_ids_violation` + its `pure_bundle_contract_violation` wiring + the `diagnostics_check_ids` param + both callers' id resolution + the `bundle_checks_ids_violation` routing category + `validate_pipeline_semantics.fortran_string_literals` if unused elsewhere, update `docs/workflow/CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md` / `phase_02_generate.md` / `LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md`, bump `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION`, and confirm a pure `validate` run passes with the gate/clause gone. (The other §2/§3 behavioral-contract clauses B-F stay distilled — only the check-id half becomes structural.)

The `pure-6` compensations were retired: clause (A) of the "Checks-module behavioral contract" paragraph was rescoped to per-id `status` honesty only (B-F kept, so the `PURE_REPAIR_STATIC_PARAGRAPH_PREFIXES` entry stays), `verify` G5 rescoped, and `codegen_bundle.m3c_checks_ids_violation` + its `pure_bundle_contract_violation` wiring + the `diagnostics_check_ids` param + both callers' id resolution + the `bundle_checks_ids_violation` routing category + the `validate_pipeline_semantics.fortran_string_literal_values` extractor family (helpers + regexes) were all deleted. Docs updated (`CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md` §1-§3, `phase_02_generate.md`, `LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md`) and `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION` bumped `pure-7`→`pure-8` with the drift pin. The remaining half — `shallow_water2d` + one other multi-check node running pure `validate` to a passing verdict on a fresh workspace — was the operator billed verification run, now **DONE (2026-07-19)**: on a fresh workspace at `62df1f0` (= pure-8), `advdiff1d_linear validate --with-deps` (6 nodes) and `shallow_water2d validate --with-deps` (5 nodes) both passed to `aggregate_verdict=pass` with the per-id ABI live — this Item's completion criterion is fully met.

- Add a **drift guard for the pure prompt ⇔ acceptance-gate coupling**: there is currently NO mechanism that detects when a `pure` prompt template (the distilled `Authoring rules` / `Checks-module behavioral contract` paragraphs in `tools/prompt_templates/pure_generate_generate.txt` / `pure_generate_verify.txt`) or a coupled gate constant (the language backend runner's `CHECKS_PUBLIC_NAMES`, `codegen_bundle` acceptance-gate literals, the `PURE_REPAIR_STATIC_PARAGRAPH_PREFIXES` set) changes without a matching `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION` bump. The distillations are hand-copied from canonical gate code into a prose the tool-less pure leaf cannot re-derive, so a one-sided edit silently ships a prompt that disagrees with the gate the same source is enforced against.
  - Motivation (measured): across the pure-4/5/6 work, single-sentence prompt edits repeatedly drew review rounds that EACH found a real correctness bug (a mis-attributed gate, a dropped default, a false-positive parser), and the sentence-level reviews did not converge — the loss is exactly the kind a version-pinned mechanical check catches but a human proofread does not. Raising the review-round count is not a fix; a machine guarantee is. See memory `[[pure-prompt-gate-drift-guard-gap]]`.
  - Fix direction: a pinned-hash test over the tuple `(template text, coupled gate constants, PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION)` — if the template or any coupled constant moves while the version literal is unchanged, the test fails and forces either a deliberate version bump or an explicit pin update. Keep the pinned set narrow (only constants a template actually distills) so it does not become a churn magnet. This is a test-only change (no `spec_version` bump, no empty contract-version bump — the guard fires only when content genuinely moved).
  - Completion criterion: editing a distilled paragraph or a coupled gate constant without bumping `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION` (or updating the pin) fails a deterministic test in `tools/tests/`, and the guard is green on the current `pure-6` state.
  - **DONE (2026-07-18, commit `9915d5c`):** implemented as `tools/tests/test_pure_prompt_contract_drift.py` — a pinned-hash test over the tuple (both pure prompt template files' bytes, the coupled gate constants, `PURE_PROMPT_CONTRACT_VERSION`), seeded on the `pure-7` state (the C072 distillation landed just before the guard, so the baseline is `pure-7`, not `pure-6`). The pin set is kept narrow per the fix direction: pin members are import constants or template-file bytes only; host-side gates a template does not distill are deliberately excluded (including them is a category violation and a churn magnet). An unchanged-content version bump alone also fails the guard (no empty bumps). Test-only change, as specified.

- (Optional) A **flow-sensitive / interface-aware deterministic check for `semantic_dependency.required_sources` reachability**, if deterministic coverage of that property is ever wanted again. Context: `_validate_problem_model_dependency_dataflow` (`Generate.static`) used to check, in addition to "a dependency RESULT reaches intent(out)" (check 1, assignment-only, sound, kept), that each `required_sources` physical input reaches the intent(out) dataflow (check 2). Check 2 has NO sound flow-insensitive form on this regex-level view: origin/main's assignment-only closure false-rejected physically-correct code (Fail #2/#3, `orch_9a5fe93e` — a source reaching intent(out) through a dependency call), and every call-crossing approximation attempted (undirected, then directed by global classification, then directed by def-use order) traded that false-positive for a fail-open — a source co-passed to an unrelated call, or fed to a call whose write a later call overwrites (Codex review rounds, 2026-07-18). Because chain-threading (`call dep1(h,stage); call dep2(stage,final)`) and dead-write overwrite (`call dep1(h,stage); call dep2(z,stage); out=stage`) are the SAME syntactic shape with opposite correct meaning, separating them needs callee argument intents AND reaching-definitions (flow sensitivity) — beyond a regex lint. Check 2 was therefore REMOVED; the property is delegated to `Generate.verify` **G5** (which reads `controlled_spec.md` and IS the semantic authority: "each intent(out)'s final expression tree reaches the required_sources"), backed by the runtime.
  - Fix direction (only if a deterministic backstop is later judged worth it): build a real dataflow pass — resolve each dependency operation's published interface to get per-argument intents (`intent(out)`/`inout` = written, `intent(in)` = read), and do reaching-definitions over statement order so a reused scratch variable's live producer is the last write before each use. This is a proper analysis, not a heuristic; scope it as its own task with test coverage for the chain and overwrite shapes above. Until then, G5 is the sole check and this is intentionally not gated.
  - **One tempting shortcut is ruled out by measurement (2026-08-12).** `gfortran -fdump-fortran-original` prints name-resolved executable code — `CALL dep__apply ((solve:x) (solve:scratch))`, `ASSIGN solve:y solve:x`, with host association already resolved — which looks like this pass handed over for free. It is not usable for a GATE: comparing the dump from gfortran 12.4.0 and 14.2.0 over the 365 in-tree models plus 41 adversarial fixtures, the `code:` section is exactly where the two versions disagree (one renders a construct as `SELECT TYPE`, the other as `BLOCK`; 27 of 319 analysable files differ overall). A verdict read from there would depend on which compiler the machine has, which is worse than no verdict. The stable part of the dump — `procedure name =`, `Formal arglist:`, `DUMMY(...)`, identical on all 4328 lines across the two versions — is enough for dummy intents (the item above uses it) and not for dataflow. If this pass is ever built, it needs its own tree (tree-sitter's statement structure, or a parser with a stable API), not the debug dump.
  - Completion criterion: N/A unless undertaken — this records the decision (check 2 removed, G5 owns it) so the removal is not re-litigated, and specifies what a real deterministic version would require.
  - **A reproduced, named test case for whatever this pass becomes** (added 2026-08-12 by the continuation-blindness work below). Check 1's CANDIDATE rule has the same flow-insensitivity problem, one step earlier: `workspace_20260712/pipelines/profile__dynamics_shallow_water_profile_2d_rusanov_p0_ssprk2__0.1.0/dynamics-shallow-water-profile-2d-rusanov-p0-ssprk2_20260711_001/source/src_20260711_001/src/dynamics_shallow_water_profile_2d_rusanov_p0_ssprk2_model.f90` yields `candidates=['flux_ok', 'u_new', 'z_b']` — a FALSE "does not propagate", and the three are NOT one defect. `z_b` and `flux_ok` are the PRODUCER-side residue: each is written by an earlier `call`, which the candidate rule does not count as a producer. `u_new` is written by the dependency call itself, so it is a correct candidate; its false flag is CONSUMPTION-side, because `u = u_new` is then read across a `call` the assignment-only closure cannot follow. A pass that fixes only one side leaves this model still flagged. That is exactly the chain-threading shape above, so it needs the same two ingredients: callee argument intents, and reaching-definitions over statement order. It is latent today ONLY because `_validate_problem_model_dependency_dataflow` returns for any `node_key` outside `problem/` and this model's domain is `profile/` — widening that guard without this pass makes the false positive real. Excluding named constants (which landed 2026-08-12) does NOT clear it; that was measured, not assumed.

- **DONE (2026-07-19)** — **Narrowed `_mcp_permissions_for_launch` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py`) to full substep-granular least-privilege.** It now takes a `substep` argument (plumbed from `build_capability_document` exactly like `_write_roots_for_launch`) and looks up an explicit `(step, substep) -> tools` table (`_MCP_TOOL_GRANTS_BY_SUBSTEP`) with a fail-closed `[]` default: only the four conductor in-process bodies that actually call a gated build-runtime tool get a grant — `build`→`compile_project`, `generate.lint`→`run_linter`, `generate.syntax`→`run_syntax_check`, `validate.execute`→`run_program`+`run_quality_checks`. (Superseded detail: a later substep merge folded `generate.lint`/`generate.syntax` into the single `generate.gate` substep; the table now keys `("generate", "gate")`→`run_linter`+`run_syntax_check` — see `_MCP_TOOL_GRANTS_BY_SUBSTEP`. The least-privilege design described here is unchanged.) **This went beyond the originally-recorded completion criterion** (which kept `generate.generate`'s linter/syntax grant): every LLM leaf (`generate.generate`/`verify`, `compile.generate`/`verify`, `validate.judge`) and every other deterministic validator (`generate.static`, `compile.static`, `validate.pre_judge`/`post_judge`) now resolves to `[]`, since none of them import a build-runtime handler and the LLM leaves are forbidden by SKILL from calling any MCP tool. Covered by deterministic unit tests (per-substep grant table through `build_capability_document`, unknown-pair/non-leaf-role fail-closed) and real-authz integration tests (`validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation` refuses `run_linter` under a `generate.verify` token, accepts `run_linter` but refuses the sibling `run_syntax_check` under a `generate.lint` token). No `spec_version` bump. Docs: `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` capability table.

- **Close the Bash read-path asymmetry that lets a confined leaf read rw-bound backend credentials** (2026-08-10 repository audit, high). The bwrap profile rw-binds the Claude backend home (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json` — OAuth credentials) for claude leaves, but the hook layer does not guard that surface: `_evaluate_bash_read_manifest_policy` (`tools/hooks/cli.py`) skips validation for any target outside the repo root, the operator-secret guard (`tools/hooks/common.py`) covers `~/.atmofab` only, and `_is_auto_approvable_readonly_bash` then classifies `cat ~/.claude.json` as a safe read-only command, so the call is auto-approved without reaching the harness permission list. The Read-tool path to the same files is blocked by the read manifest; Bash is the only open route.
  - Fix direction: treat every out-of-repo path the bwrap profile rw-binds (backend homes: `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`, `~/.codex`) the same way the operator-secret guard treats `~/.atmofab` — reject Bash reads fail-closed — and exclude out-of-repo literal reads from the read-only auto-approve set. Record the layer-boundary rule (which layer guards which path class) in `docs/HOOKS.md` as part of the change.
  - Completion criterion: deterministic hook tests show Bash reads of the backend homes (direct, and shell-reassembled forms the operator-secret guard already covers for `~/.atmofab`) BLOCK for a leaf on both backends, Read-tool parity is covered, and `docs/HOOKS.md` states the boundary rule.
  - **DONE (2026-08-10).** Suite 4385 -> 4448 passed, 1 skipped (the pre-existing calibration skip of the retention item below); `ruff check` reports the same findings on the touched files as before the change. Six rounds of independent subagent review (security-bypass / regression / tests-and-docs lenses) plus a Codex review ran against the diff; everything below the first bullet is what they forced, and each round's findings were largely defects introduced by the previous round's fixes.
    - The operator-secret guard was generalized rather than copied: `_command_reads_protected_host_path` (`tools/hooks/common.py`) takes a root SET and returns the matched root, so every spelling the `~/.atmofab` guard already covered — marker regex over the raw command, quote/backslash collapse, brace expansion, bounded glob, `..`-traversal via `.resolve()` — now covers the backend credential homes too, on both backends and regardless of argv0.
    - Every `cd` in a command is now FOLDED in order rather than treated independently, so `cd ..; cd ..` lands where bash lands; a separator glued to the cd TARGET (`cd ~;cat x`) is split off, `pushd -n` no longer manufactures a bogus anchor, and an unexpanded `$H` is no longer accepted AS a directory (`.resolve()` succeeds on any non-glob string, so the literal won and discarded the resolved spelling behind it). Four review rounds each found a new `cd` spelling that reached home, which is why the fold replaced the spelling list.
    - A cost optimization added in round 3 — keeping only the anchors related to a protected root, plus the last two — was itself a fail-open: it discarded exactly the anchor a `..` read needed (`cd ~/work && cat ../.claude.json && cd a && cd b` went silent). The rule is now a split rather than a filter: a `..`-free token cannot leave its base, so it only needs the root-related anchors; a token that walks with `..` is tried against all of them.
    - **The literal-token view was not enough, and the review proved it end-to-end.** `cat ${HOME:-/x}/.claude.json` read the OAuth file with `permissionDecision=allow` — `os.path.expandvars` understands only bare `$NAME` / `${NAME}`, and a `${…}` token never even reached the resolution branch (the brace-expansion pre-pass diverts anything containing `{`). The guard now tests **candidate spellings** per token: every `${HOME…}` operator form plus its operand branch (for an unset variable) and `${!V}` indirection, variables assigned in the same command including through another variable (`A=$HOME; B=$A; cat $B/…`), the bash-only tilde prefixes `expanduser` does not know (`~+` / `~-` / `~N`), and every `cd` anchor — `cd ~`, a BARE `cd` (which also goes to `$HOME`), `pushd`, `cd -P` / `cd --`, and a `cd` glued to a separator or in parentheses. Over-approximating candidates is the safe direction for a read guard. Each of these shapes has a regression test; the round-2 review is what found all of them except the first.
    - **The candidate generation was quadratic when first written, then exponential after the round-2 fix, and neither DoS test saw it.** N `cd` anchors x M relative tokens is one `.resolve()` syscall each and N assignments x M tokens one `re.sub` each (measured 24s for a 19 KB command, 18s for a 13 KB one); the round-2 fixpoint substitution then made a CYCLIC chain multiply the candidate per pass, so a 56-character command took 134s. Round 3 replaced the count caps with WORK bounds — candidate length, folded-`cd` count, anchor depth, plus skipping substitution on tokens with no `$` and a cached per-name regex. Those inputs now cost 0.20s, 0.11s and 0.007s. **The count caps were themselves a security defect, not just an under-tuning**: they dropped the EXTRA candidates, so throwaway assignments (or `cd`s, or `${Z}` expansions) with the real one last disarmed the guard entirely. Round 3 fixed two of the three axes and left the assignment one still capped by count while the code comment, `docs/HOOKS.md` and this record all claimed otherwise — round 4 caught that, and the bound is now RELEVANCE (an assignment is kept when something references its name) plus work bounds. The padding test pads past every shipped cap, because a cap of N is disarmed by exactly N pads. The DoS test now exercises each axis at a size where the discarded implementations fail by >20x.
    - **The quoting constructs that hide a path from every literal match.** `cat $'\057home\057…\057.claude.json'` read the OAuth file with `permissionDecision=allow`: nothing for the marker regex to match, a nonexistent relative path for the extractor, and a `$` the auto-approve check exempted because it is followed by a quote rather than an identifier. The guard now decodes ANSI-C escapes (octal, hex, `\u`, the simple set) and `$"…"` locale quoting as candidates — including the MID-token forms whose quotes shlex strips before the guard sees them (`cat ~/$'.'claude.json`, `cat /home/sei$'\171'a/.claude.json`), which round 3's token-start-only decoder missed. The auto-approve check counts `$'` / `$"` as a reference, but only where it OPENS a word: matching it anywhere cost four real commands their auto-approve, because `grep 'foo$'` ends a quoted word with the same two characters.
    - **A shell value is command text, never an `re.sub` replacement template.** A `\1` / `\d` / trailing `\` in a value makes `re.sub` raise, and the raise surfaced as `hook entrypoint failure` — a hard, unactionable block on ordinary commands like `PAT='\d+' grep -nE "$PAT" f`. The new code had this defect; so does `origin/main`'s `_command_invokes_dismiss_violation`, verified by reproducing the crash against a clean `git archive` of origin/main. Both are fixed the same way (a lambda replacement) and pinned by a test that also checks the dismiss-violation detection those substitutions exist for still works.
    - The protected set and the bwrap rw-bind set are ONE resolver: `protected_host_read_roots` / `backend_credential_home_paths` (`tools/hooks/common.py`), which `_backend_runtime_bind_paths` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py`) now calls for what it binds. The anti-drift test drives `_backend_runtime_bind_paths` itself and asserts every rw path it returns is guarded — the first version asserted the resolver against itself, which was tautological and would have stayed green if a bind were added directly to the profile (the review caught it). `_backend_runtime_bind_paths` returns byte-identical results to `origin/main` across a 448-case env/backend matrix.
    - A protected root with either containment relationship to `repo_root` is dropped rather than enforced: a `CODEX_HOME` above the checkout would fail-close every ordinary in-repo read, and one INSIDE it (`CODEX_HOME=<repo>/workspace`, the example in `_resolve_backend_rw_binds`'s own docstring) would fail-close every workspace read on a claude run. The first version dropped only the ancestor case while citing a bind-side rule that rejects both.
    - **A memory bound, found by the machine dying rather than by a reviewer.** `_substitute_variables` applied `_CANDIDATE_MAX_LEN` to its RESULT: one `re.sub` over a token carrying N references to an M-character value allocates N*M bytes first, and both factors are attacker-chosen — a 100 KB token became a single 200 MB string, and a test process reached 18 GB RSS on a 20 GB machine (measured with `ps`; the shape reproduced against the pre-fix code under a 2 GB `ulimit`). The bound is applied DURING the pass now, and the candidate dedup uses a set rather than a linear list scan. Pinned by `test_expansion_candidates_are_memory_bounded`, which asserts lengths rather than RSS so it stays deterministic.
    - The `$`-dropping heuristic that recovers a shlex-stripped `$'…'` was first applied to every `$`, which turned an ordinary `cd ~ && grep -c '\.claude\.json$' notes.txt` into a protected read (the trailing regex anchor decodes to the same text); restricting it to a `$` before punctuation then missed `~/.$'c'laude.json`, which arrives as `~/.$claude.json` and looks like a variable. The rule is now punctuation OR a name defined nowhere — a reference to a name that exists nowhere is not a reference.
    - The last count cap, on the assignment scan, was spent by throwaway assignments BEFORE the relevance filter could drop them, so 512 pads evicted the real one — the same class for the fourth round running. It is a byte budget now, and only relevant assignments spend it. The `_RESOLVE_OPS_MAX` trim also dropped the anchor a symlink read needed; each pair is a lexical `normpath` now, with a syscall only where the path exists, which made the ceiling cheap enough to raise past any plausible command.
    - Codex review then found the two remaining holes. `${X/x/y}` and its siblings TRANSFORM the value, and the code fell through to the untransformed one, so `X=/home/x; cat ${X/x/seiya}/.codex/auth.json` reached the credential home — the transforming operators (`/`, `//`, `^^`, `,,`, `^`, `,`, `#`, `##`, `%`, `%%`) are applied now, while the alternate-word operators stay the second candidate. And the assignment byte budget was the count cap wearing a different hat: pad until it is spent, append `H=$HOME; cat $H/…`. There is now NO bound on that axis at all — the work is linear in the command's own length, which the OS bounds. That is the fifth time the same class was found in a different axis.
    - A second Codex round found the wildcard branch unanchored: `cd ~ && cat .clau*e.json` was only ever checked as repo-relative, because `_glob_pattern_targets_root` rejects a non-absolute pattern and `glob.glob` would have run from the hook's own cwd. A relative PATTERN is now anchored exactly like a relative path (repo_root plus every `cd` anchor), through one helper both the brace and the plain glob branch call. The same round found `$'\UFFFFFFFF'` crashing the decoder — `chr()` raises above Unicode's maximum, and the raise surfaced as a hook entrypoint failure on an otherwise valid command; an out-of-range escape stays literal now.
    - A third Codex round found the last two. bash decides each `${X:-word}` INDEPENDENTLY, while the code generated only the all-values and all-operands spellings, so `A=$HOME/; cat ${A:-x}${B:-.codex}/auth.json` — which needs A's value with B's operand — was tested as neither; the real combinations are enumerated now while they are few, with a one-operand-at-a-time fallback past that. And the brace expander returns a TRUNCATED product past its bound rather than something still carrying braces, so the fail-closed `{…}`→`*` fallback never fired and the alternatives past #256 went unchecked (`cat ~/{x0,…,x256,.codex}/auth.json`); the glob form is added explicitly when the bound is hit.
    - A fourth Codex round: the operand of `/`, `#` and `%` is a GLOB in bash, not a literal (`X=/home/x; cat ${X/x*/seiya}/.codex/auth.json`), and `#`/`%` strip the shortest match while `##`/`%%` strip the longest — all four now match bash on the cases the test pins. It also caught the containment drop taking `~/.atmofab` with it: that rule's justification is that the bind side rejects such a configuration, which is true only of the credential homes, so the operator-secret root is now never dropped and a checkout placed inside or around it keeps failing closed.
    - A seventh review round (on the code the Codex rounds forced) found three more. `${X:2}` / `${X:off:len}` substrings were not emulated at all, so `X=QQ$HOME; cat ${X:2}/.atmofab/…` reached every protected root. The alternate-word enumeration was both exponential and incomplete past its bit cap — replaced by RESOLVING each expansion the way bash does, from whether the variable is set and whether it is empty, which is not a guess and needs no combinations. And the auto-approve's expansion check was a word-start pattern, so a MID-word `$'…'` (`cat /etc/host$'name'`) was auto-approved; it now tracks quote state, which is also what tells a construct apart from the literal `$` in `grep 'foo$'` — the two readings a word-start rule kept confusing.
    - The glob operands are matched by a bitset dynamic program, not a translated regex: `${V##*a*a*a*a*b}` backtracked for 8s at a 240-character value and never returned at the 4096-character bound, and the shortest-suffix scan it replaces was quadratic. The same input is now 0.002s, and 200 suffix strips over a 4096-character value 0.12s.
    - An eighth round, on the code the seventh forced: `${X:+w}` was resolved with the test INVERTED (`+` yields the word when the variable IS set), which made a mixed token — one expansion keeping its value, one taking its operand — expressible by no candidate; nested `${A:-${HOME}}` produced the right candidate and then never re-expanded it; an arithmetic offset (`${X:1+1}`) fell through untransformed; and a `]` as the first `[…]` member was mis-parsed. All four fixed, and the transformation now matches `bash -c` on every shape tried. The glob matcher's `[…]` class mask was also rebuilt per start position — a Python loop over the whole value — so a 4 KB value with a few classes cost 10-65s; the masks are built once per value now, with a work budget over (positions x pattern elements) behind that. 400 classes over a 4 KB value: 0.07s.
    - A ninth round ended the emulation arms race rather than extending it. Three levels of `${A:-${B:-${HOME}}}` beat the two-pass bound, and a non-additive substring offset (`${A:0x7}`, `${A:1*3}`) fell through untransformed — each round had been finding the next syntactic corner bash has and this guard did not (nesting depth, arithmetic bases, POSIX classes, the next operator). The backstop: a candidate that still carries `${` is one this guard could not resolve, and if the token ALSO spells a protected root's own path component (`.claude.json`, `.codex`, `.atmofab`), the read is treated as reaching it whatever the rest expands to. That closes the class rather than the instance, and the false positives it costs are the ones already accepted — a command that names a protected path. An unresolved expansion naming nothing protected is still allowed, which a test pins.
    - The same round found the glob-substitution budget debiting only the pattern length while the real per-position work is O(len(value)) — a 2.3 KB command cost 7.6s. `_glob_match_bounds` replaced the O(len(text)) list comprehension with bit arithmetic, the budget is work-proportional, and the reviewer's 61s shape is 0.27s. The DoS tests now assert CPU time rather than wall clock, because one of them flaked when the suite ran under load beside other processes — the assertion is about this hook's own work, not the machine's.
    - A tenth round found the guard had been testing containment only ONE way: a recursive reader handed an ANCESTOR (`grep -r sessionKey ~`, `ls -R ~`, `find ~ -exec cat {} +`, `tar cf - ~`) reads the credential file with no expansion trick at all. It also found `-C` / `--directory` moving a command's working directory unmodelled (`tar cf - -C ~ .codex`), `$(…)` stepping around the round-9 backstop when the component sits in a different token (`cat $HOME/$(echo .claude.json)`), an absolute out-of-repo path still auto-approved under a non-reader argv0 (`ls /etc`), and the anchor prune evicting a symlink anchor when padding follows it — the mirror of the round-4 finding, so the prune keeps BOTH ends now. And the round-9 backstop was blunt in one direction: this checkout has its own `.claude/` and `.codex/`, so those components now count only when the token also reaches for HOME.
    - The last unbounded cost axis was the NUMBER of transformations: the per-expansion budget bounded one `${X//pat/rep}` while n assignments x n expansions reached 26s of CPU on a 1.6 MB command. There is one work budget per COMMAND now; the same shape is 0.48s at 165 KB and 0.80s at 413 KB.
    - An eleventh round found the round-10 recursive rule producing a REAL false positive, in this repository's own hook logs: quote-collapsing turns `echo "docs / runtime"` into a bare `/` token, which is an ancestor of every root, so `grep -rn foo docs/ && echo "docs / runtime"` fail-closed. A `/` that appears only inside quotes is prose now. The same rule read flag letters as one set across all commands, which made `ls -la ~` — an ordinary listing — a credential read; flags are read per command now (`cp -a` is an archive copy, `ls -a` is not recursive), and the command set gained the spellings the review named (`cp`, `zip`, `cpio`, `pax`, `7z`, `tree`, `--archive`, `-d recurse`). Two blunt rules of my own making, both caught by replaying real traffic rather than by reasoning.
    - The same round closed: `--directory=`/`-C=` in their glued spelling; the round-10 HOME-narrowing letting `cd ../.. && cat $(echo .claude)/creds.json` through (leaving the repository now counts the same as naming HOME); a protected component glued to a substitution in one token (`.claude$(echo /)creds.json`); and POSIX classes inside a `[…]` (`${V//[[:upper:]]/_}`), where the class's own `]` had been terminating the enclosing class. The transformation agrees with `bash -c` on every shape tried, including these.
    - A twelfth round found three regressions of my own and three residual holes. The regressions: `cd .. && grep -rn foo repo/docs` blocked, because the `cd`'s own operand stayed a read candidate and resolved against the anchor that same `cd` produced; `grep -r x '/'` stopped blocking, because the round-11 prose fix keyed on "is it quoted" rather than "is it an operand" — the ancestor test now runs on real shlex operands only, which is both; and a `[a-\U0010ffff]` class materialized 1.1M characters, 293s of CPU, because the class body was expanded into a set (it is kept as ranges now — 0.001s). It also found the harness's OWN persisted tool-results unreadable from Bash: `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<session>/tool-results/<id>.txt` is where an oversized tool output is saved and the agent is then told to read it, the Read tool has always permitted it, and three real commands in this repository's logs are of that shape. Exempted now, matching the Read path.
    - Its remaining findings, all closed: `${V/#pat/rep}` / `${V/%pat/rep}` / `${V^^pat}` / `${V,,pat}` were returned unchanged (four working exfil shapes); `cat $(echo /home/seiya)/.claude/…` supplied the home prefix without `~`, HOME, `cd` or `..`, so a command substitution now counts as leaving the repository; and `_glob_pattern_targets_root` still used `fnmatch`, which does not know POSIX classes, so `~/.atmofa[[:alpha:]]/…` passed where its `[a-z]` twin blocked (spelled `~/.met-d[[:alpha:]]l/…` when it was found — the root was renamed in issue #127) — that check uses this module's own matcher now.
    - A thirteenth round found six defects, ALL of them regressions from round 12's own fixes — the clearest statement of this change's shape. Excluding a `cd`'s operand by SPELLING let a prepended no-op `cd ~` delete `~` from every later position, so `cd ~ && grep -r sk-ant- ~` stopped blocking (it is excluded by INDEX now). The persisted-tool-result exemption was applied BEFORE symlink resolution, and `~/.claude` is an rw bind, so a leaf could plant a link at an exempt-shaped path and read the credential through it (resolved first now). Treating any `$(…)` in the command as "left the repository" blocked `echo $(date) && cat .claude/settings.json` — an ordinary settings read one substitution away from a protected component; the signal is the token's now, and a literal home path in the token counts as home-ward. The exemption was also spelling-narrow (only the absolute form, no `~` / `$HOME` / glob) and did not restore the auto-approve. And the new case selector was read as a class or a literal, so `${V,,?}` / `${V,,*}` left the value unconverted, while `${V/#/rep}` with an empty pattern ignored the prepend.
    - A fourteenth round: `-C` was treated as a directory option for every command, but `rsync -C` is `--cvs-exclude`, `scp -C` is compress, and `ls -C` / `tree -C` take no operand at all — so those deleted the NEXT token, the real read target, from the ancestor rule (`rsync -a -C ~ /tmp/out` copied the whole home directory unflagged). It is a directory option only for the commands that have one now. A hardlink at an exempt-shaped path resolves to itself, so the round-13 symlink fix did not cover it (a persisted output has one link; more than one is not one). A glob under the exempt directory that walks back out with `..` skipped the root checks entirely. `${V/pat/rep}` ignored a backslash-escaped `/` in the pattern and left the replacement escaped. And `_blank_persisted_tool_results` backtracked — 6s of CPU on a 120 KB command, from a `[^\s'\"]+` that could swallow slashes; it matches single components now, 0.03s.
    - A fifteenth round: the glob branch of the persisted-tool-result exemption never re-tested the exemption after symlink resolution, so a link planted at an exempt-shaped name was readable through `cat …/tool-results/z*` while its literal spelling blocked; `env -C` / `env --chdir` / `gtar -C` were missing from the directory-option commands; a command NAME appearing as an ARGUMENT made a fake `-C` (`ls -R tar -C ~` deleted `~` from the ancestor rule); the `${HOME}` spelling of a legitimate persisted result was blocked by the `${`-catch-all, which ran before the exemption; and `${V::4}` (empty offset) was unmodeled. The owning command of an option is now the segment's argv0, found in ONE forward pass — the backward scan it replaces was O(index) per token and cost 13.6s of CPU on a 32 KB command (0.046s now).
    - Restoring four helper functions this round's own edit deleted cost a full regression sweep: 33 previously-fixed block shapes and 21 previously-fixed allow shapes, all re-verified, plus the 8048-command corpus replay. A block edit that spans from one function name to another silently takes everything between them.
    - The marker boundary is `(?:/|(?![\w.\-]))`, not `\b`: `\b` is satisfied by `-` and `.`, so a `~/.claude` root matched `~/.claude-notes.txt` (over-block, caught by a test written for the in-repo `.claude/` case) and swallowed every `~/.claude.json` hit (mis-attributed message). Roots are ordered longest-first for the same reason. Verified against `origin/main` that the only `~/.atmofab` decisions this changed are sibling names that were false positives, including when those siblings are symlinks to the real root (the `.resolve()` token layer still catches those).
    - Second half of the finding: an out-of-repo read target withholds the read-only auto-approve (`_evaluate_bash_read_manifest_policy` reports them; the caller gates on it). Not a block — auto-approval bypasses the harness permission list, and out-of-repo paths are exactly what the repo-relative manifest could not authorize, so the fallback to that list is the correct owner. Five ways the earlier versions still leaked into the auto-approve, all found by review and all now closed: a `~`/`$HOME`-spelled target (literal resolution read it as the in-repo path `<repo>/~/…`, missed as nonexistent, and auto-approved — `_expanded_target_path` now decides containment after expansion, in the literal and glob-prefix branches alike, and treats an unexpandable `~-` / `~+` / `~N` as outside); a glob whose prefix is in-repo but whose expanded matches escape through a `..`; a glob match that escapes through a SYMLINK (containment is now decided on the resolved match, as the literal path already did — `cat docs/*.txt` had authorized what `cat docs/link.txt` withheld); a `~` word under a command the extractor does not model at all (`ls -la ~`); and a variable reference, which makes the target unprovable because the extractor declares `$VAR` operands as residue. The variable check is `\$[A-Za-z_{]` on the RAW command: the first version tested the quote-stripped copy, which passed `cat "$HOME/.aws/credentials"` while refusing the identical unquoted read — bash expands inside double quotes. A `$` that names nothing (`grep 'foo$'`, `$?`, `$$`) keeps its auto-approve, and a 278-command corpus harvested from real run logs plus all 111 `Bash(...)` settings entries lose none.
    - `docs/HOOKS.md` states the layer boundary as three path classes with one owner each (in-repo = read manifest; protected out-of-repo = fail-closed Bash reject; other out-of-repo = harness permission list, minus the auto-approve), scoped explicitly to `Read`/`Bash` — `Glob`'s unvalidated `pattern` is named as the standing exception, since the first wording's "no path is left to a layer that does not see it" contradicted that pre-existing residue four lines above it. The residue the boundary does not close (earlier-call variables, command substitution, earlier-call `cd`) is listed rather than implied. `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` §Enforcement surface, the parallel canonical description, now points at it.
    - Read-tool parity is pinned by a test that asserts the block REASON, not only the exit code. Precisely: an absolute home path cannot appear in `allowed_read_roots` because the manifest WRITER normalizes every root through `_normalize_rel_posix`; the reader would honor an absolute entry, so this is a writer invariant, not a hook-layer one. `docs/RUNBOOK.md`'s hook-recovery table gained a row for the new policy id (and for its pre-existing sibling), since an agent can now hit it. Three `skills/*/SKILL.md` audit procedures that read a backend session transcript carry an operator-context note: replaying 8046 real Bash commands from this repository's own hook logs, those reads are the ONLY commands the guard newly blocks, and the auto-approve narrowing costs one command (`echo "$PWD"`) once `/dev/null` is exempted (a `diff -u /dev/null <file>` shape appears 12 times in that corpus).
    - NOT addressed, and not claimed: the Bash WRITE path to these same out-of-repo roots (this finding is the read asymmetry); command substitution (`cat "$(echo ~)/.claude.json"`), a variable assigned in an EARLIER Bash call, and an earlier-call `cd` — the guard sees one command string at a time; the shapes the work bounds discard (a substitution that grows past the candidate-length bound, more `cd`s than the fold bound, a working directory past the depth bound); and, larger than all of these, the interpreter route the agent contract sanctions (`python3 workspace/tmp/<arid>/x.py` reads the rw-bound credential home directly, with no Bash target to inspect). `docs/HOOKS.md` states this: the Bash guard is an obstacle on one route, not a boundary around the paths. A known false positive is accepted and documented — the marker match fires on a command that merely NAMES a protected path, and this repository's own docs contain the string.

- **Make the MCP capability gate fail-closed for orchestration-less calls and sanitize the `env` argument** (2026-08-10 audit, high). `_maybe_enforce_orchestration_mcp_gate` (`mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`) returns without any check when the caller omits `orchestration_id` (`docs/HOOKS.md` records this as a known gap), so a leaf holding the committed `mcp__build-runtime` permission can call `run_program`/`compile_project` with no capability, no role/phase check, and no audit attribution. Independently, `_run_command` merges the caller-supplied `env` into `os.environ` unsanitized, so `LD_PRELOAD`/`PATH`/`BASH_ENV` pass through and defeat the argv constraints. Combined, this is unattributed arbitrary execution inside bwrap.
  - Fix direction: when the server runs under the workflow (`ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_MODE=1` / `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ID` present in its environment), refuse gated tool calls that omit `orchestration_id`; strip or refuse loader-affecting environment variables (`LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `PYTHONPATH`, `PATH`) in `_run_command` regardless of mode.
  - Completion criterion: unit tests cover the omission refusal in workflow mode and continued standalone (non-workflow) operation; an `env` carrying `LD_PRELOAD` is refused or stripped with a surfaced violation; the `docs/HOOKS.md` known-gap sentence is replaced by the enforced rule.
  - **DONE (2026-08-11).** Suite 4448 -> 4509 passed, 1 skipped (the pre-existing calibration skip of the retention item below); `ruff check` reports the same findings on the touched files as before the change. Verified end-to-end through the real server process (`mcp_servers/mcp_call.py`): standalone runs, `ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_MODE=1` without `orchestration_id` refuses, an `env` carrying `LD_PRELOAD` refuses.
    - **The `env` rule is an allowlist under an orchestration, not the denylist the fix direction proposed.** Review demonstrated two exec paths the denylist missed, both against the tools' real argv: `COMPILER_PATH` redirects the gcc driver to a planted `f951`, which made `run_syntax_check` return `ok=True` on Fortran no compiler had parsed (a `Generate.syntax` gate that passes anything), and `MAKEFLAGS='--eval=$(shell ...)'` runs arbitrary commands before the certified Makefile is read. Neither is `LD_*` or `PATH`. Enumerating such names cannot terminate — make imports every environment name as a make variable, so `FC` alone replaces the compiler — so an orchestrated call now accepts only the six make variables `Validate.execute` declares (`OBJDIR` / `BINDIR` / `RUNDIR` / `BIN` / `SPEC` / `CASES`) and refuses everything else. Standalone keeps the denylist, extended with the demonstrated names, as a guardrail against mistakes; a test pins that it is not claimed to be a boundary.
    - **The same allowlist had to cover the argv, not only the environment.** Review demonstrated two more redirects after the env rule was in place: `compile_project`'s `extra_args` / `target` go straight into the make command line, where an assignment overrides even a hard assignment in the Makefile (`FC=/tmp/x`, `--eval=$(shell ...)`), and `run_syntax_check`'s `sources` is appended to the compiler argv, where `-B<dir>/` makes the driver exec a planted `f951` — again returning `ok: True` on Fortran nothing had parsed. Under an orchestration `extra_args` now accepts only assignments to the six declared make variables and a `target` is refused outright; `sources` must be plain file names in `project_dir` in every mode. The environment alone was not the surface: an allowlist over it while the argv stayed open constrained nothing. Still not covered, and stated in `docs/HOOKS.md` rather than closed: `run_program`'s `command`, which is caller-chosen argv by design.
    - The check is applied where each tool READS the `env` argument, not inside `_run_command` as the fix direction proposed: the server composes its own additions into the same dict afterwards (`OMP_*` for `run_program`, `PYTHONPATH` for the pytest preset), so a check in `_run_command` cannot tell a caller's key from the server's own. The pytest preset no longer consults a caller `PYTHONPATH` at all, since it can no longer arrive.
    - `detect_build_system` holds no capability — it executes nothing and no substep is granted it, which `test_probe_claude_mcp_registry_passes_without_detect_build_system_grant` pins from the permission side. It is refused outright under the workflow instead (see below), so the grant table is unchanged.
    - The allowlist is pinned at the HANDLER, not only at the helper: the restriction is chosen per call from `orchestration_id`, so a call site that asked for the standalone rule would leave the workflow on the weaker one with every helper-level test still green (review demonstrated exactly that mutation surviving). Key comparison is case-exact — `objdir` is a different environment variable, and accepting it would leave the make_test re-run on the Makefile's own default.
    - The allowlists cover key names AND values: a value reaches the make recipe's shell unquoted (`cd $(RUNDIR) && $(BINDIR)/$(BIN) --cases $(SPEC) $(CASES)`), so `CASES="c1; <command>"` was accepted and executed until the values were constrained too. A path value is judged by where it resolves, plus a residual check for the characters that recipe's shell or make would act on — a character allowlist over paths refused every Build on a checkout whose path holds a non-ASCII character, and still accepted the space that breaks the interpolation it was protecting. A checkout whose own path holds a shell metacharacter is still unusable under the workflow, which is a constraint on where a repository lives rather than a check that can be dropped. `command_log_path` is constrained to the repository root for the same reason it is a write at all.
    - **Two defects in the host gate itself, found once the review looked past the server.** `orchestration_id` and `agent_run_id` were interpolated unvalidated into the paths every artifact the gate trusts is read from, so a `..` in either relocated the whole check — preflight, phase_state, the capability file, and the audit append — to a directory the caller could write, making the capability it validated against one the caller authored. Both ids now have to be plain `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` tokens, the predicate the write_root pins already use for the same reason. And `_impl_resolved_build_system` line-scanned the IR for any key CONTAINING `build_system`, ignoring nesting and taking the first hit, so one line of prose in `algorithm.invariants` — free text the same substep authors — decided the toolchain and exempted the make-only contract entirely (`preset: pytest` accepted, i.e. arbitrary Python through a `conftest.py`). It reads the field structurally now, and an unreadable IR means make, as does a launch request that carries no `ir_ref` at all — every absence on the way to that contract had to be closed, not just the first one found. The twin `_impl_resolved_language` was the same scanner and the next round found it: it decides `_resolved_makefile_host_authored`, so a decoy line there flipped who owns `src/Makefile` — the conductor authors it and omits it from the leaf's write set, while record_launch concluded it was not host-authored and pinned it back as a writable path, handing the leaf the make recipe this whole item exists to keep it away from. `_impl_is_leaf_node` stays line-scanned on purpose: it is anchored at a top-level `dependency:` key and a value nested under any other key cannot produce a physical line at column 0.
    - The same class once more, and the instructive one: `run_syntax_check`'s source rule was applied to the `sources` ARGUMENT, which the workflow never passes, while the list the tool discovers itself was filtered on suffix alone. A leaf that writes `src/-o.f90` put an option into the certified gate's compiler argv. Both readings are one reading now — and the check runs BEFORE the compiler-availability skip, since the rule is about the names rather than about what a compiler would do with them: while it sat after the skip, a machine without the compiler accepted `/etc/passwd` and a staged `-o.f90` alike, and the test that pinned the rule only passed because it mocked the compiler as present.
    - Closing a hole has to pick the right failure class, and the class has to be named by more than the exception type: a staged source name the tool refuses is the leaf's own output, so `Generate.gate` records it as a `syntax_error` content failure routed back to `generate.generate`, not the transport `fail_closed` an uncaught raise would have produced (the substep's other two fail_closed causes are things the leaf cannot repair). Catching `ValueError` there was itself the inverse defect — every argument refusal the tool raises is about a value the CONDUCTOR supplies — so the refusal has its own exception class and only that one is caught. `detect_build_system` is refused outright under the workflow: it holds no capability by design, but it reports which marker files exist in any directory it is pointed at, which is a read the manifest boundary governs.
    - **A caller-named `repo_root` made the whole gate optional, and was carried as accepted residue for five review rounds on a false premise.** Everything the gate trusts is read from under that path, so a forged tree — preflight, phase_state, launch record, capability with its own token — is accepted and `run_program` runs arbitrary argv (reproduced). It was written off as needing a primitive the file-tool hook denies; the primitive is the scratch directory the agent contract grants (`workspace/tmp/<agent_run_id>` is rw-bound and `Bash(python3 workspace/tmp/*)` is committed). Under the workflow the root must now be the server's own checkout. Found by Codex review after seventeen subagent rounds had passed it over.
    - Residue, documented rather than closed: `run_program`'s `command` is caller-chosen argv by design, so the `validate.execute` grant stays a general execution grant. And the workflow signal is the server process's own environment, so the refusal binds calls through the harness-registered server, not a leaf that starts the module itself with the variables cleared (the pre-existing Bash/interpreter route).
    - The existing `tools/tests/test_build_runtime_server.py` cases call a gated handler with no `orchestration_id` at nine sites and never pinned `os.environ`, so after this change their verdict would have depended on whether the starting shell still exported the workflow variables. Pinned in a separate preceding commit.

- **Close the `build_system`-omission fail-open in the compile MCP gate** (2026-08-10 audit, high). The phase gate in `validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py`) checks `if req_bs and req_bs != "make"`, so omitting `build_system` skips the make-only check entirely, while the server side auto-detects a build system from marker files (`_recommended_build_system`) — a leaf bypasses the policy by planting `CMakeLists.txt` in its write root and dropping the argument.
  - Fix direction: in the gate, treat an absent/empty `build_system` as `make` (or reject the omission); align the server-side default so gate and server semantics agree.
  - Completion criterion: a gate test that omits `build_system` while a `CMakeLists.txt` marker exists is refused (or forced to make), and one test asserts gate and server agree on the omission semantics.
  - **DONE (2026-08-11).** Landed with the item above (same suite figures). The gate reads an absent/empty `build_system` **and an IR that declares none** as `make`, matching how `record_launch` already reads an IR that omits `toolchain.build_system`; the server forces `make` instead of marker detection whenever the call carries an `orchestration_id`, and keeps marker detection standalone, where there is no toolchain declaration to agree with.
    - The argument-side edit alone would have been a no-op: `req_bs or "make"` is the same predicate as `if req_bs and ...`. The check that was actually inert sat one line above it — `_impl_resolved_build_system` returns `None` for an IR with no `build_system` key, so `if bs == "make"` skipped the whole block and exempted a cmake compile and a ctest preset outright, while `record_launch` had already read the same IR as make and pinned its Makefile. Both absences now mean make.
    - The bypass was narrower than recorded here: `_recommended_build_system` checks `Makefile` before `CMakeLists.txt`, so planting the marker in a directory that already has a Makefile does nothing. It needed a `project_dir` with no Makefile — still the leaf's choice.
    - Residue, stated in the code comment rather than fixed: an IR that legitimately declared `cmake` plus an omitted argument now builds with `make` rather than detecting `cmake`. That is sound only because the workflow is make-only (`Conductor._require_make_build_system`), which is the same premise `record_launch`'s default rests on.

- **Consolidate the top-level comma splitter into `tools/backends/language/fortran/lines.py` and remove the shadowed duplicate** (2026-08-10 audit, high — reproduced defect). `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py` defines `_split_top_level_commas` twice ~9,500 lines apart; the later quote-unaware definition shadows the earlier quote-aware one, so `_runner_write_unit`/`_runner_write_format_token`/`_depth0_assignment_rhs` run with the wrong semantics (reproduced: `_depth0_assignment_rhs("sep = ','", "sep")` returns `"'"`), and a broken RHS silently drops out of the runner JSON descriptor gate — fail-open direction. A third near-copy lives in `tools/orchestration_runtime.py`. This is the issue-#23 shared-scanner class recurring on the splitter.
  - Fix direction: one public quote- and bracket-aware, depth-clamped splitter in `tools/backends/language/fortran/lines.py`; migrate all callers in both modules; delete both in-file definitions.
  - Completion criterion: a single definition remains repo-wide, a regression test pins the reproduced quoted-comma case, and all existing splitter consumers stay green.
  - **DONE (2026-08-10).** Suite 4360 (`origin/main`) -> 4385 passed, 1 skipped (the pre-existing calibration skip of the retention item below). Verified before closing: across all 876 Fortran sources in the tree, the six deterministic gates this change touches return the same verdict as `origin/main` in both directions. Every source form named below is accepted by `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008`.
    - `fortran_lines.split_top_level_commas` is the single splitter: quote-aware, bracket-aware (`()` and `[]`), depth-clamped. It shares a body with `split_fortran_statements` through a private `_split_top_level`, which rejects a separator that is not a single character unclaimed by the brackets (raised, not asserted, so `python3 -O` cannot elide it). No definition of `_split_top_level_commas` remains repo-wide, and `backends.language.fortran.signatures` imports the shared splitter instead of the validator's private copy.
    - `fortran_lines.continuation_state_after_line` is the single rule for whether a character literal survives a line break: the last non-blank character of the last contributing line is `&`, where an all-blank or comment-only line does not contribute and carries the previous answer through (F2008 3.3.2.4). It is a forward fold, so each scanner threads the state; the backward-search form it replaced rescanned the whole run of skipped lines at every newline inside an open literal, which is quadratic in that run. Whether that skip applies depends on whether the text is masked, and `_split_top_level` DERIVES it (masked text is a fixed point of the mask) rather than taking it as an argument. It is stated as a derivation because it was got wrong in four separate places while it was a caller's choice.
    - `fortran_lines.mask_code_lookalikes` blanks `!` comments and character-literal contents while preserving length and every surviving character's offset, for rules that match keyword structure over raw source. Quote delimiters, the opening `!`, and `&` inside a literal survive. Rules that inspect literal CONTENT (the forbidden-filename scans) must not use it.
    - Four further members of the class existed beyond the three copies the audit named: `_split_fortran_names` (the splitter renamed), `_iter_fortran_calls` (a quote-blind paren extractor duplicating `_extract_balanced_parens`), the `subroutine ... end subroutine` envelope regex that selects the text those parsers read, and `_validate_dependency_operation_on_model_files` (three keyword-presence tests over raw text). Searching for the helper NAME finds only the first three; the decomposition item below is to search for the shape, for the paired operation, for the rule that selects the input, and for keyword-presence tests.
    - Harm directions, all reproduced at gate level: a comma inside a literal or comment manufactured a phantom identifier that suppressed a `Generate.static` dependency-dataflow violation; an unbalanced paren inside a literal truncated the runner JSON gate's format token, inline and through the named-`fmt` path, so a forbidden descriptor went unscanned; a comment naming `end subroutine` truncated a subroutine body so its gate skipped it, and a commented-out procedure minted a phantom match; a commented-out `use` or `call` satisfied a presence requirement, and a literal naming an operation raised a redefinition violation. `signatures.declaration_atoms` reached the §5.1 comparison only with an unbalanced paren inside the literal (`:: msg = 'a(b', tail = 'z'`); a balanced literal mis-splits identically on both sides and cancels.

- **Close the `&`-continuation blindness that remains in the Fortran-reading `Generate.static` gates** (2026-08-10, high — reproduced). The 2026-08-10 splitter consolidation closed comment- and literal-blindness across this class, but not continuation-blindness: several gates match regexes over UNJOINED text, so a legally wrapped statement is read as fragments. `mask_code_lookalikes` cannot close this — it preserves line structure by design; joining through `fortran_lines.fortran_logical_lines` can. Reproduced cases, all accepted by `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008`:
  - `_split_fortran_names` drops the first name after every `&`, so a wrapped argument list loses one name per continuation. Recovering those names is the correct parse, but on its own it flips three real models in this tree (`shallow_water2d` x2, the `dynamics_shallow_water_profile_2d_rusanov_p0_ssprk2` profile) to a FALSE "does not propagate dependency operation outputs", because `_validate_problem_model_dependency_dataflow`'s candidate and consumption rules are over-approximations those dropped names were masking (`ncomp` is a `parameter`; `z_b` / `u_new` are written by an earlier call, and the consumption crosses a call the assignment-only closure cannot follow).
  - `intent_out_pattern` (`[^\n!]+`) in the same three gates stops at the physical newline, so `intent(out) :: a, &` / `b` loses `b` — a false `literal-only assignments for all intent(out) vars` violation, and a shrunken `out_vars` in the other two gates.
  - `_fortran_source_module_deps`' `^\s*use` pattern misses a continuation placed between `use` and the module name (`use &` / `dep_model`), which empties `required_object_deps` and makes `_validate_fortran_makefile_src_dir` return before it can require a Makefile at all — fail-open.
  - Fix direction: join logical lines before these regexes. Closing the parser half alone is NOT sufficient and must not be done alone — it converts the dataflow gate's existing over-approximation into false `Generate.static` fails. The candidate rule (an actual is a dependency OUTPUT unless assigned earlier) and the consumption rule (assignment-only backward closure) must become sound in the same change, which is the flow-sensitive, interface-aware dataflow pass recorded as the optional item above. Two flow-insensitive repairs are ruled out: counting an earlier `call` as a producer and excluding `parameter`s does not clear the false flags, and closing the consumption over call actuals fails open, which `test_discarded_dep_result_flagged_even_when_call_shares_an_input` pins against.
  - Completion criterion: the three gates read joined logical lines; the dependency-dataflow gate's verdict is unchanged on every `*_model.f90` in the tree; the wrapped `intent(out)` and wrapped `use` cases have regression tests.
  - **DONE (2026-08-12).** Suite 4509 -> 4546 passed, 1 skipped (the pre-existing calibration skip of the retention item below), re-measured at the end of the branch rather than mid-way. `ruff check`: `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py` is clean on both revisions, and the two touched test files carry an identical finding histogram to origin/main (3 E401, 18 E702, 4 E741, 8 F401, 4 F541, 9 F841 across them — all pre-existing). Both figures were wrong in an earlier draft of this entry: the suite total was stale by ~20 tests, and the ruff claim said "the same single finding", which was true only of the validator module read alone. Landed as one shared view — `_joined_masked_fortran_view` (`fortran_logical_lines` + `split_fortran_statements` + `mask_code_lookalikes`) — read by the three `problem` model gates, by `_split_fortran_names`, by `_local_fortran_module_map` / `_fortran_source_module_deps`, and by both copies of the metric scan.
    - **Two claims recorded above are FALSIFIED, and the correction is what made this closable without the flow-sensitive pass.** (a) Joining flips *two* real models, not three: the byte-identical `shallow_water2d` pair under `workspace_20260706`. The third file named above is `profile/`-domain, and the gate returns for any `node_key` outside `problem/`, so it never reads it — that residue is now recorded against the optional item above, with its reproduction. (b) "Excluding `parameter`s does not clear the false flags" is true only OF THAT `profile/` file. On every file the gate actually reads, the named-constant clause clears every flip. Both corrections came from executing the measurement, not from re-reading the note.
    - **Measured differential**, gate invoked directly over all 365 `*_model.f90` in the tree, before -> after: 29 violations -> 27. **Harness, without which those absolute figures are not reproducible** (a reviewer deriving `dep_spec_ids` differently got 35 -> 33 and 31 -> 29 for the same tree): `dep_spec_ids` taken from each file's own `use <spec_id>_model` lines, `node_key` forced to `problem/`. The DELTA and every movement below reproduce under all three derivations. No `problem/`-domain FILE changes verdict. At subroutine granularity three things move, each checked against the source: `initialize_state` (two 20260319 artifacts) stops being flagged — correct, its one candidate `ng_default` is a module-level `parameter`, so this was a false positive; `shallow_water2d__run_case`, a DIFFERENT subroutine of the same file, keeps firing with `ng_default` and `shallow_water2d__gravity` (also a module `parameter`) dropped from its candidate list; `shallow_water2d__apply` (20260712) keeps firing with `f_loc` and `u_next` ADDED, names recovered from behind a `&` (fail-closed). The other two movements are the two `profile/` files, one gaining and one LOSING a violation.
    - The named-constant clause is not a relaxation: F2008 requires the actual associated with an `intent(out)`/`intent(inout)` dummy to be definable. Verified against the compiler, not the standard — `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` rejects both spellings with "Non-variable expression in variable definition context (actual argument to INTENT = OUT/INOUT)".
    - **The clause is FILE-WIDE and scope-free, and that is the most important design decision on this item.** A name is exempt only if the file declares it a constant and never declares it as anything else (variable, `associate` / `select type` binding, or `use ..., only:` import). A SCOPED version — reading Fortran's actual name resolution — was written four times across eight review rounds and defeated by every one of the following, each reproduced: a `parameter` inside a module `function`; a paren-less `subroutine`; an external procedure; a submodule `module procedure` separate body; a `block`; a NAMED `associate`; a contained procedure; a second module in the same file; a derived type's own `contains`; a statement label; an obsolescent `character*3` selector; `use` association; three spellings of an assignment to a variable named `module` (`module%v`, `module(1)%v`, `module(size(u, dim=1))`); three spellings of `use` association (`only:`, a bare rename with no `only`, and the `, non_intrinsic ::` module-nature prefix); and the one-word `selecttype`, whose blank F2008 makes optional. All of them are accepted by `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008`, because none of these words is reserved. Each was a way for a name to be constant SOMEWHERE and definable HERE, and because this set SUBTRACTS candidates, each miss was a SILENT gate. The set of mechanisms that make a Fortran name visible is not closed at this level of parsing, so a rule whose safety depends on enumerating them cannot be made safe by adding cases — asking the file-wide question instead makes the failure direction structural. Recognition of "declared otherwise" is where all the safety now sits, so it is written with the opposite polarity from the rest: any statement opening with one of F2008's specification keywords disqualifies every identifier it mentions, WITHOUT parsing its grammar.
