That closed eighteen `::`-less specification statements (`common /blk/ x`, `dimension x(3)`, `equivalence`, `data`, `namelist`, `pointer`, `target`, `save`, `allocatable`, `external`, `intent`, `volatile`, `asynchronous`, `codimension`, `protected`, `value`, `optional`, bare `common x`) in one move rather than eighteen. All reproductions are caught, because in each the name is also declared, imported or bound somewhere — though three of the sixteen were found AFTER the switch, in the recognition of "declared otherwise", so the rule moves the risk from an unbounded set of scoping mechanisms to a bounded set of declaration spellings rather than removing it. Cost: a false violation when one file legitimately uses a name as a constant in one procedure and a variable in another. Two independent reviewers converged on this rule.
    - Residues of the clause, reproduced and NOT fixed: a bare `use other_module` can import a VARIABLE whose name this file also declares constant, which no single-file rule can see; and an implicitly typed local cannot disqualify a name, which `Generate.gate` makes unreachable by requiring `implicit none` (fortitude **C001** — verified by running it; C003 is the `external` spec-list rule that `docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` tells the leaf to SUPPRESS, so the earlier citation of C003 here pointed at a check that is never enforced). Both are in the gate docstring, the second with a test pinning current behaviour so a rule change surfaces there.
    - **The join introduces one hazard of its own, closed in the same change:** `fortran_logical_lines` does not split on `;`, so `flag = .true.; call dep__op(h_in, tmp)` on one line would let `_assignment_records`' `([^\n!]+)` RHS pull the discarded `tmp` into `flag`'s sources and suppress the violation — fail-open, manufactured by a semicolon. The view therefore splits statements as well as joining lines.
    - **A fourth defect of the same class, not enumerated above, was absorbed:** the metric scans (`metrics(N) = ([^\n!]+)`, duplicated in `_validate_generate_outputs` and `_validate_generate_outputs_for_generation`) read the RHS to the physical newline, so a wrapped `metrics(1) = &` / `1.0` captured only the `&`, counted as non-literal, and let a model that hardcodes every metric slip under the literal floor of six. Both copies changed together and one test asserts both entry points.
    - Fail-closed changes were measured before landing, not argued: across all 357 directories containing `.f90` files the module-dependency map is byte-identical before and after and no directory newly requires a Makefile it lacks; no `*_model.f90` in the tree changes its metric counts. No real source here wraps a `use` or a metric assignment — which is why both holes survived unnoticed.
    - `test_split_fortran_names_masks_comments_in_a_continued_list` pinned the OLD behaviour by name (that the name after `&` is dropped), so it was inverted and renamed rather than deleted, keeping both of its original reproducers.
    - NOT addressed, and not claimed: the `profile/`-domain false positive (recorded against the optional item above); the greedy `re.sub(r"\(.*\)", "", part)` strip in `_split_fortran_names`, which still reduces an expression actual `a(i)*b(j)` to `a` — fail-closed, and bounded empirically by the tree-wide differential rather than by argument; its sibling, which is fail-OPEN and was missing from this list until a reviewer reproduced it: a component actual (`call dep__op(h_in, state%tmp)`) fails `_split_fortran_names`' identifier `fullmatch` and is dropped entirely, so a dependency result written into a derived-type component is not a candidate at all (pre-existing, silent on origin/main too); the near-identical duplication of the two gate call-site blocks, recorded as its own item below.

- **Close the `_PROBLEM_SUBROUTINE_ENVELOPE` terminator and interface-body holes** (2026-08-12, high — reproduced, pre-existing). `_PROBLEM_SUBROUTINE_ENVELOPE` (`tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py`) requires `end\s+subroutine`, so three shapes `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` accepts silence ALL THREE `problem` model gates at once, for the whole file: the one-word `endsubroutine name`, a bare `end` terminator, and an `interface` block inside the subroutine (whose interface body's `end subroutine` closes the envelope early, truncating the body before the `call` is reached). Reproduced end to end with a genuinely discarded dependency output: the gate reports nothing, and the same file with `end subroutine` reports `does not propagate dependency operation outputs`. Of the three, `fortitude check` catches only the bare `end` (S061, measured). The one-word `endsubroutine name` passes clean, and an in-subroutine `interface` whose body declares `implicit none` — i.e. one that COMPLIES with fortitude's own C002 — also passes clean, so complying makes that shape reachable rather than closing it. Two of the three shapes are therefore reachable through the whole `Generate.gate` chain; an earlier draft of this item claimed C002 covered the interface case and was wrong.
  - Not a regression — silent on origin/main too — but it is the ENTRY CONDITION for every gate the 2026-08-12 continuation work hardened, so that work is worth much less until this is closed. Deliberately not fixed in that branch: it is a different defect with a different blast radius (all three gates, whole file), and folding it in would have widened an already long review.
  - Fix direction: the envelope is a regex over a text that `_joined_masked_fortran_view` has already reduced to one statement per line — so match the terminator against the same statement forms the rest of the module now recognises (`end`, `endsubroutine`, `end subroutine`), and skip `interface` / `end interface` spans before matching, rather than widening the single regex.
  - Completion criterion: a model whose only defect is a discarded dependency result is flagged under all three terminator spellings and with an interface block present; the three `problem` gates keep their current verdicts on every `*_model.f90` in the tree.
  - **DONE (2026-08-12).** Suite: origin/main collects 4547, this branch **4579** (4578 passed, 1 skipped — the pre-existing calibration skip of the retention item below); the 32 added tests are the difference. Two earlier figures in this entry were wrong: a baseline of 4509 carried over from a previous PR and never measured, then 4569, which was correct when written and went stale as later review rounds added tests without it being re-run. Both are `pytest --collect-only` on a `git archive` of origin/main and a full run on the branch. `ruff check` reports the same single finding (E741) on the two touched files as origin/main does. Landed not as a widened regex but as one walk over the view — `_fortran_subroutine_envelopes`, returning `(name, dummy_args, body, out_scope)` per subroutine — read by all three `problem` gates, which now differ from each other only in which fields they take. `body` runs through a subroutine's contained procedures; `out_scope` stops at its own `contains` and is where `intent(out)` is read.
    - **Completion criterion, first half**: the reproduction is a matrix, five terminator spellings (`end subroutine solve`, `end subroutine`, `endsubroutine solve`, `endsubroutine`, `end`) x with and without an in-body `interface` block whose own body carries `end subroutine`, run against all three gates with each gate's own defect shape. All flagged. The ten Fortran shapes were re-checked against the compiler rather than the standard: `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` accepts all ten. `fortitude check` (the linter `Generate.gate` runs; measured under the DEFAULT rule set on 0.8.0, before issue #111 made the gate impose a declared set — S061 is in that set, so the measurement stands) reports S061 "end statement should be named" for **6 of the 10** — every UNNAMED terminator, i.e. `end subroutine`, `endsubroutine` and `end`, with or without the interface block. An earlier draft of this entry said "for the bare `end` only", which is true only of the THREE defect shapes named in the item above (`endsubroutine name` and the interface shape are both named-terminator forms and pass clean) and false as written about the ten. The item's own claim is the one that matters for reachability and it survives re-measurement.
    - **Completion criterion, second half**: gate invoked directly over all 365 `*_model.f90` in the tree — `dep_spec_ids` from each file's own `use <spec_id>_model` lines, `node_key` forced to `problem/`, files enumerated with `os.walk` — the violation lists of all three gates are **byte-identical** before and after (literal-outputs 38, dependency-dataflow 27, metric-only kernel 4). Nothing moved, so nothing needed justifying. Stronger, and the reason the differential could be trusted at all: every one of the **894 envelopes** in the tree differs from the old parse by exactly one leading newline and by nothing else. (Enumerating the corpus needs `find` / `os.walk`, NOT `grep -r`: in an interactive shell here `grep` is a ripgrep alias that honours `.gitignore`, and the workspaces holding 364 of the 365 files are ignored — a first pass reported 1 file and 0 `interface` carriers, both wrong.)
    - **Where the walk must choose, it reads a body TOO LONG.** An END statement matching no open frame is ignored rather than popping something, and a subroutine still open at end of input is emitted to the end of input rather than — as before — not emitted at all. A body read too long mis-attributes or over-reports; a body read too short is a silent gate, which is the failure being removed. Both directions have their own test.
    - The one enumeration this rests on is F2008's END statements for program units and subprograms (program, module, submodule, block data, function, subroutine, separate module subprogram), which is closed: every other `end X` closes an executable construct or a derived type. It is spelled `end\s*` (blank optional) like `_IFACE_PROC_END` and `_FORTRAN_CONSTRUCT_END` already are. The prefix words before `subroutine` are deliberately NOT enumerated — F2008 has `pure`/`impure`/`elemental`/`recursive`/`module` and F2018 adds `non_recursive`, so that set is not closed across standards; over-matching is prevented by testing the END rules first instead.
    - **The mutation check found five alternatives no test could kill, and two of them were real gaps.** `abstract interface` and the one-word `endinterface` each had no row, and the `abstract` spelling is invisible to every BEHAVIOURAL test — a missed span opener lets the interface body's own `subroutine` / `end subroutine` pair push and pop in balance, so the enclosing envelope survives and only a phantom envelope appears. It is pinned on the envelope list instead.
    - **The mutation check's third "gap" was a mistake of mine, and review caught it as a fail-open.** I removed this branch's own derived-type tracking on the argument that a type definition cannot sit inside a subroutine, citing a gfortran rejection. The rejection was real but the generalisation was not: `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` rejects a type-bound binding to a HOST-associated procedure — the only shape I probed — and ACCEPTS the same type inside the same subroutine when the binding is `nopass` to a USE-associated module procedure. Without the tracking the type's `contains` cut the body at the type definition and all three gates went silent on a discarded dependency result: a fail-open regression against origin/main, shipped in the second commit of this branch and reverted in the fourth. The test that was supposed to cover it put the type at module level, where the cut cannot happen however the walk is broken. **The rule this violates is already written down** — do not classify anything as unreachable on an argument; classify it on an executed attempt to reach it, and one probe is not the attempt.
    - **A second fail-open of this branch's own making, also from review.** Making the terminator blank optional (`end\s*`) is the point of the fix, and it also means `endsubroutine`, `endprocedure`, `endmodule` and friends are legal VARIABLE names: `real :: endsubroutine` / `endsubroutine = 1.0` in a body closed the subroutine at that line, silencing all three gates for the rest of it (`endmodule` cascaded through the module frame). gfortran accepts every spelling. The openers each carried a `(?!\s*[=(%])` lookahead for exactly this and the terminator carried none — the guard was on the rules whose misfire is fail-CLOSED and missing from the one whose misfire is fail-OPEN. Fixed as **one predicate** (`_fortran_statement_assigns_to_its_first_token` — a statement that assigns to its own first token is never structural, which is Fortran's own disambiguation) applied before every rule, with the per-rule lookaheads removed; adding a fourth lookahead would have been the move this repository keeps having to unlearn.
    - **Prose refuted by review and corrected, listed because the correction is the record**: `_FORTRAN_END_KIND_CLOSES` claimed a `module procedure` body may end with `end subroutine` or `end function` — gfortran says "Expecting END PROCEDURE statement" to both, and "Expecting END SUBROUTINE statement" to the mirror, so an END now closes only its own kind (the five cross-kind members were also unkillable by any test, which is how the false sentence survived); three of six F2008 rule numbers cited here and in the code were wrong, so the numbers are gone and the compiler is cited instead; "F2008 Table 3.1" is the special-characters table, not the blanks-optional keyword list, in this item and in two pre-existing comments.
    - **A cost this entry once accepted, and should not have.** An earlier draft recorded, as a known fail-closed price, that cutting a host body at its `contains` falsely flags a model whose dependency result is propagated to the host's `intent(out)` inside a contained procedure by host association. Review then produced the OTHER direction of the same cut — a dependency call inside a contained procedure with the `intent(out)` in the host lands in an envelope with no `intent(out)` at all, so every gate returns at its empty out-set check: **fail-open, and a regression against origin/main**, which flags it. They were one mistake seen from two sides, and "the alternative is worse" was an argument for keeping the mistake. The cut is gone; `body` runs through contained procedures and `out_scope` stops at the `contains`, so neither direction remains. Both are asserted in one test. Reachability of either was zero — 0 of the 365 `*_model.f90` define a contained procedure — which is exactly why the tree differential could not see them, and why the differential is not evidence about this class.
    - **Two mutations still survive and are recorded rather than hidden**: removing the `function` frames and the `module procedure` frame changes no envelope on any shape tried (a contained function ended by a bare `end`, a module-level function between two subroutines, a submodule separate body with its own contained procedure), because only subroutine frames emit, an unmatched END is ignored, and a host body is already cut at its `contains`. They are kept because the direction a wrong pop fails in is a subroutine closing early — fail-open — not because anything measured them. This is written in the helper's docstring too.
    - Two further weaknesses of the old regex, neither named in this item, were closed by the same walk: it required a PARENTHESISED dummy list, so a paren-less `subroutine solve` was never matched and its terminator paired with a neighbour's; and it had no notion of `contains`, so a contained procedure's `intent(out)` declarations were attributed to its host as well. A contained procedure now gets its own envelope, and its declarations stay out of its host's out-scope while its statements stay inside its host's body.
    - Corrected while writing this: an earlier draft of the helper's docstring justified blanking the interface span (rather than deleting it) by claiming deletion would break the dependency-dataflow gate's assigned-before-call comparison. It would not — deletion preserves the ORDER of what remains. What blanking actually buys is that the body stays ONE contiguous slice of a length-preserving transform of the view, so a position in a body is a position in the view. The test that pins it says so, and says why no behavioural test can.
    - NOT addressed, and not claimed: a `function` body is still not an envelope, so a `problem` model that computes inside a module `function` is invisible to all three gates — 92 of the 365 `*_model.f90` define one. Filed as its own item below. The two near-identical gate call-site blocks (item below) are also untouched.

- **Make a `function` body and a `module procedure` body reachable by the three `problem` model gates** (2026-08-12, high — reproduced, pre-existing). All three `problem` model gates read only `subroutine` definitions: `_fortran_subroutine_envelopes` (`tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py`) tracks `function` frames so their terminators pair correctly, but emits no envelope for them. A `problem` model that computes inside a module `function` is therefore invisible to every one of them, for the whole procedure. Reproduced: the same body that reports `does not propagate dependency operation outputs (candidates=['scratch'])` as `subroutine solve(x, y)` reports NOTHING as `function solve(x) result(y)`, with `_fortran_subroutine_envelopes` returning the empty list; `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` accepts the function form. Not a regression: origin/main's flat envelope span was equally blind, and the 2026-08-12 envelope work did not widen it.
  - Reachability, measured with `os.walk` (not `grep -r`, which is ripgrep here and honours `.gitignore`): **92 of the 365 `*_model.f90` in this tree define a `function`**, so this is not a theoretical shape — though none of the 92 currently carries the defect any gate looks for, which is why closing it is expected to move the tree differential and this item cannot be treated as a no-op.
  - **The same gap has a second spelling, found twice independently in review**: a SEPARATE MODULE SUBPROGRAM written `module procedure solve` (rather than `module subroutine solve(u, v)`) also produces no envelope, so all three gates are silent on the whole procedure while the `module subroutine` spelling of the identical body is analysed; both compile. It cannot be closed by emitting an envelope alone, and this is the part to get right before starting: such a body may NOT redeclare its dummies — `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` answers "is a redefinition of the declaration in the corresponding interface" — so no `intent(out)` ever appears in it, and all three gates return at `if not out_vars: continue` no matter what text they are handed. Both spellings therefore need the same missing ingredient as the `function` case: the dummy intents read from the declaration that owns them (the interface body, or the function result), not from the body.
  - Fix direction: emit an envelope for a `function` too, carrying the result name (from `result(...)` or the function name) as the definable output the way `intent(out)` dummies are for a subroutine. All three gates' rules key on `intent(out)`, so each needs a decision about what its out-set means for a function — that is the work, not the walk, which already knows where a function starts and ends.
  - Completion criterion: the reproduction above flags in both forms; a differential over all 365 `*_model.f90` for all three gates is taken and every movement justified file by file against the source (the 2026-08-12 envelope change was byte-identical, so its baseline is reusable).
  - **Do not hand-roll the missing half. Four candidate front ends were measured (2026-08-12/13) against two corpora — the 365 in-tree `*_model.f90`, and the ~41 adversarial fixtures this branch's tests accumulated, each already accepted by `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008`. Numbers, not impressions:**
    - **tree-sitter-fortran** (`tree_sitter` + `tree_sitter_fortran`): parsed 365/365 with no ERROR node, its subroutine set matched `_fortran_subroutine_envelopes` on 365/365, and it yielded every dummy's intent for 883/883 subroutines; 0.9 ms/file against the hand-rolled walk's 1.1. On the adversarial corpus, 7 of 41 carry ERROR nodes and 5 disagree — it shares the walk's LEXICAL weakness (`endsubroutine` as a variable or construct name is read as `end` + `subroutine`) — but **every disagreement carries an ERROR node: "silently wrong" was 0 of 41**, so a fail-closed contract is available. What it cannot do is resolve a name across the interface↔implementation boundary, which is exactly this item's missing ingredient.
    - **fparser2 0.2.4: rejected.** It cannot parse `module procedure` at all (`FortranSyntaxError`) — the very shape this item needs — and supports only f2003/f2008, so `implicit none (type, external)` (F2018, and the spelling fortitude's C003 asks for) fails: 7 of the 365 in-tree files do not parse, 10 carry that statement. Where it parsed it was never silently wrong (358/358 agreement), which is its one virtue.
    - **`gfortran -fdump-fortran-original`: no new dependency and no new execution point.** `Generate.static` is not a substep — the three gates are the `static` checker INSIDE `generate.gate`, alongside the `syntax` checker, which already stages the node's src plus the certified dependency closure into a throwaway dir and runs gfortran with `-J/-I` there. Adding the dump flag to that same argv was executed: the dump appears (even when a `-Werror` finding makes rc=1) and carries `Formal arglist:`, `attributes: (VARIABLE DUMMY(IN))` / `DUMMY(OUT)`, the CONTAINS nesting, and name-resolved code (`CALL dep__apply ((solve:x) (solve:scratch))`). An earlier draft of this note claimed the dump therefore had to move to Compile/Validate because it needs a compilable unit; that was measured in the wrong configuration (files compiled standalone or with siblings, no certified closure) and is withdrawn.
    - **LLVM flang: technically the strongest of the four, and deliberately kept OUT of the gate.** `flang -fc1 -fdebug-unparse-no-sema` returns CANONICAL Fortran — continuations joined, one statement per line, keywords upper-cased while user identifiers keep their spelling, so `END SUBROUTINE` (keyword) and `endsubroutine` (a legal variable or construct name) stop being the same token. It needs no dependency staging, it works on source that FAILS semantic analysis (plain `-fdebug-unparse` returns nothing there, and a gate must read broken code), and it succeeded on **365/365** in-tree files and on every adversarial fixture `gfortran` accepts (46/46; the 4 it refuses are ones gfortran refuses too). Measured across **four LLVM releases installed side by side — 17.0.6, 18.1.8, 19.1.7, 21.1.8** — the output is **byte-identical on 365/365 and 46/46**; `-fdebug-unparse` (with semantics) is identical on 244/244, and `-fdebug-dump-symbols` is identical on 244/244 once compiler-internal symbols are excluded (the only cross-version difference is that 18+ emit `__builtin_c_ptr` and 17 does not). Its symbol dump also answers this item's question directly, printing `x, INTENT(IN) … ObjectEntity dummy` under the submodule's `solve` with an explicit `moduleInterface:` link.
    - **`-emit-fir` (the MLIR/FIR path) is rejected.** It carries the most information — `fir.declare … {fortran_attrs = #fir.var_attrs<intent_out>}`, `fir.host_assoc`, SSA loads and stores — but lowering mangles names (`_QMmFsolveEscratch`), turns host association into a tuple of pointers and arrays into descriptors, so every finding has to be mapped back to source identity; the dialect is expected to move with each LLVM release, which is the opposite of what the gfortran-version comparison below was looking for; and `flang-new` accepts only `-std=f2018`, so it cannot enforce the standard this toolchain sets.
    - **Why flang is not in the gate anyway: distribution, and this repository's own precedent.** `flang-21` costs ~96 MB plus `libllvm21` (136 MB), `libmlir-21` (96 MB) and `libclang-cpp21` (60 MB); the binary is named `flang-new` on LLVM 17-19 and `flang` / `flang-21` on 21; conda-forge ships it for linux-64 and win-64 only (no macOS, and the same package name once meant the unrelated PGI-derived "classic flang"); Homebrew covers macOS. Against that, `gfortran` is already a hard requirement of the toolchain. And `_gate_syntax_check` already fixes the rule for optional compilers: `ATMOFAB_SYNTAX_COMPILERS` may add stages, but gfortran is forced first and mandatory ("the one stage post_generate certification requires to have passed") and an absent optional compiler is recorded as `skipped`, "so one configuration runs on machines with and without the target compiler". A "flang if present, else tree-sitter" gate would invert that — it would make the VERDICT depend on which machine ran it, and it would need the same rules implemented twice, which is the failure this repository keeps paying for (the shadowed `_split_top_level_commas`, the mirror gate/validator pairs, and the three copies of one rule in this very file of which only one was pinned).
  - **DECIDED (2026-08-13), CORRECTED THE SAME DAY BY MEASUREMENT: the gate path is tree-sitter ALONE; the gfortran-dump half is dropped, and flang becomes a REQUIREMENT OF THE DEVELOPMENT ENVIRONMENT, never of a run.** The dump was to supply the one thing tree-sitter cannot — the dummy intents of a separate module subprogram. Re-probing the three markers this entry declares safe to read (`procedure name =` / `Formal arglist:` / `DUMMY(...)`, gfortran 12.4.0 and 14.2.0, executed) shows they carry **neither the result name nor the function/subroutine distinction**: the result variable appears only in the version-divergent `attributes:` region this entry forbids reading. So a completed dump pipeline could only ever have rescued a subroutine-shaped `module procedure` with at least one `intent(out)` dummy — of which this tree has **zero** — and would still have refused every function-shaped one. The abbreviated form is refused outright instead (below), and the dump path is kept here as a FUTURE item for the day a real `module procedure` appears in the corpus and the refusal proves too blunt. The rest of this DECIDED note stands: Its measured strengths — canonical output that four LLVM releases agree on byte for byte, and a front end that neither breaks on keyword-named identifiers nor needs the code to be semantically valid — are exactly what an independent cross-check needs. Use it there: a differential harness over the 365 models plus the adversarial fixtures that asserts the gate's structure agrees with flang's, and that the set tree-sitter reports as ERROR is the set that genuinely needs refusing. It never contributes to a verdict, so no run's outcome depends on whether flang is installed. The dependency-declaration item below owns writing this down alongside the two Python packages.
  - **Design, and where its risk actually lives (2026-08-12, measured across two installed gfortran versions).** Take structure from tree-sitter and ask gfortran ONLY for what tree-sitter cannot resolve — the dummy intents of a separate module subprogram, and nothing else. The reason to draw the line exactly there is not taste: comparing `-fdump-fortran-original` from gfortran **12.4.0** and **14.2.0** over both corpora, 27 of the 319 analysable files differ in the dump, and the differences fall in `attributes:` lines at PROCEDURE level, the `symtree:` listing, `type spec`, and — most importantly — the `code:` section, where 14 renders a construct as `SELECT TYPE` and 12 renders it as `BLOCK`. Meanwhile **every one of the 4328 `DUMMY(...)` lines is identical across the two versions, and the extracted marker set (`procedure name =` / `Formal arglist:` / `DUMMY(...)`) matched on 319/319 files.** So:
    - read only those three markers; **never read `code:`** — that is the part that changes with the compiler on the machine, which makes it an environment-dependent verdict rather than a portable one;
    - do not use the procedure-level `attributes:` to decide what is a procedure (tree-sitter's job);
    - the sample is two Debian gcc builds, which is evidence and not a guarantee, so the extractor must **fail closed when a marker is absent** and the gfortran version must be recorded in the gate evidence;
    - the second stage will be COLD — `module procedure` occurs in 0 of the 365 in-tree files — and a cold fail-closed path that quietly stops working is how this repository produced silent gates in the first place. Pin it with a suite test that runs the REAL gfortran over a `module procedure` fixture and asserts both that the markers are found and that a missing marker fails closed; with more than one gfortran installed, run it against each.
    - Changing `run_syntax_check`'s argv is a change to what the capability gate constrains, so it goes through `atmofab-enforcement-change` (surface inventory, failure attribution, fail-closed on absence) rather than being treated as a parser refactor.
  - **DONE (2026-08-14).** Both spellings are closed, in two changes: the front-end swap recorded above, then the visibility change itself.
    - **A `function` is an envelope, and its RESULT VARIABLE is a definable output** — the `result(...)` name, or the function's own name when it declares none. Which of the two is a variable was re-checked against the compiler rather than the standard: with `result(y)` present, `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` rejects an assignment to the function name ("'f' at (1) is not a variable") and accepts one to `y`; with no `result(...)` it accepts the assignment to the function name (both executed). The item's own reproduction now flags in BOTH forms — one body, two spellings, one verdict, `candidates=['scratch']` in each — asserted as a pair so that silencing one cannot look like a passing suite, and across all five function terminator spellings with and without an in-body interface.
    - **The abbreviated `module procedure solve` is REFUSED, always**, with a message naming the full form (`module subroutine s(u, v)` / `module function f(x) result(y)`). Not because its body is hard to find — the parser reports it exactly — but because F2008 forbids that body from redeclaring its dummies (`gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008`: "is a redefinition of the declaration in the corresponding interface"), so no `intent(out)` can appear in it and every gate would return at its empty out-set check while looking like it had run. The refusal is per-PROCEDURE, not per-file: the gates still read the file's other procedures, because an earlier version stopped the whole file there and thereby hid a real literal-only violation in a sibling subroutine behind an unrelated instruction (found by mutation).
    - **THE MEASUREMENT THAT CHANGED THE DESIGN.** Putting the result variable into all three gates' out-set unchanged was executed against all 365 in-tree `*_model.f90` first: it produced **10 new literal-outputs violations, all 10 legitimate, 0 defects** — every one a predicate whose input dependence lives in its BRANCH CONDITIONS (`res = .false.` … `if (lhs(k) < rhs(k)) res = .true.`), which that gate's flow-insensitive test cannot see, or a constant accessor (`ghost_width() result(ng); ng = 1`). So the literal-outputs gate still requires an `intent(out)` DUMMY to fire; the result stays in the out-set, where it can only ever exempt. The other two gates take the full set. A function WITH an `intent(out)` dummy is now checked by all three, which is the widening this delivers.
    - **Final differential, `origin/main` (`4bbeece`) vs the branch, over all 365 models**: envelopes **894 → 1316** (+422, the function and separate-module-subprogram bodies that were invisible), violations **byte-identical** — literal-outputs 38, dependency-dataflow 27, metric-only-kernel 4, 0 files refused. The verdict movement is zero and the visibility movement is the +422, which is exactly the completion criterion this item asked for. `--flang` (Debian flang 21.1.8) agrees on 365/365 with both kinds compared.
    - Suite **4650 passed / 0 skipped** at the end of the review rounds (origin/main 4616; the figure was 4638 when this bullet was first written, three review rounds and 20 pins earlier); `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 on both, identical histogram. Two leaf-doc ceilings were bumped WITH the reason recorded rather than the rules trimmed away (`skills/workflow-generate-generate/SKILL.md` 37900→38400, `skills/workflow-generate-verify/SKILL.md` 28200→28500): a reviewer checklist still saying "subroutine" would tell the leaf to skip exactly the procedures the gate just started reading.
    - **Twin docs updated in the same change** (the rule these state is the one that moved): `docs/workflow/phases/phase_02_generate.md` (the static-check dataflow paragraph), `skills/workflow-generate-generate/SKILL.md` (the authoring rule + the full-form requirement), `skills/workflow-generate-verify/SKILL.md` (the metric-only-kernel review item). `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` and `mcp_servers/README.md` needed nothing: neither states what the gate reads, and no MCP argv changed — the dump half being dropped is what keeps the capability-gate surface untouched by all of this.
  - Dependency policy: adopting tree-sitter adds two packages to a repository that declares no dependencies at all — see the MCP-tests-and-CI item below, which owns that gap. The gfortran half adds nothing.
  - **FRONT END SWAPPED (2026-08-13), half of this item. The gap itself is still open** — a `function` body is still not an envelope; what changed is what the envelopes are computed FROM. `_fortran_subroutine_envelopes` is now an adapter over `tools/backends/language/fortran/structure.parse_view` (tree-sitter-fortran 0.6.0 / tree-sitter 0.26.0) and the regex walk is deleted, so widening the gates to a function's result is now a change to ONE reader instead of a sixteenth patch to an enumeration of spellings. Deliberately semantics-preserving, and measured rather than asserted: over all 365 in-tree `*_model.f90` (enumerated with `os.walk`), the listing of all **894 envelope tuples** and all **69 violations** is BYTE-IDENTICAL to `origin/main` (`4bbeece`) — literal-outputs 38, dependency-dataflow 27, metric-only-kernel 4, 0 refused. `tools/backends/language/fortran/structure_differential.py` is that listing, run at two revisions and diffed; its `--flang` half agrees with `flang -fc1 -fdebug-unparse-no-sema` (Debian flang 21.1.8) on 365/365. Suite 4630 passed / 0 skipped (4616 before); `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 on both, identical histogram.
    - **What the swap COSTS, measured over the test matrices rather than argued.** A parse carrying an ERROR/MISSING node is refused (a content violation naming the rewrite) instead of read partially. Of the 48 rows assigning to a variable named after a keyword, **38 are still analysed and flagged and 10 are refused** — `endsubroutine`, `interface` and `contains` at all three shapes each, plus `real :: type(3)`, an array declaration of a variable named `type` being character-for-character a derived-type declaration. Of the 35 construct-name rows, **20 are analysed and 15 refused** (the same three names, at all five constructs). Both counts were re-measured; an earlier draft of this bullet said 44 of 48, derived by subtracting NAMES from ROWS rather than counting the refused set the test pins two lines away, and thereby understated the cost of the swap by 2.5x. Review caught it. Every refused row is a legal program (`gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008` accepts each, executed) and every one is repairable by a rename, which the violation message asks for. Both sets are pinned as SETS, so a change that refuses one more row fails a test instead of surfacing as a refused Generate in a billed run. Inputs gfortran REJECTS (a stray `end do`, an unmatched `end function`, an unterminated body or interface, a subroutine at module level) are refused too, where the walk read them deliberately long; none is reachable **for a node whose `toolchain.language` is `fortran`**, because `_gate_inproc` runs the static check only when lint AND syntax passed (`tools/workflow_conductor.py:7745`, read). That qualifier was missing from an earlier draft and review supplied it: `_gate_syntax_check` records `skipped_reason` and still returns `status=pass` for any other language (`:7974`), so a non-fortran node's static check runs with no compiler having run in front of it. Latent — a sweep of every `.yaml`/`.json`/`.md` in the tree finds `language:` set to a fortran value in all 101 occurrences and nothing else — but the unqualified sentence was false, and it is the sentence the refusals lean on.
    - **An absent parser is a TRANSPORT failure, not a content one.** `FortranStructureUnavailableError` → the validator's dedicated exit code → `_gate_static_check` → the terminal category `static_frontend_unavailable` (`GATE_FAILURE_TERMINAL`), so an operator's uninstalled package terminalizes the run instead of burning the leaf's retry budget on something no source can fix. **This sentence used to end "the marker is imported at the scan site, never spelled there"; there is no scan site any more** — TODO:269 moved the decision to the exit code, and `_gate_static_check` imports only the two `*_EXIT_CODE` constants. The marker survives in the message for a human reader and carries no decision. Exercised in a real subprocess with the import really broken (not `mock.patch`), asserting both that the marker comes back AND that the literal-outputs violation the same source would otherwise earn does NOT — i.e. the failure mode being closed is a silent PASS.
    - **A fail-open of my own, and where it was caught.** The first version blanked interface spans one line too far (a node span may include its terminating newline), deleting the first statement after `end interface` from the body — all three gates silent when that statement is the only assignment to the `intent(out)` dummy. The **365-file differential could not see it** (0 of the 365 declares an interface inside a body); the terminator x interface acceptance matrix did. Recorded because it is the second time on this item that the corpus differential has been mistaken for evidence about a class it structurally cannot reach.
    - **Declared at the LAUNCH check, not only in prose (Codex review, 2026-08-14).** Nothing in this repository installs the two packages, so a machine that satisfies RUNBOOK §0-1 today does not have them — and their absence surfaced only at the first `Generate` node's gate, after lint and syntax passed, i.e. part-way into a billed run, where it correctly terminalizes. Correct failure, wrong moment. `tools/run_workflow.py` now checks them at startup beside `REQUIRED_CLI_TOOLS` (same precedent, same rationale: "missing any one fails the run before init") and names the pip distributions in the message; RUNBOOK §0-1 lists them. A real dependency MANIFEST is still the MCP-tests-and-CI item's, and is still the right home — this closes the timing, not the declaration.
  - Installed to `~/.local` (user site), not system dist-packages: the three gates run conductor-side on the host (`_gate_static_check` shells `python3 tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py`), and no leaf launch prompt runs `validate_pipeline_semantics` under bwrap, so a user-site install covers the production path. If a leaf-side path is ever added, bwrap ro-binds `/usr` and not `~/.local` (`_runtime_ro_bind_paths`), so that path would fail CLOSED on the dedicated exit code rather than go quiet (this said "on the marker" before TODO:269 moved the decision off the text) — but it would fail, and the fix is a system-wide install. The formal dependency declaration is still owned by the MCP-tests-and-CI item.

- **A source needing statement labels both kept and stripped is refused** (2026-08-14, low — reproduced, declared residue of the front-end swap). The canonical view strips statement labels so every rule can anchor on a statement's own keyword; a parser needs some of them (a labelled `DO`'s terminator, a `FORMAT`) and is broken by others (tree-sitter cannot parse `10 contains` or `20 subroutine helper(v)`, both of which gfortran accepts). `_fortran_procedure_envelopes` therefore parses the stripped reading first and the label-preserving twin as a fallback, and refuses only when NEITHER resolves — which a file carrying a labelled `contains` AND a labelled `DO` does. `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008 -Wall` accepts such a file.
  - Fail-closed, and pinned as refused (`test_a_file_needing_both_label_readings_at_once_is_refused`) so it is visible rather than rediscovered. Reachability: **0 of the 365 in-tree `*_model.f90` carry a statement label of any kind**, so no model needs either reading today.
  - Closing it means choosing the reading PER STATEMENT rather than per file — an error-guided repair loop (strip the labels the parser reports errors on, re-parse, bounded) rather than a fourth rule about which labels matter. Three such rules were written and each was defeated by a legal program in the next review round, which is why this is recorded rather than attempted a fourth time.

- **A never-called contained procedure silences the `problem` model gates** (2026-08-14, low — reproduced, pre-existing on both revisions). An envelope's `body` runs to the procedure's own `end`, its `contains` section included, so an internal procedure that nothing calls still contributes assignments to the gates' reasoning. A `solve` whose dependency result is discarded is flagged; adding a dead `contains`-ed procedure that assigns the discarded name to the `intent(out)` silences it. `gfortran -fsyntax-only -std=f2008 -Wall` accepts both, and both `origin/main` and the front-end branch behave identically — this is a property of the flow-insensitive assignment closure, not of the parser swap.
  - **The one-line fix is known and is known to be WRONG.** Cutting `body` at `contains` (the way `out_scope` already is) was tried during the 2026-08-12 envelope work and reverted: it makes a `call` inside a contained procedure, with the `intent(out)` in the host, land in an envelope with no `intent(out)` at all, so every gate returns at its empty out-set check — fail-OPEN, and a regression against origin/main. Both directions are documented in `_FortranProcedureEnvelope`'s docstring. The honest closure needs the flow-sensitive pass already recorded above, which can tell a dead procedure from a called one.
  - Reachability: 0 of the 365 in-tree `*_model.f90` define a contained procedure at all.

- **`STALE_DEPENDENCY_IR_MARKER` is forgeable from a leaf-chosen path, the same way the frontend marker was** (2026-08-14, medium — reproduced, pre-existing). `_gate_static_check` (`tools/workflow_conductor.py`) classifies a `post_generate` failure by searching the validator's whole output for `[stale-dependency-ir]`, and most violations interpolate a path the LEAF chose. A source file or directory whose name contains that literal therefore turns an ordinary content violation into `stale_dependency_ir`, which `GATE_FAILURE_TERMINAL` fail-closes — the run stops instead of warm-retrying `Generate.generate`, and the operator is pointed at a certified dependency IR that is fine.
  - Direction is fail-CLOSED, so this is misattribution and a burnt retry budget, not a gate bypass. Reproduced for the sibling marker `[fortran-structure-unavailable]` with a model source named `[fortran-structure-unavailable]_model.f90`, which produced the ordinary "present but must be named" violation and was classified `static_frontend_unavailable`.
  - The sibling was fixed by emitting the marker at the START of its violation and anchoring the conductor's match to the start of a violation line. That fix does NOT transfer as-is: `_validate_infrastructure_generated_signatures` interpolates its location first (`f"{loc}: {STALE_DEPENDENCY_IR_MARKER} …"`), so anchoring would have to move the marker there too, and that message's shape is pinned by its own tests.
  - Fix direction: emit the stale-dependency violation marker-first as well and anchor both scans, or replace text-scanning with a channel the leaf cannot write into (a distinct validator exit code, or a machine-readable sidecar the gate reads instead of stdout). The second closes the class rather than the instance, and there are only two instances.
  - Deliberately not done in the front-end branch: it predates it, `origin/main` scans the marker identically, and changing the message shape of an unrelated gate widens that branch's review surface.
  - **The same misattribution exists at `post_execute`, for BOTH markers, and there it has no terminal route to reach.** `validate --stage post_execute` runs the same `_validate_generate_outputs` → `_run_problem_model_gates` path (read, confirmed), so an absent Fortran front end or a stale dependency IR can be reported there too — but `_execute_inproc` classifies any non-zero gate rc as `post_execute_violation`, and every entry of `VALIDATE_EXECUTE_FAILURE_ROUTING` is a warm `("generate", "reuse")`. So a machine problem asks the leaf to re-author its source, which cannot fix it, and the retry budget burns. Not fixed with the Generate-side anchor because it needs a TERMINAL category in the execute routing table — a different subsystem's contract — and because the same one-line change would be needed for the stale-IR marker anyway. This is the third instance of the class, which is the argument for the structured-channel fix above rather than a third anchored scan. **`--stage pre_judge` is the fourth**: it dispatches through the same `validate()` path and its conductor call site scans for neither marker either (review, read).
  - **DONE (dedicated validator exit code, 2026-08-21)** — closed as a CLASS, by the structured channel this entry named as the second fix direction, not by a third anchored scan. `validate_pipeline_semantics.STALE_DEPENDENCY_IR_EXIT_CODE = 4` joins the front end's rc 3, and the decision is carried by the violation's TYPE (`StaleDependencyIRViolation(str)`, wrapping the single emit site) so `main()` maps it without reading its own output. The message is byte-identical — a `str` subclass compares equal to the string it wraps — so every existing message pin stayed green. All four conductor readers were enumerated and answered: `_gate_static_check` and `_execute_inproc` and `_post_judge_inproc` now classify on the code, and `classify_failure` grew `VALIDATE_EXECUTE_FAILURE_TERMINAL` (a separate constant from `GATE_FAILURE_TERMINAL`, pinned equal today) with the trial_meta read hoisted above the C2 counter so a machine failure never reopens `Compile`. The two remaining readers, `_build_inproc`'s post_build gate and `_compile_static_inproc`, carry a comment recording why they read no terminal code.
  - **Review-loop disclosure (4 rounds, 9 reviewers + 1 Codex pass, 18 fix commits on top of 7 implementation commits).** Class descent: R1 = missing witnesses and wrong numbers; R2 = a test whose docstring stated a rule it did not pin, plus a missed twin doc; R3 = **one mechanism with no witness at all** (see below) plus an over-refusal my own R2 fix introduced; R4 = zero functional defects from both reviewers, and a reviewer's own 11-mutant sweep fully killed. Codex ran once in R2 and returned clean, which is recorded as one independent pass consumed, **not** as convergence evidence.
    - **The defect my own fixes introduced twice, both times inside a test written to answer a review finding: over-refusal.** R2's rewrite of the exit-code distinctness row refused a legitimate ALIAS of an existing code; R3's fix of that had to state what it stops pinning. This is the same direction recorded on PR #67 and PR #81, and the countermeasure that worked again is the one those entries name — construct the legitimate input the check rejects, and default a floor to FILL rather than REFUSE.
    - **The worst finding was mine to have caught: exit code 3 had no behavioural witness.** Mutating `_main_dispatch`'s `return FORTRAN_STRUCTURE_UNAVAILABLE_EXIT_CODE` to `return 1` left 1555 rows green, while this change added three readers keyed on that code, a `--help` line and a RUNBOOK entry — and built a real-subprocess witness for the TWIN (rc 4) in the same commits. The test that carried the name said in its comment "this runs `main` in a real interpreter" and never called `main`. One test per OCCURRENCE of a rule, not per rule; and grep a test's body for the functions its docstring claims to drive.
    - **The twin-doc sweep took three rounds and two methods.** Reading and grepping found 2 of 6 sites; matching every `.md` under `os.walk` for `disposition`+`recoverable` found 5 and was written up as "the record of how many sites there are"; a reviewer using a different match expression found the 6th, stated with the word "severity". A census is a census only when it does not depend on one phrasing.
    - **Three rounds of numeric rot in one paragraph, twice inside a commit written to cure it.** Closed by changing the FORM, not the numbers: every measurement now names the commit it was taken at, and the paragraph states the property the count stands for. This is the fourth entry in this file to record the same lesson, which is itself the evidence that restating numbers does not work.
    - **Not met: the superior stopping condition** (the security axis producing nothing new for two consecutive rounds). R1's security reviewer found the unpinned dev category set, so the run is at most one clean round. Stopped on the ordinary condition — class descent plus both bounded-remainder proxies holding in R4 — with the remainder being descriptive accuracy, which ran 6-10 findings per round and did not fall.
  - **CORRECTION to the paragraph above.** "The same misattribution exists at `post_execute` … for BOTH markers" is **wrong for the stale-IR marker**, on this revision and on `origin/main`. The stale-IR violation has ONE emit site (`_validate_infrastructure_generated_signatures`) with ONE caller (`_validate_generate_outputs_for_generation`), which only the `post_generate` path reaches; `--stage post_execute` and `--stage pre_judge` reach `_validate_generate_outputs`, a different function that does not call it. The front-end marker's half of the claim IS correct — rc 3 reaches all three stages. The rc-4 branches at post_execute and pre_judge are therefore wired for a condition that cannot arrive today, and say so in their comments: the alternative is that the day the emit site gains a second caller, the failure arrives as a warm retry no leaf can converge on.
  - Verification: the channel is driven end to end by REAL subprocess tests (a helper-level assertion would stay green while an unwrapped `append` or a rebuilt violations list degraded production to rc 1), and the forgery is pinned at all three classifying readers: `_gate_static_check` replays the front-end marker's full four-payload defeat history at rc 1, while the post_execute and pre_judge rows replay two payloads each (one per marker), which is what those readers need — neither consults any text, so the rows pin the ABSENCE of a scan rather than sampling one. **Every measurement below names the commit it was taken at, because this paragraph rotted three times without that.** Each restatement was correct when written and was falsified by the next round's own commits — twice by a commit whose stated purpose was to restate it. Numbers about a moving branch are historical records, not claims about the present; written as records they cannot go stale, and a reader who needs today's figure runs the command.

Mutation-checked over the whole `origin/main...HEAD` range with `mutation_check.py`. **At `7c6d187`: 17 non-test hunks at git's default `-U3` (21 at `-U0`, 15 at `-U5` — a bare "N hunks" means nothing without the width), 10 killed, 6 prose-only, 1 the deletion half of the `trial_meta` read's MOTION** (reverting it restores a redundant second read of the same file below, which changes nothing — the moved copy above still runs). So every behavioural hunk is pinned: the `main()` mapping, each `elif`, and the placement of the terminal branch above the C2 counter each fail their own row when reverted. That property is what the count stands for, and it is the part that does not rot.

Suite **at `7c6d187`: 5064 collected on the branch against 5048 on `origin/main`; a node-id diff is 17 added, 1 removed** — the one removal is a test this loop RENAMED (`test_the_cli_answers_with_a_dedicated_exit_code`, whose comment claimed it drove `main` when it did not), so "0 removed" was refuted by a commit on this same branch. The `origin/main` control was taken in a `git worktree`, where 3 of those 5048 are location-dependent and NOT comparable (2 `ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests` rows resolve `../..` against the checkout, and one skips itself with "needs the checkout under $HOME"); all three pass or skip identically in the main checkout, so the collected counts are the honest comparison. (`7c6d187` is the last commit before this entry; the entry's own commit touches only `TODO.md`, which is outside both measurements.) `ruff check --statistics` per touched file is byte-identical to `origin/main` on all four: **1** in `workflow_conductor.py` (F841), **0** in `validate_pipeline_semantics.py`, **6** (4 F821 + 2 F401) and **1** (E741) in their tests — every one pre-existing (ruff 0.15.20, no in-tree ruff config). An earlier version of this line said 3/1/7/2, because the counter used to produce it was `ruff check <f> | grep -c "^[A-Z][0-9]*"` and `[0-9]*` matches zero digits, so `No fixes available` and its neighbours were counted as findings. The identical-to-`origin/main` half was never affected — the same wrong counter ran on both sides — but the absolute numbers were, which is the reason to read a count out of `--statistics` rather than out of a counter written for the occasion. The token baseline was regenerated twice, for **5** occurrences total: `tools/workflow_conductor.py` 75 -> 79 and `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py` 372 -> 373. Every one is the existing `FORTRAN_STRUCTURE_UNAVAILABLE_EXIT_CODE` identifier — an import and a comparison at each of the two new conductor read sites, and one interpolation in the `--help` epilog — i.e. a symbol the validator already exports, quoted in a neutral role rather than new technology knowledge. A reviewer verified the regeneration launders nothing: regenerating from a clean checkout reproduces the committed file byte-for-byte, and the 5 occurrences arrive on 5 added token-bearing lines with none removed (the baseline FILE's own numstat is 2/2 — two counts changed in place).
    - **The counter-argument, recorded because it is a judgement call and not a free pass**: `AGENTS.md` says flatly that adding to the measured neutral-core debt is forbidden, and 3 of the 5 were avoidable by spelling — a local alias bound once per site instead of importing the long name twice, and an epilog built from the value rather than the constant. Rejected deliberately: renaming an identifier to get under a substring counter satisfies the instrument and not the rule, and it is the same habit this repository names as the ratchet's own failure mode (teaching people to regenerate without reading the rule). The debt is real and is recorded here rather than hidden.
    - A **third** trip was NOT regenerated, because it was a genuine addition: a first draft of the `docs/RUNBOOK.md` recovery entry spelled the two parser distribution names, which `run_workflow.py`'s `REQUIRED_PYTHON_MODULES` already owns and prints. The entry points at that refusal instead. Three trips on one branch, all from ordinary prose, is the practical lesson: **the ratchet reads whole files, so any commit touching a scanned file has to run it** — one of the three was caught two commits late because the commit's verification ran only the test file for the module it changed.

- **What the launch-time host probe does NOT cover** (2026-08-27, low — recorded with the issue #109 fix rather than left implicit). `tools/host_prerequisites.py` refuses a host missing the `static lint` tool, the build system, or the compiler the run's axis selection implies, before the first leaf. Three limits are deliberate and are stated in the module's own docstring; they are here so the next person adds to the probe rather than to whichever check they happened to be reading — the failure mode that produced the issue.
  - **It resolves the DEFAULT selection, not a node's IR.** At a cold start the first phase is `Compile`, which is the phase that authors `spec.ir.yaml`, so there is nothing to read. It is not a guess: `_validate_toolchain_backend_supported` fails any other `(build_system, language)` pair at `Compile.static`, so the default pair is the only one a run can reach. The consequence is narrow and real — an IR that pins `impl_defaults.toolchain.compiler` has its BUILD compiler unprobed (its mandatory syntax stage is still covered, since that stage is `DEFAULT_COMPILER` whatever the IR says, and a skipped mandatory stage is a `Generate.gate` fail_closed rather than a silent pass).
  - **A `--with-deps` closure is not walked.** The target node's selection stands for the closure, which holds only while the toolchain gate above pins every node to the same pair. Walking it would need the closure resolved before `_run_with_dependency_closure`, which is where it is resolved today.
  - **It closes the TIMING, not the DECLARATION.** A dependency MANIFEST is still owned by the MCP-tests-and-CI item and is still the right home for the declaration — the same split `REQUIRED_PYTHON_MODULES` was landed under.
  - The mid-run gates stay as the backstop. This is an earlier detector, not a replacement — the condition `atmofab-enforcement-change` imposes on this shape, and the same one the stale-certified-IR entry below imposes on its own fix. (Cited by NAME, not by line: inserting this entry moved that one, and a line reference written today is wrong at the next insertion.)

- **`run_syntax_check`'s missing-compiler `skipped` is closed under the workflow and open outside it** (2026-08-27, low — recorded because it was raised as a fail-open while fixing issue #109 and did NOT hold). `tool_run_syntax_check` returns `{"ok": true, "skipped": true}` when `shutil.which(adapter["exe"])` is None (`mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`), which reads as "an absent compiler passes the gate". It does not: `_gate_syntax_check` raises `generate.gate syntax check: mandatory gfortran stage unavailable` for a skipped MANDATORY stage (`tools/workflow_conductor.py`), which surfaces as a `deterministic_generate_error` transport fail_closed. An OPTIONAL stage — a future target compiler listed in `ATMOFAB_SYNTAX_COMPILERS` before its adapter ships — is skipped on purpose, which is what the tool-side return exists for.
  - Recorded rather than dropped, because the misreading is easy to repeat: the permissive return is in the TOOL and the closure is in its only workflow caller, one file away. A reviewer who reads the server alone reaches the wrong conclusion, as this one did.
  - The consequence issue #109 DID fix is the timing, and it is the same for the compiler as for the linter: an absent `gfortran` failed at `Generate.gate`, after `Compile` and `Generate.generate` had been billed. The launch probe moves it before the first leaf.

- **A stale certified IR is detectable at launch but surfaces mid-run, burning one billed `Generate.generate`** (2026-08-21, medium — the asymmetry measured, the cost not). `validate_pipeline_semantics` answers rc 3 and rc 4 for the two conditions no leaf can repair, and TODO:269 made both route terminally. rc 3 additionally has a LAUNCH-time check — `run_workflow.py`'s `REQUIRED_PYTHON_MODULES` refuses to start without the packages, and its comment states the reason ("agents never hit a partial-failure state"). rc 4 has no counterpart.
  - The cost: the condition is a certified dependency IR lacking the `public_api.module_parameters` the current §5.1 pins, and it is first seen at `Generate.gate` — after `Generate.generate` has already run, since `SUBSTEPS["generate"] == ("generate", "gate", "verify")`. So the run shape that produces it (a `--resume` across the harness contract change, where `Compile.static` is checkpoint-skipped) spends one billed leaf per occurrence before the terminal verdict.
  - Both inputs are readable at launch: the closure IRs and the `controlled_spec` §5.1 the freshness check already resolves. This is the "right failure at the wrong moment" shape `atmofab-enforcement-change` §3 describes, and the precedent for the fix is the rc-3 check sitting a few lines above in the same file.
  - **Keep the gate's fail-closed as the backstop** if this is done — the launch check is an earlier detector, not a replacement, and a `--with-deps` run can re-certify between launch and the gate.
  - Not done with TODO:269: that change moved a classification channel and added no new detector, and a launch-time check over the dependency closure is a different mechanism in a different file. Surfaced by a review of that branch.

- **`classify_post_judge_violations` still decides a DISPOSITION by scanning violation text** (2026-08-21, low — **reachability NOT verified**, surfaced by a review of the TODO:269 branch and recorded rather than pulled into it). `classify_post_judge_violations` (`tools/workflow_conductor.py`, just below the two basename frozensets) classifies each `--stage pre_judge` violation bullet by taking its leading token, reducing it to a basename, and testing that against `_POST_JUDGE_RECOVERABLE_BASENAMES` / `_POST_JUDGE_UNRECOVERABLE_BASENAMES`, plus THREE substring tests — the two `_POST_JUDGE_UNRECOVERABLE_MARKERS` and `"orchestrations/" in token`. (Cited by line number in the first draft, which named a member line of the frozenset rather than the function, and undercounted the substring tests by one.) That is the same read-side shape TODO:269 closed one layer up, and it is the one place a marker/basename scan still decides anything.
  - Direction is fail-CLOSED only: a bullet whose leading token has one of the seven unrecoverable basenames upgrades a warm judge resume to `fail_closed`. The fail-OPEN direction — forging `recoverable` — needs the basename `semantic_review.json`, which is the judge's own single deliverable, so it buys nothing.
  - **What is NOT established, and must be before anything is written or deleted**: whether any `--stage pre_judge` violation interpolates a path a leaf chose. If none does, this is unreachable by construction and the right answer is a comment saying so, not a channel change. Do not classify it either way from this entry — run the attack.
  - TODO:269's exit-code channel does not extend here: the exit code says which of {front end absent, stale IR, everything else} happened, while this classifier splits the "everything else" bucket by artifact ownership. A structured channel for it would have to be a machine-readable violation record, which is a larger change than the finding justifies at this severity.

- **Collapse the two near-identical `Generate.static` gate call-site blocks** (2026-08-12, medium — structural). `_validate_generate_outputs` and `_validate_generate_outputs_for_generation` (`tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py`) carry near-identical bodies: the same `index(case_id)` scan, the same literal-metric floor, and the same three `problem` model gate invocations, differing only in how they reach `src_dir`. Every rule change in this area has to be made twice, and "fixed one copy, missed the twin" is a repeat failure mode in this repository — the 2026-08-12 continuation work had to assert its one new rule at both entry points to be sure.
  - Fix direction: extract the shared body to one helper taking `(repo_root, execution, src_dir, violations)`; leave the two callers as the two ways of resolving `src_dir`.
  - Completion criterion: one definition of the scan-and-gate sequence remains; the existing tests of both entry points stay green without being duplicated further.
  - Deliberately NOT done with the gate fixes that exposed it: a structural refactor riding on an enforcement change widens the review surface, which is the trade this repository has repeatedly decided against.
  - **DONE (2026-08-13).** Suite **4616 passed** on both `origin/main` (`deef342`) and the branch — this change adds and removes no tests, so an unmoved count is the point. Note the standing "1 skipped" that earlier DONE notes in this file report is **gone**: the L242 work (PR #58) took the calibration test's input out of the operator's workspace, so the baseline is a clean 4616 with no skip. `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 errors on both revisions, identical histogram (35 E702, 19 F401, 12 F841, 5 F541, 5 E741, 4 F821, 3 E401); the two touched files carry 1 E741 between them, unchanged.
    - **The fix direction above was written on a wrong premise, and the correction is the reusable part.** It says "extract the shared body to one helper … leave the two callers as the two ways of resolving `src_dir`", which assumes each of the two functions carries its own resolution prologue. Only `_for_generation` does; the other is handed an already-resolved `src_dir` by its caller (`_execution_in_scope_src_dir`, via `_validate_impl`). And `_validate_generate_outputs`'s body **was** the shared block exactly — 4691-4762, ending with nothing of its own — so it already **was** the helper the direction asked for, at that exact signature. Extracting a second one would have left it a one-line passthrough. Landed instead as: `_validate_generate_outputs` keeps its signature and its body untouched and widens its return to `tuple[list[Path], list[str]] | None`; `_for_generation` keeps its `gen_dir`/`src_dir` prologue and calls it. The 72 duplicated lines appear in the diff only as deletions.
    - **Why the return had to widen rather than stay `-> None`.** `_for_generation`'s tail (runner sources, checks module, dep-op-on-model-files, infra signatures, component surface, OpenMP floor) consumes `model_files` and `dep_spec_ids`, both computed inside the shared block, and the block's one early return (absent/mis-named model source) must skip that tail. So the shared part has to hand both values back and signal that early return — hence `None`, which reproduces the old `if not model_files: return` exactly, with the violation still appended inside the callee.
    - No new tests. `WrappedLiteralMetricAssignmentTest` already drives both entry points behaviorally and becomes the wiring pin for the new structure unmodified; only its docstring changed, which had stated the duplication as an explicit contract and would otherwise now be false. `test_reaches_the_post_generate_sibling_list` still pins the tail, which this change keeps verbatim.
    - **Observed and deliberately left**: `_validate_post_build_stage_impl` (`tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py`) carries a third copy of the Makefile trio (`_validate_fortran_makefile_src_dir` + `_validate_makefile_test_no_relink` + `_validate_makefile_test_invokes_cases`) on its own `src_dir`. That is the same three checks in a different stage rather than a copy of the scan-and-gate sequence this item names, and folding it in would change what `post_build` validates — out of scope here.

- **An unrecognized `agent_role` is INFERRED by one reader and SKIPPED by four others, leaving the sandbox bound, the manifest empty, and the terminal write audit silently disabled** (2026-08-13, **high** — reproduced on `origin/main` and unchanged by the branch that found it). Five readers of one field, three different fallbacks:
  1. `build_capability_document` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py:4721`) INFERS: lines 4730-4736 turn `agent_role="bogus"` into `substep` when `substep` is present, minting a normal capability whose `write_roots` are the IR run dir WITH its trailing slash.
  2. `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch` (`:6531`) SKIPS the phase-contract check for an unrecognized role and returns those write_roots through `_normalize_rel_posix`, which STRIPS the slash. The write hook grants directory authority only to entries ending in `/` (`tools/hooks/common.py:4188-4201`), so the manifest's one entry matches nothing: measured on the captured `compile_generate.request.json`, `Write` to `spec.ir.yaml`, `ir_meta.json` and an arbitrary `evil.md` are ALL blocked.
  3. `_validate_child_write_contract_preflight` (`:7545`) SKIPS.
  4. `build_bwrap_profile` (`:7979-8000`) reads the CAPABILITY document, where the slash survives — so the sandbox rw-binds the whole IR run dir exactly as for a legitimate substep. The filesystem is not locked down; only the file-tool manifest is.
  5. `_validate_actual_write_paths` (`:9585-9591`) and its terminalization caller (`:14585-14591`) both RETURN EARLY for a role outside `{orchestration, step, substep}`, so the FS-diff attribution `docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md:22` names as authoritative never runs. Measured with a terminal status: `role="substep"` reaches the check (`ValueError: run write baseline missing`), `role="bogus"` returns without validating.
  - **Net effect, stated as measured**: the leaf cannot author its own deliverables (2), the sandbox still grants it the directory (4), and the audit that would flag an unmanifested write there is off (5). Rated high because a backstop this repository designates authoritative is silently disabled — that is a removed defence, not a hypothesis. **NOT executed, and therefore not claimed**: whether a write can actually land in that bound directory, which needs a write path the file-tool hook does not intercept (the Bash-detector gaps in the medium-severity audit item below are the candidate). Anyone picking this up should run that step before rating the exploitability.
  - **How this entry's severity was wrong three times, which is the transferable part.** Draft 1: medium, from an unexecuted premise citing a function that does not exist. Draft 2: high, from a reproduction that stopped at the manifest PRODUCER. Draft 3: medium, from running the manifest CONSUMER (the hook) and concluding lockout — while never opening the sandbox binder or the FS-diff. Each draft executed one more layer and asserted the rest. The rule that would have prevented all three: **enumerate every reader of the field first, then execute each one**; a severity claim is finished only when no reader is left unopened.
  - **The second, milder gap in the same function**: `_matches_phase_contract` ends every phase branch in `path in <set>`, so it is a membership allowlist, not the required set `compile_required`'s name implies — a `compile.generate` request declaring ONLY `ir_meta.json` is accepted although the conductor (`workflow_conductor.build_launch_request`) always declares both. Under-declaring costs the declarer authority rather than gaining any. `agent_role="step"` is likewise accepted on a compile payload although `child_agent_role()` assigns `substep` to every compile launch and `step` only to `build`.
  - Fix direction: normalize `agent_role` ONCE, where the payload is validated, using the same inference `build_capability_document` performs, so all three readers see one value; then key the expected set on `(agent_role, step, substep)` and require set EQUALITY per substep (`compile.generate` = both files, `compile.verify` = `ir_meta.json`, `compile.static` = `compile_static_meta.json`). Do NOT leave the skip-on-unknown-role branch reachable: an unrecognized role must fail closed, not fall through to `write_roots` — but budget for the migration, since several `record_launch` tests omit `agent_role` entirely and currently depend on the inference (making the unknown role fail closed turns 8 of them red). Deriving each substep's set from ONE named constant is what finally makes it testable: until the set has a single definition, a test outside the function can only SAMPLE names it must reject, so adding one more name to `compile_required` is invisible to the suite (demonstrated escapes, all green against both pins: one more literal name `<ir_ref>/summary.md` in the set; a subtree prefix `<ir_ref>/scratch/`; an extension family `path.endswith('.log')` at the IR root. The probes those tests carry are shape-samples — a name, a subtree, a directory entry — and three review rounds each broke the previous sample's shape, which is the evidence that sampling is the wrong instrument here.)
  - Completion criterion: a payload whose `agent_role` the conductor never assigns to that phase is rejected at record-launch (not silently normalized into one path and skipped in another), and a request that omits a declared deliverable is rejected — with a test per compile substep, driven by the captured production payloads under `tools/tests/data/conductor_launch_requests/`, and one that drives `build_capability_document` -> `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch` in sequence the way `record_launch` does, since testing either alone is what hid this.
  - **DONE (both chokepoints closed, 2026-08-13).** Commits `953bc05` (launch) + `f9c79a7` (terminal). The fix direction above was written for record-launch alone; **that is not sufficient, and the reason is the reusable part**: readers 5 and its caller read the record-agent-run / finalize-child TERMINAL payload, a different input from the launch request, so normalizing the launch payload cannot reach them. Two chokepoints were needed. Enumerating the readers first (the rule this entry itself proposed) also turned up a **sixth**: `record_launch:17451-17458` has its own fallback chain — a present-but-bogus role is taken verbatim, an absent one is derived from `step`, and an unknown `step` yields the literal `"unknown"`, written to `session_run_index`.
    - **A claim in this branch's commit messages is WRONG, and a blank-slate reviewer caught it.** `953bc05` says of the conductor's `child_agent_role` and the runtime's `STEP_REQUIRED_CHILD_AGENT`: "Two definitions of one fact were survivable while nothing compared them." **Something did compare them.** `workflow_launch_check` took the conductor's answer as `require_child_agent` and reported `required_child_agent_kind_mismatch` when it disagreed with the table; the conductor fed it `child_agent_role(phase)`. Unifying the two REMOVED that live comparison rather than adding one. The unification still stands — drift made IMPOSSIBLE beats drift REPORTED — but two consequences have to be stated rather than glossed: (1) the launch-side role check is a **tautology for every conductor-produced request** (`x != x`), so its whole value is against a non-conductor caller of the `record-launch` CLI, which is exactly the threat this entry described; and (2) `required_child_agent_kind_mismatch` now has no reachable producer on the conductor path. It is NOT deleted — "unreachable" is a classification, and this repository has shipped fail-open by acting on one — but it is dead weight the next person should decide about deliberately.
    - **What was built.** The required predicate ALREADY EXISTED: `validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation` and `_validate_run_gate_permissions` both demand `capability.agent_role == _required_child_agent_kind(step)`. It was extracted as `_require_child_agent_role_for_step` and applied to the launch request that MINTS the capability, so there is no new vocabulary and no new set literal. On the terminal side, `AGENT_RUN_ROLES` (the complete `agent_runs.jsonl` vocabulary) and `WRITE_AUDITED_AGENT_ROLES` (the subset owning a write window) are named once and read by the four sites whose question they actually answer (the two terminal-audit early returns, `record_agent_run`, and its CLI-dispatch twin). Three verbatim copies of the same three members REMAIN on purpose — two in `build_capability_document` ("which roles may a capability name") and one in the `repair_legacy_agent_runs` backfill ("which rows represent an agent that ran"); they coincide with the write-audited set today but are not defined by it, and a comment at the constant says so. An earlier draft of this annotation claimed every copy had been folded in. `workflow_conductor.child_agent_role` now reads `STEP_REQUIRED_CHILD_AGENT` instead of restating `"step" if step == "build" else "substep"` — two definitions of one fact stop being survivable once record-launch rejects the disagreement.
    - **The unexecuted layer this entry flagged, now executed — and the severity was wrong a FOURTH time, in the safe direction.** The entry said a write landing in the rw-bound directory needed "a write path the file-tool hook does not intercept". Confirmed that such a path exists: `_detect_bash_write_targets` (`tools/hooks/cli.py`) returns NO target for `cp` / `mv` / `dd of=` / `install` / `truncate` / `touch` / `ln` (7/7 executed), and `cli.py:1632` returns `common_decision` when there are no targets, so the write guard never runs. **(That measurement was of `origin/main` at the time; item 378(d) has since closed it — all seven now produce a target. The reasoning below about what the audit would have caught is unaffected, because it never rested on the extractor.)** **But the audit would not have caught that write anyway**: a stray file INSIDE `write_roots` is authorized for every role by FS-diff containment (`docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md:22`) — executed for `substep` / `step` / `bogus`, all three clean. The role only changes the verdict for writes OUTSIDE `write_roots` (`substep`/`step` FLAGGED, `bogus` clean), and enumerating every location `build_bwrap_profile` rw-binds outside `write_roots` — `workspace/tmp/<arid>`, `gates/<arid>/`, `hooks/`, `audit/`, `access_logs/<arid>.jsonl`, the `mcp_owned_audit_logs` file pins — every one is exempt or authorized for a legitimate role too. **So under an enforced bwrap sandbox no write was found that the bogus role makes newly undetectable.** NOT executed: a host where `sandbox_enforced` is false, and whether a leaf can reach `record-launch` at all. The defence was still silently off, and "the sandbox covers it" is the second-layer argument this repository does not accept, so the fix stands on its own.
    - **Two probes decided what NOT to build.** (a) Swapping a step child's terminal role to `orchestration` to get a different FS-diff baseline does not weaken the audit — it flags MORE, because the orchestration branch requires every changed path to be declared in `output_refs`; measured all three ways. (b) `skipped_by_checkpoint` is in the vocabulary but not write-audited, which would be an evasion if it could also be terminal — it cannot, since it requires `status=skipped` and `skipped` is not in `TERMINAL_STATUSES`. No launch-role cross-check was added, and the reason is an execution record rather than a judgement.
    - **Completion criterion, both halves.** A payload whose `agent_role` the conductor never assigns to that phase is rejected AT record-launch, before the session-run-index row is appended (so it adds no orphan `running` row to the audit's item (f)); and the terminal payload is rejected rather than silently skipping the write audit.
