Seven pins, all generated by ITERATING `STEP_REQUIRED_CHILD_AGENT` / `AGENT_RUN_ROLES` rather than sampling names — the property the phase-contract tests could not express from outside the set — plus one driving the seven captured production payloads through the real `_validate_launch_request_payload`, and one driving `build_capability_document` -> `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch` in record-launch's own order (testing either alone is what hid this).
    - **The migration figure in this entry was wrong, and so was my correction of it — measured a third time, it is 32 red tests over 28 payloads.** The entry recorded "8 tests turn red", which counted payloads reaching `build_capability_document` with no role — a different predicate. I then published **37**, which was the failure count from an EARLIER placement of the check (before the deterministic-flag and `agent_model` checks); I moved the check to preserve those tests' error ordering and never re-measured. Re-measured on the final placement, by dropping only the two changed source files onto `main`'s tree: **32 tests turn red — 22 on a missing `agent_role`, 10 on a role the step does not take.** The fixture side is **28 launch payloads**: 28 gained `agent_role`, 4 had it corrected (`step` -> `substep` on `generate` and on `validate`), 20 gained `allowed_output_paths` — that third class **never appears as a per-test count**, because a missing output only becomes an error once the role is supplied, which is itself the shape of the gap: with an unknown role `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch` returned before its own "requires explicit allowed_output_paths" check. The transferable part is not the number: **a figure measured against a draft you then changed is a stale figure, and re-measuring is not optional just because the first measurement was real.**
    - **Mutation check, across three passes as the branch grew.** The terminal commit alone: 5 hunks, 3 killed; one survivor was real (the library-side rejection in `record_agent_run` was unpinned while only its CLI-dispatch twin was covered — "helper pinned, handler not") and got a test. Branch-wide: 10 hunks, 6 killed; the 4 survivors were all behaviour-preserving substitutions, and the two capability-side ones exposed that `validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation` / `_validate_run_gate_permissions` had **no test at all** for a mismatched capability role — pinned in round 1. Round-1 hunks: 2 of 2 killed. Two MECHANISM-level deletions (the predicate never rejects; the vocabulary check removed from BOTH terminal readers) are each caught, by 4 and 2 tests — hunk reverting cannot see a mechanism that is live but unobserved, which is why they were run separately. An independent reviewer's 10-mutation sweep killed 9; the survivor was `child_agent_role` restating the literal, now pinned on the only behaviour that distinguishes it (an unsupported step raises instead of silently answering `substep`).
    - Suite **4595 passed / 1 skipped** at the branch tip; `main` at `08dff50` measured **4580 / 1** on this checkout, so **+15**. The figure moved with every review round (4587 -> 4589 -> 4591 -> 4592 -> 4595) and was stale in this file twice; treat any number written here as of its round. `ruff` per touched file identical to `main` (3 / 1 / 45 / 6). Re-running from a copied tree outside `$HOME` shifts the absolute by 5 checkout-depth `test_hooks_*` failures present on `main` too — compare the DELTA.
    - **Round 4 is where the class finally dropped: zero functional defects from the blank-slate reviewer (10/10 mutations killed, 3 of them mechanism-level), and the targeted reviewer's two findings were both bounded.** (a) Round 3 kept the validator write-back as an explicit "backstop for a caller that skips prepare" and DELETED the only test that drove the validator without prepare — neutering the assignment left the whole suite green. Keeping a mechanism while deleting its witness is this branch's own recurring class one layer up; the witness is restored. (b) The round-3 defect survived verbatim on the OTHER branch of `prepare_launch_request_payload`: when the caller supplies its own prompt nothing is re-rendered, so a prompt built from `"SUBSTEP"` shipped while the persisted request recorded `"substep"` — the same record-vs-prompt divergence, one branch over. Closed by REFUSING that pair (caller-supplied prompt + non-canonical spelling), which is narrow by construction and unreachable from the conductor, rather than recorded as a residual.
    - **The test-class census is no longer prose.** It was written three times and wrong all three (all-seven-iterate when five did; a test name a rename had already deleted; "eight tests" when there were nine). Restating a census is the wrong shape — it goes stale silently on any add or rename. It now lives in `_DEFINITION_DRIVEN` / `_SAMPLE_DRIVEN` data asserted against the class's actual methods by `test_this_class_census_is_accurate`, verified by renaming a test and watching it fail. The distinction it tracks is the one this whole entry keeps rediscovering: a test that ITERATES a definition extends automatically, a test that SAMPLES shapes can show a rejection but never set identity.
    - **Codex could not be used as a convergence signal on this branch.** Two adversarial-review runs were launched; both stalled (46 min, then 28 min in `verifying`) and neither returned a report. The second produced only an early partial verdict — `needs-attention`, naming the same validator-mutation drift path the subagents found — before stalling. A stall is not a clean result, so the skill's upper stopping condition (security-clean twice AND Codex clean) is NOT met here, as it has not been in any recorded loop.
    - **Round 3 found the round-2 fix was placed AFTER the state it guarded, and it is the worst self-inflicted defect of the branch.** Round 2 canonicalized `agent_role` inside `_validate_launch_request_payload` to stop `"SUBSTEP"` producing an empty Task Card. But `record_launch` calls `prepare_launch_request_payload` FIRST, and that is what renders `launch_prompt_full` (embedding `_build_task_card`, which reads the role with `.strip()` and no `.lower()`); the canonicalization ran 15 lines later. So the symptom was not fixed — and the record got WORSE: the persisted `launches/<arid>.request.json` said `substep` while the prompt beside it had been rendered from `SUBSTEP` with no card, meaning an auditor recomputing the card from the request obtains one that was never sent. Two independent reviewers found it; the test that "proved" the round-2 fix was hollow because it called `_build_task_card` itself after mutating the payload instead of reading the prompt `record_launch` would persist. Fixed by normalizing at the TOP of `prepare_launch_request_payload`, before the render (normalization only — an unknown role is left untouched for the validator to reject, so it can never launder a bad value); the test now drives prepare -> validate -> extract and asserts on the RENDERED TEXT, and was verified to fail against the round-2 code.
    - **Three more of my own claims were false and are corrected in place.** (a) `_write_allowed_output_manifest` was named in a comment, a test docstring and a commit message as a reader harmed by an unlowered role; it is not — record-launch calls it with no `agent_role`, and its other call sites pass the literal `"orchestration"`. (b) `_normalized_agent_role`'s docstring said "every reader of the field normalizes this way"; four do not, which is precisely why canonicalizing at the chokepoints rather than trusting readers is the right shape. (c) A comment said "six downstream readers" and then listed five — the sixth is `_register_codex_thread`, closed in this same branch. Also pinned this round: the write-audit USE SITE reads the narrower constant (a reviewer's mutation swapping it for `AGENT_RUN_ROLES` had survived the whole suite — pinning the definitional difference between the two sets was not the same as pinning which one the early return consults).
    - **NOT closed by this work**: the set-equality gap in `_matches_phase_contract` (the "second, milder gap" bullet above), deliberately left out so a structural change does not ride on an enforcement change. It is now its own item below.

- **`_matches_phase_contract` is a membership allowlist where its own name says required set** (2026-08-13, medium — split out of the `agent_role` item above, which closed the role dimension and deliberately left this one). Every phase branch of `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch._matches_phase_contract` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py`) ends in `path in <set>`, so a `compile.generate` request declaring ONLY `ir_meta.json` is accepted even though `compile_required` names both files and the conductor (`workflow_conductor.build_launch_request`) always declares both. Under-declaring costs the declarer write authority rather than gaining any, so this is a liveness gap, not a bypass — which is why it was not bundled with the enforcement fix.
  - Fix direction: key the expected set on `(agent_role, step, substep)` and require set EQUALITY per substep (`compile.generate` = both files, `compile.verify` = `ir_meta.json`, `compile.static` = `compile_static_meta.json`). **Equality is only meaningful for the compile substeps and the validate gate substeps**; `generate` / `build` / `validate.execute` name files whose ids and basenames vary per run, so those branches stay membership tests and the change must say so rather than pretend uniformity.
  - The reason this needs ONE named constant per substep rather than a test: until the set has a single definition, a test outside the function can only SAMPLE names it must reject, so adding one more name to `compile_required` stays invisible to the suite. Three demonstrated escapes, all green against both existing pins: one more literal name (`<ir_ref>/summary.md`), a subtree prefix (`<ir_ref>/scratch/`), and an extension family (`path.endswith('.log')` at the IR root). Three review rounds each broke the previous sample's shape — that is the evidence sampling is the wrong instrument here. `AgentRoleFailClosedTests` shows the shape that works: assertions generated by iterating the definition.
  - Completion criterion: a request that omits a declared deliverable is rejected for each compile substep, driven by the captured production payloads under `tools/tests/data/conductor_launch_requests/`, with the expected set read from one named constant rather than restated in the test. Note `compile.static` has NO captured payload (nor do `generate.gate` / `validate.pre_judge` / `validate.post_judge`), so it must either be captured first or the test must say it is synthesized.


- **Reconcile the Compile.generate SKILL's `algorithm.summary.md` step with the write contract** (2026-08-10 audit, high). `skills/workflow-compile-generate/SKILL.md` instructs the leaf to save `algorithm.summary.md` under the IR run directory, but the conductor's `allowed_output_paths` for `compile.generate` (`tools/workflow_conductor.py`) lists only `spec.ir.yaml` and `ir_meta.json` — a compliant leaf triggers a hook block by following the SKILL.
  - Fix direction: either add `algorithm.summary.md` to the allowed outputs (and the FS-diff expectations) or delete the step from the SKILL; keep SKILL, conductor, and `docs/workflow/phases/phase_01_compile.md` in agreement.
  - Completion criterion: the SKILL instruction and `allowed_output_paths` agree, covered by a test or doc-sync check.
  - **DONE (2026-08-13).** Deleted the artifact rather than widening the allowlist: the SKILL bullet now states the deliverables are exactly `spec.ir.yaml` + `ir_meta.json`, and the stale third reader — `compile_required` in `orchestration_runtime._matches_phase_contract`, which the audit item did not name — dropped the entry. Suite **4580 passed / 1 skipped** at the branch tip (the skip is the retention item below, unchanged; the figure moved with the review rounds, so it is the tip's, not the first commit's); `ruff tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 errors, identical to `origin/main` at `1a511a1`.
    - **Completion criterion, both halves.** Runtime side: `test_phase_contract_compile_generate_admits_only_conductor_declaration` drives the captured production request (`tools/tests/data/conductor_launch_requests/compile_generate.request.json`) through the real `_allowed_output_paths_for_launch`, so the conductor that declares and the runtime that accepts are asserted against each other. SKILL side: the deliverables sentence is anchored in `test_compile_generate_skill_quotes_live_gate_messages`, with a negative pin that the SKILL never names the retired artifact again. Both mutations executed: restoring the entry, and deleting the SKILL bullet, each fail.
    - **The stale entry was vestigial from the retired `plan` phase.** Every `algorithm.summary.md` in the corpus (7 files, enumerated with `os.walk` because interactive `grep` is ripgrep and skips gitignored trees) sits under `workspace_*/plans/<node>/<id>/`; none under any `ir/`. No compile-phase request or manifest in the corpus ever declared it, which is why the fail-open was latent rather than live.
    - **Correction to this branch's commit messages, which cannot be rewritten once pushed.** `0d444c2` states "all 30 commits touching TODO.md — 26 add a DONE sub-bullet". Those figures came from a review report and I repeated them as measurement without running them. Measured: **71** commits touch this file (36 on `--first-parent`), **42** add a `**DONE` line, **6** remove a top-level item line. The conclusion the numbers were offered for — that the practice is to annotate rather than delete — holds under the real figures.
    - **Disclosure — two claims of my own I had to correct.** (1) The first commit's causal sentence said record-launch accepted the path and the file-tool hook then blocked it. Measured wrong: `allowed_file_tool_paths` is auto-derived from the declared outputs, so a request that DECLARED the file would have had the hook allow it too. The real pre-change failure is the other path — the conductor never declares it, so a leaf obeying the SKILL hit `output_manifest_write_guard`; the runtime entry was reachable only by a request nothing builds. (2) The first test was named for an exact-set pin but was a three-name denylist: widening `compile_required` with `io_contract.yaml` + `algorithm_summary.md` left it, and all 1878 tests of the three relevant suites, green. Both were found by review, not by me.

- **Define a workspace retention policy and restore the real-IR calibration test** (2026-08-10 audit, high). ~14 GB of `workspace_*` run artifacts (95% of the checkout) carry no retention rule, and the loss of a pinned run directory has already converted `test_real_full_fidelity_predicate_set_is_not_degenerate` (`tools/tests/test_validate_pipeline_semantics.py`) into a permanent silent skip — the suite's standing "1 skipped" — losing the real-shape calibration line the test's own docstring declares as its purpose.
  - Fix direction: state a retention rule in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (what may be deleted when; what must be captured into `tools/tests/data/` first); re-pin the calibration test to a committed capture of a real full-fidelity IR so a fresh clone runs it.
  - Completion criterion: the calibration test runs (not skipped) on a fresh clone, and the retention rule is documented.
  - **DONE (2026-08-13) — but only one half was implemented; the other is REJECTED by decision, not deferred.** The retention-rule half of this item is **not adopted**: `workspace/` and `workspace_*/` are the gate's execution workspace, and how they are used is the operator's decision, not something this repository legislates. No `docs/RUNBOOK.md` or `docs/WORKSPACE_LAYOUT.md` change was made, and **zero bytes were deleted** — `du -sb workspace workspace_*` measured `15,040,129,410` both before and after. The second clause of the completion criterion above is therefore void, not unmet.
    - **The item mis-located the defect.** The absence of a retention rule is not what broke the test; the test reading the operator's execution workspace is. The fix is the general one: what a test needs lives in the repository. The real IR was captured **byte-for-byte** (`cmp`-verified, sha256 `b6a1352d…f15e`, 23,835 bytes) from `workspace_20260719/ir/problem__shallow_water2d__0.4.0/shallow-water2d_20260718_003/` into `tools/tests/data/real_ir/shallow_water2d_20260718_003.spec.ir.yaml`, and `skipTest` is gone — a missing fixture is now repository corruption, so it fails. Not hand-trimmed — a statement about how the fixture was made, verifiable by `cmp` against the archive, and **not** something the test enforces: two reviewers independently substituted a hand-written document of a couple of hundred bytes with one conforming pass predicate, and the test passed. Almost none of the fixture's 23,835 bytes are load-bearing; the rest are there so the calibration is against a real document rather than a plausible one, which is a provenance argument, not an assertion. Same precedent as `tools/tests/data/conductor_launch_requests/`. `repo_root` stays the temp dir deliberately (comment says why): passing the real root would additionally drive the tests.md set-equality path and mix unrelated drift into this calibration, so only the input's provenance changed, not the semantics.
    - **Class guard: `tools/tests/test_skip_reasons_are_declared.py`.** Every declared skip in the tracked corpus must carry a **string-literal** reason appearing in one table, `_DECLARED_ENVIRONMENT_SKIPS` — twelve entries, each naming a property of the host (no gfortran, no bwrap, no symlinks, no `/proc`, running as root, checkout outside `$HOME`). Recognition is by identity, not spelling: the API set is resolved through each module's own imports, so an alias or the defining module's longer name is seen while an unrelated local `def skip` is not. A skip API named without a readable reason — a bare decorator, a bare `raise`, `importorskip` without `reason=`, a computed message, `__unittest_skip__` set by hand — is a violation, so it fails closed. The corpus comes from `git ls-files` rather than a glob; `test_no_declared_reason_is_unused` removes the table entry when its skip goes, so the permission list cannot only grow. The retired reason — `"real IR artifact not present in this checkout"` — is the probe driving the reject path.
      - **What it does NOT cover, stated because an earlier version of this entry and of the module docstring claimed "any route".** It checks declared skips. Review demonstrated four ways to stop a test that declare nothing — `__test__ = False`, defining the test under an `if`, `del test_f`, and an early `return` where the old code called `skipTest`. The last is this very defect with one word changed. Catching those needs a different instrument (observing what the runner executed), and it is not built here.
      - **This guard was NOT driven to convergence, and the review was stopped by decision.** Seven rounds; rounds 5, 6 and 7 each produced three to six findings, every one of them the same shape — one more Python binding form the static resolution did not model (`unittest.case`, `_pytest.outcomes`, star imports, assignment aliases, tuple targets, `pytestmark`, a `lambda` override, a nested class) — with no descent in severity across the last three. **None of these shapes exists in the corpus**: all 23 skip sites use two spellings, `unittest.skipUnless` and `self.skipTest`. Every finding after round 4 was a construct an adversarial reviewer wrote specifically to defeat the checker. The instrument is therefore regression prevention against ordinary spellings, not enforcement against someone routing around it; the fixed defect and every spelling in use are caught, and a determined author is not. **The right instrument for the strong claim is a runtime skip inventory** — run the suite and compare what the runner actually reported against `_DECLARED_ENVIRONMENT_SKIPS`, which needs no Python semantics and closes all ~15 evasions at once. Not built here: it cannot be a test inside the suite it must run, and this repository has no CI to host it (see the CI item below). That is the follow-up, and it is the reason this entry does not claim convergence.
      - **Stopping conditions not met, recorded rather than glossed.** The skill's upper condition (security-angle reviewers clean two rounds running, plus Codex clean) was never reached: Codex was launched three times and returned `needs-attention` every time, twice pointing inside the previous round's fix. The proxy pair also never agreed — an independent 170-to-193-mutant sweep recommended stopping in rounds 4 and 6, while the blank-slate reviewer returned live functional routes in both. The sweep can only mutate mechanisms that exist; it is blind to a missing one, which is what every round after 3 actually found.
    - **Disclosure — the guard I first wrote answered the wrong question, and review proved it in one round.** It AST-scanned path expressions and required any repo-root-anchored path to be git-tracked. Three reviewers (two subagents and Codex, independently) produced ~20 evasions: `.joinpath` (two modules), f-strings, `os.path.join`, string concatenation, `glob`, and simply putting `.resolve()` on its own line — that last demonstrated end to end, a module reading `workspace/` and skipping was added and the guard stayed **green**. Two further defects in it: the sweep itself was unpinned (replacing the module glob with a single file left every test passing), and one assertion comprehended over an AST with zero matching nodes, so it could not fail. Earlier in the same file, an initial module-wide name set produced **382 false positives**. The question "which repository path does this source construct" has no bounded answer at the AST level; "is this skip reason in the table" does, which is why the instrument was replaced rather than patched. Nothing of it is kept.
      - **The anchor-spelling census, measured (I published it twice from other people's summaries and got it wrong both times).** Under `tools/tests/`, `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent` — invisible to that guard — appears in **4** modules: `test_audit_orchestration.py`, `test_build_runtime_server.py`, `test_llm_config.py`, `test_run_workflow.py`. `test_validate_pipeline_semantics.py` does not use that spelling; it reaches the repository root by a third one the guard also could not see, `Path(vps.__file__).resolve().parent.parent`, at **7** sites. (It does have a `parents[2]` anchor — the one this branch added for the fixture — so read the sentence as being about the spelling, not about the file having no anchor.) So the claim that stood — an unreadable repo-root anchor in the very file this item fixes — is true, but not for the spelling I named.
    - **Measured at the branch tip, and only there.** Baseline taken in place (a `git worktree` under `/tmp` is NOT a valid baseline — two host-dependent tests change behaviour outside `$HOME`): `origin/main` collects 4596 and runs **4595 passed / 1 skipped**; the tip runs **0 skipped**, the difference being the un-skipped calibration test plus the new guard's tests. `ruff check tools/ mcp_servers/` = 83 errors, unchanged. Per-commit figures are deliberately not restated here: they moved every review round, I published them wrong three times, and the last attempt "corrected" a right number with a wrong one. The tip figure is the one worth having, and `python3 -m pytest tools/tests -q` prints it.
    - **The calibration test asserted one bit and could not tell an empty fixture from a good one.** Review overwrote the fixture with `{}`, and with unparseable YAML, and it passed both times: "no violation" is also what a document the gate cannot read produces, so the clean run proved nothing about this fixture. Mutation (B) is now inside the test — the same captured document is driven twice, as-is and with its pass predicates rewritten to `verdict.overall`, and the second drive must fire. Both corruptions now fail. The comment's census (6 pass blocks, threshold maps, metric addresses) is dropped rather than pinned: counting a fixture's contents in prose is a claim the assertion does not stand behind.
    - **Three wrong suite figures, and the third was a "correction" that denounced the right number.** Round 1 published `4601 / +5` (right for its commit), round 2 "corrected" it to `4603 / 7` calling the original unmeasured, round 3's entry then declared `4603 / 7` to match no commit — by which time it matched the tip exactly. Every one came from re-running the arithmetic against a figure measured at a different moment, while the tree kept moving under the review rounds. The durable fix is not a fourth correction: it is to stop publishing per-commit counts in prose, which is what the bullet above now does.
    - **Note for whoever prunes the archives.** After this change **no test reads any `workspace*` path**, so deleting an archive breaks nothing. What it costs is the ability to re-verify citations: run `git grep -n 'workspace_2026'` for the current list — every hit is a provenance comment or a synthetic string fed to a path classifier, never a read. One archive is worth naming: **`workspace_20260719`** holds `ir/problem__shallow_water2d__0.4.0/shallow-water2d_20260718_003/`, the sole provenance of the committed calibration fixture; deleting it costs the ability to `cmp` the fixture against its source, nothing more. Given as a command rather than a list, because the previous three versions of this note were each stale in a different way — a line number this branch's own edit had moved, a missing file, and archives attributed to the wrong file. Enumerating file:line in prose is the failure this repository keeps repeating; here it took three tries in one branch.

- **Rewrite `README.md` to describe the current system** (2026-08-10 audit, high). The README still declares the repository "specification-first", omits the canonical entrypoint `python3 tools/run_workflow.py <spec_ref> <until_phase>`, lists a phase sequence that contradicts `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, lists 4 of the 6 MCP tools, uses legacy artifact names (`case.resolved.yaml`, `impl.resolved.yaml`), and omits `tools/`/`skills/` from the layout.
  - Completion criterion: README states the entrypoint, the `Spec -> Compile -> Generate -> Build -> Validate` sequence with optional `Tune`/`Promote`, all six MCP tools, and the actual layout; no legacy artifact names remain.
  - **DONE (README rewritten against the canonical sources + a doc-sync test, 2026-08-13).** `README.md` was rewritten against the canonical sources and the code rather than edited in place: the phase table and primary artifacts from `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, the entrypoint and its options from `tools/run_workflow.py`'s own argparse (`--with-deps` / `--resume` / `--mode` / `--no-run-conductor`, the `./llm.yaml` default and its `llm_config_default_missing` stop), the six MCP tools and the capability rule from `mcp_servers/README.md`, the `workspace/` layout from `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1-1 plus `phase_01_compile.md` (`dependency_graph.json`, which `docs/WORKSPACE_LAYOUT.md`'s tree omits), the `spec_kind` set from `docs/SPEC.md`, and the in-tree `spec_id` list from `spec/registry/spec_catalog.yaml` (11 entries, matched against the 11 `controlled_spec.md` on disk). `case.resolved.yaml` / `impl.resolved.yaml` / "specification-first" / the `Spec -> Plan -> … -> Judge` sequence no longer appear.
    - The substep table is read from `workflow_conductor.SUBSTEPS`, not from prose: `compile` = `generate` / `static` / `verify`, `generate` = `generate` / `gate` / `verify`, `build` = one step with no substep, `validate` = `pre_judge` / `execute` / `judge` / `post_judge`. `Build` is stated as deterministic and conductor-run (`_build_inproc`); `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1-3's "the `Build` step … runs by launching a `step agent` independently" contradicts the code and belongs to the documentation-drift item below.
    - **Three of the README's tables are now pinned rather than proofread** (`tools/tests/test_readme_sync.py`): the substep table against `workflow_conductor.SUBSTEPS`, its `LLM`-leaf column against `llm_config.LLM_LEAF_SUBSTEPS`, the MCP tool list against `build_runtime_server.TOOLS`, and the `spec` table against `spec_catalog.yaml` AND the `controlled_spec.md` on disk. This is the doc-sync-test preference the drift item below states, applied to the copies this change created. Nine mutations were run against it — a dropped `spec_id`, a renamed substep, an extra `LLM` leaf, a dropped MCP tool, a `Build` substep, a `Build` leaf, and the deletion of each table or heading — and each was killed by the test that names the fact, none vacuously. The CLI option table is deliberately NOT pinned: `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md` makes `run_workflow.py --help` canonical for it, and the README now says so instead of standing as a second source.
    - **`docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1-3 was corrected in the same change**, because the README would otherwise ship contradicting a canonical doc: it said the `Build` step "runs by launching a `step agent` independently", which its own §1-2 and §2 already contradicted and which `SUBSTEPS["build"] == (None,)` / `_build_inproc` settle. The line now states the deterministic in-process execution and the empty `substep_agent_run_ids`.
    - **Residue, deliberately not touched**: `case.resolved.yaml` is still named by 12 `spec/**/controlled_spec.md` / `tests.md` files (every in-tree `spec`, `harness_fortran_cpu` excepted). Those are human-authored specification content whose edit is a `spec` change, not a README change; it is carried by the documentation-drift item below.

- **`_detect_bash_write_commands`' substitution recursion is exponential in nesting depth.** `tools/hooks/cli.py`: the top-level `_extract_command_substitution_bodies` already flattens every nested body, and the recursion then re-extracts from each one. Measured by a reviewer on issue #74's branch, `"echo " + "$(echo "*d + "hi" + ")"*d`: depth 10 = 0.022s, depth 14 = 0.365s, depth 16 = 1.458s, depth 18 = 6.242s — roughly 4x per level, on a 151-character command. This hook is synchronous and runs on every tool call, so a slow path is a stall. **Not reachable today**: no construct of that shape exists in this tree's leaf corpus (283 commands) or in an operator session corpus (51,425), and it is not a `leaf shortcut` — a leaf gains nothing by nesting substitutions. Directions: pass the already-flattened body list down instead of re-extracting, or bound the body count. Termination itself is fine (an unterminated `$((` or backtick scan returns at end of string; no stack overflow observed).
- **`_detect_sed_inplace_targets` mis-reads the two `sed -i` spellings a leaf actually writes.** `tools/hooks/cli.py::_looks_like_sed_script` recognises only a `/`-delimited script (plus `c\` / `i\` / `a\` and an `s=`/`y=` form), so (a) `sed -i 's|old|new|' <path>` — any non-`/` delimiter, `|` `#` `@` alike — reports the SCRIPT as the write target and never examines the file, which both refuses a legitimate in-place edit under `allowed_tmp_root` and leaves the real path unchecked; and (b) `sed -i -e '<script>' <path>` and `sed --in-place '<script>' <path>` report NOTHING, so `validate_write_access` never runs. (b) is the one with a `leaf shortcut` route: `sed -i -e 's/xfail/pass/' <managed pipeline artifact>` is a gate edited rather than satisfied, with the write guard recording nothing. **Measured on `5f254ef` as well as on issue #74's branch — identical on both, so this is pre-existing and was deliberately left out of that branch**, which touches the redirect / `tee` / argv grammars and not this one. Corpus figures from a reviewer's census over 51,425 unique Bash commands mined from an operator's session transcripts: 104 rows where a `sed` script is named as a write target, 10 rows carrying a `sed` with both `-i` and `-e`. Direction: the delimiter is whatever character follows the command letter, so read it from the token rather than enumerating `/`; and treat `-e` / `--expression` / `--in-place` as the option grammar they are instead of scanning for a bare `-i`.
- **The Bash write-target extractor reports a destination it cannot resolve, and that refuses legitimate work.** `tools/hooks/cli.py::_detect_bash_write_targets` returns a `$VAR` destination unexpanded (`cmd > $OUT` -> `['$OUT']`), a glob unexpanded (`cmd > workspace/ir/*.txt` -> `['workspace/ir/*.txt']`), and a destination relative to an earlier `cd` un-anchored (`cd workspace && cmd > b` -> `['b']`). Each then fails the manifest check and blocks a write the leaf was entitled to make, naming a path that is not the one being written. **All three are pre-existing** — every row above is measured identical on `5f254ef` and at HEAD, and they live on the REDIRECT branch, which is the only branch that names a path at all. (An earlier version of this entry illustrated them with `cp a $DEST` and `cd … && cp a b`; issue #74's branch later replaced the operand-destination extractor with a refusal, so those spellings now return nothing and the redirect ones are the whole of it.) The read side already has the half that matters for `cd` — `extract_bash_read_targets` anchors on it — and declares `$VAR` operands as residue it cannot see, so the two halves of the same module disagree today. Directions: anchor the write side on `cd` with the read side's own resolver; and for a destination carrying `$` or a glob metacharacter, decide between reporting nothing (fail-open, no false block) and refusing with a message that says the destination could not be resolved — do not leave it reporting the unexpanded token, which is the worst of the three because the block names a path nobody wrote.
- **Close the medium-severity enforcement findings from the 2026-08-10 audit** (grouped): (a) a missing/unparsable capability `expires_at` skips the expiry check in both `validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation` and `_validate_run_gate_permissions` — reject instead; (b) the `run_program` command constraint is a substring test (`"spec.ir.yaml" not in joined`) — anchor it to an actual argv element; (c) bwrap write-root binding resolves through directory and parent symlinks (only leaf file pins are lstat-checked) — verify the bound root and its parents; (d) **DONE** (issue #74's branch), but **not as written** — `_detect_bash_write_targets` (`tools/hooks/cli.py`) missed `cp`/`mv`/`dd of=`/`install`/`truncate`/`touch`/`ln` destinations, so those writes never reached `validate_write_access`. It was first closed by EXTRACTING the destination (last operand, `-t DIR`, `dd of=`, per-command option tables). Three review rounds each found the same defect one function further on — a value read off one copy of the command while a decision was made on another — and each fix regenerated the family: `cp src dst 2>/dev/null` refused naming the path `2`; `cp a b # copy the artifact` naming `artifact`; `cp a run-$(date +%s).json` naming `.json`; `cd x; cp a <managed path>` extracting nothing at all. Each of those is reproducible at the commit that carried it — `cbb8e15` for the fd prefix and the glued separator, `98956af` for the comment and the embedded substitution — so the measurements can be re-taken without reading the branch's history. **The question was changed to a weaker one that a lookup can answer**: `_detect_bash_write_commands` recognises one of those commands at a FRAGMENT HEAD — after `common._strip_bash_fragment_syntax` strips a `VAR=` assignment or a shell keyword/grouping — and the hook REFUSES the command. A fragment headed by a member of `_BASH_SCAN_WHOLE_FRAGMENT_HEADS` (wrappers, the shells, `command`/`exec`/`eval`, `case`) has every WORD checked instead of its argv0: hopping to "the program" would mean modelling which of the head's own options take a value, which is the getopt grammar this design exists to avoid, and the hopping spelling was defeated by `env FOO=1 cp`, `timeout 5 cp` and `sudo -u root cp`. Command-substitution and process-substitution bodies are recursed into (`forbid_bash_file_write_command`) with the remedy the contract already gives every leaf. It fails CLOSED and costs nothing legitimate: measured on `leaf_config/claude/settings.json`, `permissions.allow` has 17 entries and not one matches any of the seven, so the harness refuses them all already. **Residue, yielding nothing**: a writer invoked by a program outside the wrapper set (`find -exec cp`), named by a variable or substitution (`$CP a b`), wrapped in a shell function or alias, or reached through an interpreter (covered separately by `forbid_python_inline_write`); (e) `orchestration_id` bare-component validation exists only for the codex feature cache path — centralize it where `_extract_orchestration_id` normalizes the value; that function (`tools/hooks/cli.py:322-334`) also resolves payload-first — a hook-payload `orchestration_id` outranks the host-set `ATMOFAB_ORCHESTRATION_ID` env var, which is what makes an unvalidated value reachable at all — so the fix should either validate at that resolution point or flip it to env-first; (f) `record_launch` appends the session-run-index row before prompt validation (a raise leaves an orphan `running` row, contradicting the in-code design comment) and its Claude serial-launch guard is check-then-write without a lock across the long `record_launch` body.
  - Completion criterion: each of (a)-(f) has a deterministic test demonstrating the closed behavior, or a recorded decision that the gap is intentionally left to another layer.

- **Fix the conductor-layer robustness findings** (2026-08-10 audit, medium, grouped): (a) `_classify_leaf_infra_error` misses the Python/urllib spellings — a socket `timed out` and `[Errno -3] Temporary failure in name resolution` (EAI_AGAIN) classify as non-retryable and fail the run in one shot, while the Node spellings (`etimedout`/`eai_again`) retry; also record whether `response_deadline_exceeded`/`response_too_large` are intentionally non-retryable; (b) **partly done, and the original wording was wrong about the failure mode** (issue #142). Done: the call site in `_run_pure_verify_substep` is now wrapped the way the producer's is, so a builder raise becomes a recorded `pure_context_assembly_failed` fail_closed outcome before any leaf is spawned; and the fifth document the reviewer now receives — the `## 1.`-`## 4.` slice of `docs/workflow/CHECKS_MODULE_CONTRACT.md` — fails closed on an unreadable (`UnicodeError` included) or unsliceable contract instead of degrading. Residual: the four NODE artifacts (`controlled_spec.md`, `tests.md`, `spec.ir.yaml`, `codegen_bundle.json`) still degrade to `""`, which is the same deliberate design as the producer's ir/tests reads. Correction to the premise: an empty string never reached a billed leaf anyway — `_validate_pure_launch_request_payload` requires a non-empty string for every `PURE_CONTEXT_REQUIRED_KEYS` entry at `record-launch`, and `Conductor.runtime` turns that refusal into a `RuntimeError`, so today's failure mode for an empty node artifact is an UNRECORDED conductor crash, not a billed launch. That is what makes the remaining work worth doing, and it is a different repair from the one this entry described; (c) `substep_start` emits the substep ordinal as `attempt` — emit the real attempt count so the field is uniform with `phase_start`.
  - Completion criterion: classification tests pin the Python spellings as retryable; verify-side missing-document tests fail closed before launch; run-log `attempt` semantics are uniform and tested.

- **Remove the dead apply_patch gate cluster and consolidate the mirror gate/validator pairs** (2026-08-10 audit, medium). Dead code presenting as live defense: `_reject_noncanonical_phase_write`, `_write_phase_authority_violation`, `_resolve_current_phase_state`, `_phase_write_requires_child_running`, and `PHASE_ARTIFACT_GUARDED_PREFIXES` in `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` have no callers (the enforcement moved into `tools/hooks/`); the orchestration tail of `_validate_actual_write_paths` is unreachable and its `directory_authorized` list is never populated (so `_write_directory_authorized_paths` never runs); `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` and `docs/workflow/LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md` still describe the retired gate through a function name that does not exist (`_validate_apply_patch_gate_coverage`). Mirror pairs whose one-sided edits diverge gate and validator: `_validate_run_gate_permissions` ↔ `validate_mcp_build_tool_invocation`, and `_preflight_allows_agent_launch` ↔ `_validate_preflight_payload` (its comment states the mirror requirement explicitly).
  - Fix direction: delete the dead cluster and the stale doc references; extract each mirror pair's shared body into a single helper both sides call.
  - Completion criterion: repo-wide grep finds no reference to the deleted names; each mirror pair shares one implementation; existing gate/validator tests stay green.

- **Replace the static skip guard with a runtime skip inventory** (2026-08-13, medium — the named residue of the L242 work above, which stopped by decision rather than by convergence). `tools/tests/test_skip_reasons_are_declared.py` decides whether a skip is declared by reading source with `ast`. Seven review rounds each produced another Python binding form it did not model — `unittest.case`, `_pytest.outcomes`, star imports, aliases bound by assignment, tuple targets, `pytestmark`, a `lambda` override, a class nested in a class — with no descent in severity across the last three, which is the signature of an instrument approximating Python's semantics one construct at a time. Its own docstring now says it is regression prevention against ordinary spellings, not enforcement.
  - Fix direction: run the suite and compare the skips the runner **actually reported** against `_DECLARED_ENVIRONMENT_SKIPS`. That needs no Python semantics, so every evasion found in those seven rounds closes at once, and it also reaches the routes no reading of skip syntax can (`__test__ = False`, a test defined under an `if`, `del test_f`). It cannot be a test inside the suite it must run — hence the dependency on the CI item below, or on a `tools/` entry point the operator runs.
  - Completion criterion: a check that fails when any reported skip reason is absent from the table, driven by a real suite run; and the static guard either deleted or demoted to a fast pre-filter with the runtime check named as the authority. Note the table must then have exactly one definition shared by both.

- **Add MCP-server core tests, a dependency declaration, and CI** (2026-08-10 audit, medium). `mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py`'s command-construction and RPC layers (`_build_command`, `_run_command`, `_recommended_build_system`, `_handle_request`, `_read_message`, `_write_message`, `tool_detect_build_system`) and `mcp_servers/mcp_call.py` have no direct tests, although every compile/run is required to traverse them; the repository declares no dependencies (no `pyproject.toml`/`requirements.txt` — PyYAML and pytest are implicit) and has no CI.
  - **The parser decision above adds three entries this item now owns (2026-08-13)**: `tree_sitter` and `tree_sitter_fortran` become RUNTIME dependencies (the gate reads Fortran through them), while **LLVM flang becomes a DEVELOPMENT requirement** — it never runs in a workflow, but the differential harness that checks the gate against an independent front end needs it, and the measurements behind that split (four LLVM releases agreeing byte for byte) were taken with 17.0.6 / 18.1.8 / 19.1.7 / 21.1.8 installed together. Note for whoever writes the install line: the binary is `flang-new` up to LLVM 19 and `flang` from 21, so anything that invokes it must probe both names.
  - Completion criterion: command-construction and RPC framing tests cover the server success paths; a `pyproject.toml` (or requirements files) declares runtime and test dependencies and the supported Python version; a CI workflow runs the suite on push.

- **Sweep the documentation drift found by the 2026-08-10 audit** (medium, checklist): `AGENTS.md` uses the retired substep name `Generate.syntax` (now one checker inside `Generate.gate`); the `docs/workflow/LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md` step↔skill table uses the retired `plan` token and lists `execute`/`judge` as steps; the `docs/workflow/phases/phase_04_validate.md` retry table keys on `failure_class` values the host no longer produces (`evidence_mismatch`/`runtime_error`); the `docs/workflow/CODEGEN_BUNDLE_CONTRACT.md` JSON example omits three required keys (`files[].modules`, `entrypoints[].module`, `state_bindings[].module`) and is rejected by `validate_bundle` as written; `docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md` names a nonexistent `_validate_workflow_bash_policy` (actual: `evaluate_common_policy`) and, with `LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md`, misspells `validate_write_access` with a leading underscore; the `tools/run_workflow.py` `--wait-usage-reset` argparse help predates human-form reset support and contradicts current behavior; `docs/workflow/MCP_COMMAND_LOG_PLACEMENT.md` uses the pre-rename `generate/<id>/` path segment, `step=execute`, and an undefined `<exec_id>`; the `docs/GLOSSARY.md` dependency-coverage entry still keys on legacy `dependency.resolved.yaml` (same class: `docs/TESTS.md`); `docs/WORKSPACE_LAYOUT.md` has an extra level in the reservations path and drops the `active_children`/`cleanup_committed` file extensions; `spec/schema/ir/shape_expr.schema.json` patterns use the `^...$` anchors `spec/schema/SCHEMA.md` forbids; the `dynamics_shallow_water_profile_2d_rusanov_p0_ssprk2` profile `tests.md` pins `time_update_2d_ssprk2@0.1.0` (catalog carries 0.3.0 only); `tools/prompt_templates/step_agent.txt`/`substep_agent.txt` justify the launch-prompt prohibition via a nonexistent `Agent` tool (leaves launch via `claude -p`/stdin); the `{{ACTOR_ROLE}}` substitution in `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` is a no-op (no template contains the placeholder) — remove it or use it.
  - **One more, found by the README rewrite (2026-08-13):** the retired `case.resolved.yaml` survives in 12 `spec/**/controlled_spec.md` / `tests.md` files (every in-tree `spec` but `harness_fortran_cpu`) — the `dependency.resolved.yaml` class above under a different name. Unlike every other entry in this list these are human-authored `spec` content, so the edit is a `spec` change and carries whatever `spec_version` consequence that implies; that is why the README rewrite left them. (The sibling finding of that rewrite, `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1-3 claiming `Build` launches a `step agent`, was fixed there rather than deferred here.)
  - Completion criterion: each listed item is fixed or explicitly recorded as intentional; where a class recurs (retired names surviving in canonical docs), prefer extending the doc-sync test family (`test_cli_reference_sync` style) over prose-only fixes.

- (Optional, larger) **Decompose the two outsized modules along the seams the 2026-08-10 audit identified** (structural). `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` (21.8k lines, ~400 top-level definitions, a ~1,100-line `main()`) and `tools/validate_pipeline_semantics.py` (13.4k lines) each hold six to nine domains with one-way dependencies; the splitter-shadowing defect above is the size class producing real bugs. Seams: Fortran interface extraction, JSON transaction/lock infrastructure, capability/write-roots gates, bwrap profiles, FS-diff write validation, preflight/probe, launch-prompt rendering, and records+CLI (runtime); stanza parsing/normalization, Makefile analysis, runner static gates, and orchestration hierarchy validation (validator). A split also converts the cross-module private-name imports (`workflow_conductor` and `backends.language.fortran.signatures` importing `_`-named helpers) into explicit public APIs.
  - Completion criterion: not scheduled; recorded so any future decomposition follows these seams instead of ad-hoc extraction.

- **The gate stderr-capture form the documents teach is refused by Claude Code 2.1.234** (2026-08-18, medium — measured, CLI version regression). `docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, `docs/WORKSPACE_LAYOUT.md`, `docs/workflow/LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md`, and two hint strings in `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` instruct every leaf to capture a gate result with `2>workspace/tmp/<agent_run_id>/last_gate_stderr.txt`. That redirect is refused: the permission layer answers `Output redirection to '<absolute path>' was blocked. For security, Claude Code may only write to files in the allowed working directories for this session: '<repo root>'.` — naming as the allowed directory the very root the target sits under. The refusal covers the `2>`, `>`, and `>>` spellings, a new and an existing target alike; `2>/dev/null` is unaffected. The same command without the redirect is allowed, so the entry `Bash(python3 tools/orchestration_runtime.py *)` is not what fails.
  - **Measurement (unbilled, 2026-08-18, CLI 2.1.234).** A local HTTP server on `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` implements the Messages endpoint: the first request is answered with a synthetic Bash `tool_use` carrying the command under test, and the second request body carries the `tool_result`, which is the permission layer's own verdict. The CLI is launched with the permission-relevant part of the agentic leaf's argv (`--setting-sources project --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config .mcp.json --disable-slash-commands --output-format json -p`); the leaf additionally passes `--disallowedTools` and, when the configuration names one, `--model`, neither of which bears on a Bash permission decision. Every verdict below was reproduced in a clean `git worktree` of `origin/main`, which carries only the committed `.claude/settings.json` — no `.claude/settings.local.json` and no `~/.claude.json` project entry — so no operator-local grant is in force. **The measured ARGV and FILE above are those of 2026-08-18 and have both since changed** (issue #63 final form): a leaf now launches `--setting-sources user` against a private `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and its permission layer is `leaf_config/claude/settings.json`. The verdicts are about how the CLI classifies Bash commands, which is independent of the tier; a re-measurement should use the current argv and file.
  - **Severity is bounded by the tool result.** A command's stderr is returned to the leaf inside the `tool_result` (measured: a failing `python3 tools/check_artifact_syntax.py …` returns `Exit code 1` followed by its stderr line). The taught form is therefore a broken convenience, not a lost diagnostic. **Unmeasured residue of that bound**: `run-gate` emits its result as ONE stderr line and `_extract_gate_violations` caps nothing, so a large `violations[]` produces a very long line, and whether the harness's tool-output truncation keeps its tail was not measured. A gate failure with many violations is where the file capture would have mattered most; that case belongs to issue #77.
  - **Version attribution.** The 2026-08-07 run (`preflight.json` records CLI 2.1.223) executed that exact redirect: its `hooks/native_hook_events.jsonl` carries a `post_command_execute` event for the command. The behaviour changed between 2.1.223 and 2.1.234; 2.1.223 was not re-run.
  - **Re-measure when the CLI version changes.** `test_committed_allowlist_covers_the_commands_the_repository_instructs` models this CLI's `Bash(...)` matching in Python — compound decomposition, a trailing ` *` permitting the argument-less form, a redirect refused whatever entry the rest matches — and every one of those is an undocumented behaviour of an external tool, measured on 2.1.234 and recorded with its spellings in that test's docstring. It is a mirror with no drift protection: on a later CLI it can pass while the layer refuses a command every leaf runs, or fail on one the layer allows. The unbilled harness that took the measurement is described in this entry; re-run it against the five spellings when the pinned version moves.
  - **Fix direction**, one of: (a) drop the capture form from the five documents and the two runtime hints and state that a gate's stderr is read from the tool result; (b) keep the form and add a committed `permissions.allow` entry that admits the redirect, if one exists that the CLI's redirect check honours — unverified, and the refusal text suggests the check is not allowlist-driven; (c) have the runtime write the gate's stderr to the leaf-readable path itself, so no leaf-issued redirect is needed. (a) and (c) are independent of CLI behaviour; (b) is not.
  - **Completion criterion**: no repository document or runtime hint instructs a leaf to issue a Bash redirect that the measurement above refuses, and the replacement route is exercised end-to-end by the harness described here or by a billed run.
  - **DONE (fix direction (a), 2026-08-19).** The five documents and the two `tools/orchestration_runtime.py` hints now state that a gate result is the last line of the command's own stderr and is read from the command result; no surface instructs a redirect. Two premises fell with it and were rewritten rather than narrowed: `docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md` and `docs/workflow/LAUNCH_PROMPT_REFERENCE.md` both said a redirect is correct when appended to an already-permitted command (the stdout capture on the allowlisted `python3 workspace/tmp/*` route was measured refused too), and `test_instruction_surfaces_admit_only_permitted_redirects_into_the_tmp_root` encoded that same premise as its admission rule. It is now `test_instruction_surfaces_teach_no_redirect_into_the_tmp_root`, admits nothing, reads `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md` as well, and no longer skips a bare idiom or a line-continuation tail — strictly stricter, with `_allowlisted_bash_matchers` deleted as its only consumer. The runtime hint is pinned on the RENDERED launch prompt (`GateRunbookTests.test_runbook_teaches_reading_stderr_from_the_result_not_a_redirect`), asserted on the split half that governs the gate result, since the line states two rules. Mutation-checked: restoring the old hint fails the rendered pin; restoring the old sentence in `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md` or in `docs/AGENT_CONTRACT.md` fails the surfaces check, and the second of those is the shape the retired admission used to allow.
  - **DONE (fix direction (c), issue #77).** A gate result now outlives the tool call that produced it: `run_gate` writes the same one-line summary it prints to stderr into `workspace/tmp/<agent_run_id>/gate_results/<gate>.json`, one file per gate name, replaced on each run of that gate. Direction (c) was chosen over (b) because it is independent of CLI behaviour, and over the `--stderr-file` variant of it because a leaf-named path would have added a leaf-supplied input to a gate for a path that is fully derivable. The two costs the issue named were already paid in the tree: the child-window FS-diff already exempts every path under `manifest_allowed_tmp_root`, and `_build_access_policy` already puts `workspace/tmp/<agent_run_id>/` into `allowed_read_roots`. The copy is a convenience for the leaf and never evidence about it: `gates/<agent_run_id>/<gate>.json` is what the audit reads, and a leaf that forges the tmp copy gains nothing. **Round 3 narrowed that reason**: it is not that nothing reads the copy — the audit SKILL cross-checks the six shared fields when the copy survives — but that the copy decides nothing, so forging only it manufactures a disagreement the auditor is told to chase. **Round 1 corrected the first draft of that sentence**, which said the canonical record is one "no leaf can write": `gates/<arid>/` is rw-bound into the leaf's own sandbox because the leaf runs `run-gate` itself, `_should_ignore_runtime_snapshot_path` exempts the prefix from the terminal FS-diff, and the interpreter route (`docs/HOOKS.md` §"Layer boundary", the widest recorded residue of the Bash write refusal) reaches either file. Write authority never separated the two; which file is READ does. The write is best-effort: it cannot change the verdict, and on failure it removes any earlier copy rather than leaving a stale result that reads as this one's. A stale copy is narrowed, not eliminated: `run-gate` invalidates the copy immediately after the gate-name check, so every refusal raised from that point on removes it (the unsupported-gate-name refusal precedes it and can key no copy that was ever written). **Round 2 shipped that as an unconditional "present iff a run completed since your last attempt" and round 3 measured it false four ways** — two guards then still ran ahead of the unlink, an `argparse` rejection or a permission-layer refusal never reaches the code at all, a failed unlink is swallowed, and none of it speaks to the ARTIFACT changing after a verdict. The first three are closed or declared; the fourth is a property of the persisted gate document too and predates this change, and is recorded as its own residue below. Round 1 shipped the weaker version — `evaluated_at`, then `args_json` and `exit_code`, plus three prose warnings — and round 2 replaced the warnings with the invariant, keeping the fields because they say WHICH run a copy belongs to and are shared with the persisted gate document, which is what makes the audit cross-check possible. **Still unmeasured, and now inconsequential**: whether any `permissions.allow` entry can lift the redirect refusal. The long-`violations[]` residue recorded above is narrowed but not closed — the file is not truncated, so a violations list too long for the tool result survives on disk, but whether the tool result truncates it in the first place was still not measured.
  - **Not a defect, measured in the same pass (issue #75, CLOSED 2026-08-18).** The read shapes that issue was opened about — `sed -n '1,200p' <file>`, `find <root> -iname … -print 2>/dev/null`, `cat <file> 2>/dev/null | head -100`, and pipes of them — all execute, although no committed `permissions.allow` entry matches them: the CLI applies its own read-only-command analysis ahead of that list. The controls that prove the layer is live: `curl …` answers `This command requires approval`, and `find . -name '*.nope' -delete` answers `find with '-delete' executes commands or modifies files — cannot be auto-allowed by a Bash(find:*) prefix rule`, while `sed -i` and `rm` are refused as writes. The 2026-08-07 run corroborates it independently: 10 of the 11 commands its hook plain-allowed carry a `post_command_execute` event and therefore executed. Extending `_is_auto_approvable_readonly_bash` to `sed` / `find` / a `/dev/null` discard was planned and abandoned on this measurement, because it would change no outcome while adding an admission grammar to a fail-closed predicate. `docs/HOOKS.md` §"Layer boundary" carries the measurement.

- **There is no development documentation, and this branch created a tree that needs one** (2026-08-19, decided by the user rather than measured into existence). Everything under `docs/` documents the WORKFLOW — the phases, their contracts, the orchestration, the boundary the generated system must respect. Nothing documents DEVELOPING this repository: how a change to it is reviewed, what the enforcement machinery expects of a change, what an operator's interactive session loads. Until 2026-08-19 that absence was invisible because the material lived in one operator's home directory; moving the two review skills into `.claude/skills/` makes it a directory in the repository that no document accounts for.
  - Two known consequences to write down when the document exists, both measured on this branch. First, `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Scope reads as an exhaustive partition — seven in-scope bullets, then "Out of scope, each for a stated reason" — and `.claude/` appears in neither half, so a new tree of documents sits outside the instrument by silence rather than by decision. Second, that tree carries sampled tokens that the ratchet never reads: `.claude/skills/**` matches none of `_SCANNED_GLOBS`, and the baseline holds 48 files with no dotted entry. Measure it rather than quoting a figure — **the number moves with every edit to those files**, and it moved twice inside the branch that wrote this entry (33 at the time of writing, 36 two commits later). One line, runnable as stored:
    ```bash
    python3 -c "import sys,pathlib;sys.path[:0]=['tools/tests','.'];from test_backend_boundary import _COMPILED as C;fs=[p for p in pathlib.Path('.claude/skills').rglob('*') if p.is_file()];n=[(p,sum(len(r.findall(p.read_text(errors='replace'))) for r in C.values())) for p in fs];print(*[f'{p}: {c}' for p,c in n if c],sep='\n');print('total',sum(c for _,c in n))"
    ```
  Where it sits is not where the eye goes, and this is the part to keep after the numbers rot: the Fortran spelling checklist in `atmofab-enforcement-change` is a small minority of the total, and the rest are episodes and identifier names spread across BOTH skills — including `atmofab-review-loop`, which has no checklist at all. A future reader who fixes only the checklist will have moved a fraction of it. (Two earlier versions of this entry quoted a split; both were falsified by the next commit of the branch that wrote them.)
  - Deciding the boundary question inside a documentation vacuum is what this defers: whether `.claude/` is declared out of scope with a stated reason, or brought into the scanned globs so the growth is bounded the way it is everywhere else, is a decision a development document should make and record, not a line added to a workflow document in passing.
  - **An operator step this repository cannot perform for itself, recorded here because nothing else can hold it**: the two skills also exist at `~/.claude/skills/atmofab-review-loop/` and `~/.claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change/`, and a user-level skill WINS the name collision — measured, a session in this checkout loads the home copy, whose description is still Japanese and whose `mutation_check.py` is the pre-move version. Until those two directories are deleted, every correction in the repository copy is inert. Verify the repo copy loads (its description is English), then delete them.
  - Completion criterion: a development-facing document exists and is reachable from `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md`; it accounts for `.claude/skills/` and states the boundary decision for it; `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Scope names `.claude/` in one half or the other.

  - **DONE (2026-08-21).** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` exists and every clause of the completion criterion is met. It is reached from `AGENTS.md` §"Dedicated rule documents", from the new `AGENTS.md` §"Canonical record placement", from `CLAUDE.md`, and from `docs/README.md`; it accounts for `.claude/` in its configuration-layer table; and `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Scope now names `.claude/` in its **out-of-scope** half.
    - **The boundary decision: `.claude/` is out of scope, and the reason is what it IS rather than what it costs to measure.** The rule bounds technology knowledge in the neutral core — what a run reads on its way to producing a target-stack system. A workflow leaf loads nothing under `.claude/` (measured; `CLAUDE.md` carries the measurement and its limits), so that tree is the operator's own session, and the technology it names, it names ABOUT this repository's own instruments. That is the `tools/tests/` reason, applied to a different tree. The COST is recorded next to the decision in `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`: the ratchet does not read that tree, so its spellings are unmeasured and a technology change has to sweep them by hand.
    - **The figure, re-measured at `a2cc438` with the one-liner this item stores** (that commit predates the rename, so the skill directories are spelled `.claude/skills/metdsl-*` there — `docs/GLOSSARY.md` §13) (it moves with every edit to those files, so it is pinned to a commit rather than quoted loose): **38** total. `atmofab-enforcement-change/SKILL.md` 20, its `references/dual-read-pairs.md` 9, `references/verification.md` 5, `references/failure-routing.md` 1, `atmofab-review-loop/SKILL.md` 3. The earlier characterisation half holds and half does not. It holds, and by more than it claimed: the explicit spelling checklist is **3** of the enforcement skill's 20 (its five bullets, `SKILL.md` lines 179-192 at that commit), the other 17 being episodes and identifier names. An earlier draft of this line said "roughly 5", written from reading the checklist rather than from scanning its span. It does NOT hold that the material is spread across both skills in any balance worth planning around: `atmofab-review-loop` carries 3 of the 38.
    - Re-measured at `e2de95a` (spelled `metdsl-*` there, as above), after the two skills were split into rule + episode files, and the PROPERTY is what to keep: `.claude/skills/**` holds **43** occurrences and **none of them is in either `SKILL.md`**. Every target-stack spelling now sits in a `references/` file, which is read on demand rather than on every invocation — the explicit checklist moved whole to `atmofab-enforcement-change/references/source-text-surface.md` (5), the rest being episodes and identifier names in `references/judgment-episodes.md` (9), `dual-read-pairs.md` (13), `input-surfaces.md` (7), `verification.md` (5), `failure-routing.md` (1) and, in the other skill, `signs-episodes.md` (1) and `class-descent-log.md` (2). The total moved 42 -> 43 across that commit; the relocation is the point, not the delta. **The line above naming `SKILL.md` lines 179-192 as where the checklist sits is a record of `a2cc438` and no longer a place to look** — a sweep starts at `source-text-surface.md`.
    - **The operator step this repository could not perform for itself is DONE, and this item's sub-bullet describing it was stale.** `~/.claude/skills/` no longer holds `atmofab-review-loop` or `atmofab-enforcement-change` (measured 2026-08-21; only an unrelated skill remains there), so the repository copies are the ones that load and every correction in them is live. The sub-bullet above is kept as the record of why it mattered.
    - **Landed with it, each because the material had no owner in the tree:** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §0-3 (the Codex backend's operator setup, every item of which is machine-local state no committed file can declare — it had never been written down); the host side of `docs/HOOKS.md` §"Layer boundary" (the workflow's trusted code does not read the operator's personal backend homes; since issue #64 it reads homes it AUTHORED, so the line is authorship rather than location); `docs/CONTROLLED_SPEC.md` §Purpose (language-neutrality as a requirement on the whole document — `docs/BACKEND_BOUNDARY.md` §Scope was citing this document as the owner of a requirement it did not state); and two `.gitignore` entries this repository was carrying per-machine, `.claude/settings.local.json` through the operator's global excludes file and `.claude/worktrees/` through `.git/info/exclude`, both of which are per-clone and so would have offered one machine's state for commit in any other checkout.
    - **The records that moved, and where they went.** Four measurement episodes and one rejected design were posted as issue comments (#63 the CLI-internal reading behind the configuration-directory levers, the lever classification, and the seven axes the closure does NOT make reproducible; #64 the closure record and the retention decision, which this issue had none of; #65 the verification of the expectation its 2026-08-14 comment left open; #75 the abandoned grammar-gated admission design in full; #77 a cross-reference to it; #47 the field verification that is still outstanding). Fourteen standing rules were routed **9 / 2 / 3**: nine into `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, two into the documents that own their subject, and three needed no edit because the two development skills and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` already stated them in full — read before being ruled out, not assumed. **The rule-to-destination table is in PR #90's description**, one row per rule; do not try to recover the split by counting bullets in those sections, which two reviewers did and got 14 — one rule produced two bullets, and four further bullets there are rules with no operator-memory ancestor at all.
    - **Residue, both measured while doing this and neither pulled in.** (1) `docs/README.md` and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` now require a `docs/design/` note to state its `Status`; **one of the seven does not** — `codex_backend_support_plan.md`, whose only occurrence of the word is an unrelated sentence about an exit status. Deciding that note's actual status is the work, not adding a line. **An earlier draft of this residue said three**, produced by an anchored `^status` grep: `dependency_build_followup_prompt.md` states its status as a leading blockquote and `deterministic_followups.md` as a `## Status` heading, and the requirement names no form, so both satisfy it. The measurement was wrong, not the notes. (2) **PR #48 has never been exercised by a billed run**, so issue #47's property — every leaf records a usable cost — is closed but unconfirmed in the field; the check to run against the next billed run is on #47.
    - **Not migrated, deliberately**: the operator's runtime state and credentials under `~/.atmofab` and the backend CLI homes (that is what those directories are for, by the design issue #64 settled), and the session-safety configuration of an operator's own CLI, which is per-user by construction — `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` §"Repository environment" records the FACTS an operator builds one from instead.

- **The codex `hooks` preflight check measures a configuration the leaf does not use** (2026-08-21, medium — measured, latent, surfaced by a review of the TODO:414 branch and recorded rather than pulled into it). `hooks_enabled` gates a codex launch and is read from `codex features list` run under the **operator's** `CODEX_HOME`. A leaf runs under the isolated home `_prepare_codex_workflow_home` builds, whose host-authored `config.toml` contains only a `[projects."<repo_root>"] trust_level = "untrusted"` table and **no `[features]` table**. So the gate certifies the operator's configuration and the leaf loads a different one.
  - **Measured (codex-cli 0.147.0, 2026-08-21)**, four probes of `codex features list` with `CODEX_HOME` pointed at: the operator's home (`hooks = true` in its `config.toml`) → `true`; an EMPTY directory → `true`; a home carrying only `[features]\nhooks = false` → `false`; a home carrying a copy of what `_prepare_codex_workflow_home` actually writes → **`true`**. So the value is decided by the home's `config.toml`, and the isolated home inherits the CLI's own default.
  - **Latent, not live.** The default is `true`, so today the leaf gets its hook layer and the gate's verdict happens to be right. The failure is a CLI release whose default flips, or an isolated-home `config.toml` that ever gains a `[features]` table: the gate reads `true` from the operator's home, the run launches, and the leaf comes up with no read guard, no write guard, and no capability gate — the enforcement boundary the gate exists to certify. Fail-open, and silent.
  - Fix directions, either or both: have `_prepare_codex_workflow_home` write `[features] hooks = true` into the isolated `config.toml` so the leaf's configuration is declared rather than inherited; and/or run the feature probe with `CODEX_HOME` set to a prepared home so the gate measures what the leaf will load. The first is the smaller change and makes the second's verdict trivially true.
  - Completion criterion: the value of `hooks` a leaf runs under is decided by a file this repository authors, and a test drives the probe against that file rather than against the operator's home. Touching `_prepare_codex_workflow_home` is an enforcement-machinery change (`.claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change/`).
  - `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §0-3 carries the measurement and states what the check does not establish.

- **`WritePreflightConcurrencyTests::test_concurrent_preflight_and_mark_do_not_clobber_verified` fails intermittently in a full-suite run** (2026-08-21, low — observed, root cause NOT investigated; surfaced by the TODO:414 branch, which changes no runtime code, and recorded rather than pulled into it). Observed **twice** in full-suite runs of `python3 -m pytest tools/tests/ -q -p no:randomly` on the same host, several runs apart. In both cases the row passed on the next full-suite run and passed three times out of three when run alone, so the failure needs the rest of the suite running beside it.
  - Not diagnosed. The two plausible directions, neither checked: contention on a shared temporary root under load, and a real race in the preflight write path that only widens when the machine is busy — which is what the test is about, so the second would make the flake the finding rather than the noise.
  - Danger of leaving it: an intermittent red in the suite is read as noise by the next person, and this repository's own review procedure tells a reviewer to compare a suite DELTA against a baseline. A row that fails at random makes that comparison unreliable in exactly the direction that hides a real regression.
  - Completion criterion: the failure is reproduced deliberately (a loop of full-suite runs, or the same load synthesised) and either fixed or shown to be a fixture-contention artefact with the fixture corrected — not "ran it N times and it passed", which is not a non-existence proof.
- **[CLOSED 2026-08-22, on the same branch] A leaf now holds `Glob`, and issue #42's unvalidated-`pattern` residue applied to it** (2026-08-22, medium — measured, surfaced by the review of the issue #71 branch and recorded rather than pulled into it). **[The next two sentences describe the code as it stood when this was filed; both were made false on the same branch — see the CLOSED note at the end.]** `tools/hooks/cli.py` `_PATH_SEARCH_TOOLS` validated the `path` of `Grep`/`Glob` fail-closed (an absent one is validated as the repository root and blocks) and stated that **`Glob`'s** `pattern` was NOT validated: an absolute or `../` pattern reaches outside the validated root. `Grep`'s `pattern` is a CONTENT regex and names no path (`docs/HOOKS.md` is canonical for that distinction), so it is NOT part of this item — validating it would refuse legitimate regexes such as `\.\./config` or `^/usr/local`, which is the over-refusal direction this repository keeps paying for. An earlier version of this entry said "their `pattern`", which prescribed exactly that mistake. MEASURED on CLI 2.1.238: `Grep` and `Glob` are absent from the CLI's DEFAULT roster, so the pre-issue-#71 denylist argv gave a leaf neither one — the residue was unreachable for a claude leaf on this CLI, and unreachable for a codex leaf, which has no such tools. The issue #71 allowlist names both (it is derived from the `PreToolUse` matcher coverage, and those matchers long predate the vendor's change), so the residue is now REACHABLE. The first plan was not to close it on that branch — narrowing a second enforcement layer is a different change from choosing which tools a leaf gets — and the closure is not a refusal but a validation — `Glob`'s pattern literal prefix should go through `validate_read_access` the way `path` does, so an absolute pattern INSIDE an allowed root keeps working rather than being refused wholesale. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` both recorded the residue already; what neither recorded is that it was dormant and stopped being so. **CLOSED rather than filed**, after two review rounds raised it independently — and then REDESIGNED four more times, which this note records because the first version of it was the one that shipped a live gap. **[SUPERSEDED on 2026-08-25 by the entry below: that design was DELETED and the check now refuses only an absolute pattern. Kept because it is the design whose first version shipped a live gap, and because a reader tracing this closure needs to know it existed.]** The closure was: normalize the pattern, expand brace alternatives (bounded, and continuing past an unbalanced `{`), judge each alternative's literal prefix through the same `validate_read_access` as `path`, and judge an out-of-repository expansion on its resolved location. The first version validated only the literal prefix `_glob_literal_prefix` gives the Bash extractor — so an ordinary pattern resolved under `path` and was authorized by the entry that authorized `path`, while `../*`, `../../etc/*`, `/etc/*` and `~/.ssh/*` were blocked. **At HEAD only the last two are**: `../*` and `../../etc/*` are ALLOWED, deliberately, because the tool reads nothing through them. `Grep`'s pattern is untouched: it is a content regex naming no path, and validating it would refuse a legitimate search for `\.\./config` — witnessed too. This entry stays as the record of why a tightening commit had to close a second layer.
- **Two hook rows depend on the checkout sitting exactly two levels below `$HOME`** (2026-08-22, low — root cause MEASURED 2026-08-23 by a reviewer of the issue #71 branch; recorded rather than pulled in). `tools/tests/test_hooks_common.py::ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests::{test_blocks_bash_only_tilde_prefixes,test_directory_options_anchor_like_cd}` pass in the main checkout and fail at the SAME commit in a `git worktree` checkout of it. The consequence is operational: every worktree-based mutation run carries two constant failures, which a mutation harness reads as a red baseline (`mutation_check.py` exits 2) or, with `--skip-baseline`, as two hunks wrongly scored `killed`. MEASURED since: it is not worktree-specific — a plain `git clone` fails the same two rows, at `origin/main` as well as on the branch. The fixture uses `repo_root = os.getcwd()`, and both rows resolve upward: `cat ~-/.claude.json` and `--directory=../..` reach the operator's real `$HOME/.claude.json` only when the checkout is exactly two levels below `$HOME`, which the checkout it was measured in was and a scratch clone is not. So `test_blocks_bash_only_tilde_prefixes`'s middle row passes for a different reason than its docstring claims — it witnesses a `..` walk onto a real protected file, not a `~+` prefix — and the same holds for `test_directory_options_anchor_like_cd`. The fix is to build the relationship in the fixture instead of borrowing the host's, which is the shape `.claude/skills/atmofab-review-loop` describes for a test that reasons about a relationship whose two halves the fixture does not both build. A THIRD row in the same file skips for the same reason (`needs the checkout under $HOME`); it was omitted from an earlier version of this entry, which called the set two. **The set is a function of the depth, not a fixed pair** (measured 2026-09-01 at `0d88bf6`, in a checkout ONE level under `$HOME`): there the two rows named above pass and a different two fail — `test_folds_chained_relative_cd`, which asserts the depth outright, and `test_a_variable_cd_target_folds_from_where_bash_lands`, which builds `$HOME/work` and expects one `cd ..` to land on `$HOME`.
