Naming rows rather than the property is why an earlier version of this entry could be read as a closed list. **DONE (2026-09-02, `2f7a7a0`).** The remaining coupling is gone in the direction this entry names: `ForbidBackendCredentialReadTests._built_home` BUILDS a temporary `$HOME`, a checkout at a chosen depth under it, and the plain subdirectories a case needs, and `_call` / `_policy` take that root instead of `os.getcwd()`. `test_folds_chained_relative_cd` runs at a fixture-chosen depth of 2 and now asserts BOTH directions — the chain that reaches `$HOME` blocks, one `cd ..` short of it does not — where before it asserted the host's depth and skipped or failed on it; `test_a_variable_cd_target_folds_from_where_bash_lands` builds its own `work/` subdirectory. That second row had a cause this entry did not name: `/home/seiya/work` is a SYMLINK to `/work/seiya`, so the fold resolved the link and the following `cd ..` landed on the link target's parent rather than on `$HOME` — a depth assumption AND a shape assumption, both borrowed from the host. A THIRD row was found by running the file from several locations instead of only from the red one: `test_padding_does_not_disarm_the_bounded_candidates` fails at every depth EXCEPT this checkout's, because its two `~/work` rows read `../.claude.json` and reached the protected file from `repo_root` — one level under `$HOME` here — instead of from the far-away anchor the row exists to exercise. It runs at a built home now too. The declared skip is gone with the depth assertion that raised it; `"needs the checkout under $HOME"` was its only use, so the capability left `_DECLARED_ENVIRONMENT_SKIPS` in the same commit (`test_no_declared_reason_is_unused` would otherwise fail, which is that table working as designed). Both fold rows still witness the fold: mutating `_cd_anchor_dirs`' fold step (`current = landed` -> a no-op) fails both. MEASURED at `2f7a7a0`, `python3 -m pytest tools/tests/test_hooks_common.py tools/tests/test_skip_reasons_are_declared.py -q`: **378 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** in the primary checkout (`/home/seiya/atmofab`, depth 1) and in five `git worktree` checkouts of the same commit — depths 2, 3, 4 and 5 under `$HOME`, and one outside `$HOME` entirely under `/tmp` — where the same command with `fc821b7`'s `tools/tests/` gives **2 failed** at every one of the six (plus the declared skip in the `/tmp` one). Full suite in the primary checkout at `2f7a7a0`: **5595 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, against **5593 passed / 2 failed** at `fc821b7`, taken in a worktree placed at the same depth 1 under `$HOME` so the comparison is not itself location-dependent. The two failures there are `test_folds_chained_relative_cd` and `test_a_variable_cd_target_folds_from_where_bash_lands`, NOT the pair this entry opened with — at depth 1 that pair passes, which is the whole point of the sentence above about the set being a function of the depth. An earlier version of this line called them the opening pair and so contradicted its own paragraph. A round-1 reviewer then found the DEPTH sweep was only half the instrument: it varied where the checkout sits and never varied the SHAPE of `$HOME`, and two more rows of the same class reconstructed `$HOME` as `/home/` plus its basename — `test_blocks_separator_glued_cd_targets` (`H=/home/<name>; cd $H && cat .claude.json`) and `test_applies_parameter_transformations_to_the_value`, red on any host whose home is not that shape (macOS, a container's `/root`, a CI `/github/home`). The second one goes red through its `${X/x/<last>}` / `${X//x/<last>}` rows, which break as soon as the home path CONTAINS the constant `x` they spell; its `${X/x*/<name>}` sibling stays GREEN while resolving to `/home/<name>`, which off this host is not `$HOME` at all — the passing-in-the-wrong-place half of the same defect. (An earlier version of this sentence hung the containment failure on the `x*` spelling; measured, the failing row is `${X/x/…}`.) Both are fixed the same way — take the literal from `Path.home()`, and pick the glob's marker character against the actual value (`_char_absent_from`) instead of spelling it as a constant. The first version of that helper drew the marker from a fifteen-character pool and so was itself a partial function of the host: a round-2 reviewer built a `$HOME` containing all fifteen and got a red row belonging to no change, which is the cost this whole entry exists to remove. The alphabet is `[A-Za-z0-9]` now, and the `fail` is kept rather than softened to a fallback — a marker that IS in the value would leave the row green while asserting something else. MEASURED at `96657b5`, `test_hooks_common.py` under five synthetic `$HOME` shapes (plain, one containing `x`, one with uppercase components, one with digits, one directly under the temp root), each in a worktree at a built depth: **364 passed, 0 failed** at each, against **2 failed** at each with `73a7733`'s copy of the file; a `$HOME` carrying the old fifteen-character pool is green at `96657b5` and one carrying all 62 alphanumerics is the single row that fails, deliberately. **Re-running the shape sweep needs `PYTHONUSERBASE` pointed at the real home** — overriding `HOME` moves `~/.local`, so `python3 -m pytest` otherwise dies with `No module named pytest`, and that is not a finding about the suite. What this does NOT close is the class: a row that reasons about the checkout's location or about the shape of `$HOME` without building it can reappear, nothing in the suite detects that shape, and each time it was a sweep over environments — not a red row in this checkout — that found the rows. MEASURED at `96657b5`, what the file still assumes about `$HOME`, whole-file runs against the same runs on `fc821b7`: a home containing a SPACE gives 16 failed (17 on `fc821b7`), a home that is a SYMLINK 38 (39), a home containing `[`/`]` 35 (35), and `HOME` UNSET 11 (13). None is touched by this branch, all are pre-existing, and the same reviewer applied the gain criterion to the one that looks like a guard defect rather than fixture noise — an unresolved `~`-spelled path under a symlinked home — and put it out of scope: what a leaf gains by reading the operator's credentials is nothing toward reporting a `substep` done (`AGENTS.md` §Development premises). Three further rows borrow the checkout's CONTENTS rather than build them (`test_the_backstop_fires_once_a_path_leaves_the_repository`, `test_the_backstop_does_not_fire_on_the_in_repo_dot_claude`, `test_a_substitution_elsewhere_does_not_block_the_repo_s_own_dot_claude` need a cwd containing `.claude/`): repository-shaped rather than host-shaped, since `.claude/` is tracked.
- **Four mechanisms of the Bash credential guard are unobserved by the credential-guard suite** (2026-09-02, low-to-medium — measured by a round-2 reviewer of the issue #84 branch over 26 mechanism mutants, on `fc821b7` and on the branch alike; recorded rather than pulled in, since the branch changed no guard code). Deleting any one of these leaves `tools/tests/test_hooks_common.py` fully green: (i) `_command_reads_protected_host_path`'s raw-command MARKER-REGEX scan — check (1), the half that exists to catch `~` / `$HOME` / `${HOME…}` / `<abs-home>` spellings when adjacent punctuation mangles tokenization — deleted outright gives 364 passed; (ii) the HEAD half of that function's resolve-budget anchor truncation, whose comment says it was added because padding `cd`s can precede the decisive one; (iii) the `lexists` / `.resolve()` symlink fallback at the end of the per-base loop, which is the only defense against a symlink planted inside the rw-bound `~/.claude`; (iv) `${V/#pat/rep}` / `${V/%pat/rep}` anchored substitution. **It was FIVE when this entry was first written, and the fifth was wrong**: `left_repo` — the discriminator that reads an in-repo name differently once a `cd` has left the checkout — IS witnessed. Both spellings of the mutant (`left_repo = False`, and `or left_repo` -> `or False`) fail `test_the_backstop_fires_once_a_path_leaves_the_repository` on `fc821b7` and on this branch alike. The entry carried it because a reviewer's list was transcribed instead of re-run — the one step `.claude/skills/atmofab-review-loop` requires before classifying anything as residual, skipped on the residue list itself. A round-3 reviewer re-ran all five and returned the correction; the other four reproduce exactly. The route is the next change to `tools/hooks/common.py`: a refactor can remove any of them and the guard's own suite reports nothing, so a leaf whose Bash command is shaped to the removed spelling reads a credential home and carries the material into the `substep` it is trying to report done. Two of the five are cheap to witness (i and v are one constructed command each); (ii) needs a command that reaches the truncation at all, which the reviewer probed and could not construct from the existing rows — that one may be dead code rather than an unwitnessed mechanism, and the census question is which. NOTE the measurement's scope: green was read over `test_hooks_common.py` only, so a guard mutant killed by `test_hooks_cli.py` or another file is not excluded for (ii)-(v); (i) was checked across the three hook test files.
- **The claude read-side preflight gate has no required-check-name set, and the roster check has no launch-time backstop** (2026-08-22, low — measured, surfaced by the security review of the issue #71 branch; ATTEMPTED and backed out, with the measurement below as the reason). `_preflight_allows_agent_launch` / `_validate_preflight_payload` enumerate required check NAMES only for codex (`CODEX_REQUIRED_LAUNCH_CHECKS`). Every claude check is instead ANDed into `can_launch_agents` at the moment it is probed, which covers documents this code produces but says nothing about a `preflight.json` produced by OLDER code and read back by `_require_preflight_launchable` — on a resume, or with the live probe disabled. Such a document carries no `claude_leaf_tool_roster_classified` row at all, and `status=pass` alone launches a run whose leaf tool set was never measured. That matters more for this check than for its siblings because it has no launch-time backstop: the conductor cannot see a roster, only the endpoint can. The mirror (`CLAUDE_REQUIRED_LAUNCH_CHECKS` = the four claude checks, required PRESENT and not-false — `not False` rather than `is True`, because two of the rows are legitimately `null` on the advisory-skip path) was written and MEASURED: it took the suite from 5120 passed (the figure at the commit where this was measured) to **56 failed**, because that many fixtures build a claude preflight payload without those rows. Fixtures are a proxy for the older documents the gate would refuse, so the blast radius is the finding — a change of this reach belongs in its own branch, with the fixtures migrated deliberately, not folded into the branch that made the read side load-bearing.
- **Three `test_orchestration_runtime.py` rows are green only because the operator has a real `~/.codex/auth.json`** (2026-09-02, low — measured by a round-3 reviewer of the issue #84 branch, in the same environment sweep that branch's own entry above asks for; recorded rather than pulled in). `OrchestrationMetaAndJudgeHookTests::test_record_launch_and_terminal_record_agent_run_update_session_run_index` and `PreflightLiveProbeTtlTests::{test_record_launch_re_probes_after_ttl_expiry,test_record_launch_skips_probe_on_second_call_within_ttl}` fail under a synthetic `$HOME` with `RuntimeError: record-launch sandbox enforcement failed: Codex auth.json not found for isolated home`, and pass the moment the synthetic home is given a `.codex/auth.json` — so it is the home's CONTENTS, not its depth or location. `docs/RUNBOOK.md` documents `codex login` as an operator step and nothing documents it as a SUITE prerequisite. Two costs, and the second is the one that matters: on a fresh machine or in CI the three are red for a reason belonging to no change, which is the delta-comparison problem this file's neighbouring entry exists to remove; and they currently pass by BORROWING a credential rather than by witnessing the enforcement, so what they pin about the sandbox auth gate is unmeasured. The fix is a fixture that builds the auth file the way `_built_home` builds a `$HOME`; whether the gate should also be witnessed against an ABSENT credential is the question to answer with it.
- **[CLOSED 2026-08-25, measured negative] `Grep`'s `glob` / `type` filters cannot leave `path`** (filed 2026-08-25, medium). The argument for validating `Glob`'s `pattern` on that branch was that the `--tools` allowlist hands a leaf `Glob` for the first time on a CLI whose default roster omits it. The same is true of `Grep`, and `docs/HOOKS.md` carries forward, unchanged, that its unvalidated `glob` / `type` filters "only narrow the search within `path`". That sentence predates the allowlist and has not been re-measured against the tool. It is now known that a neighbouring claim of the same shape — what the `Glob` TOOL can reach — was false when written as measured (bare `ripgrep` had been measured instead of the tool), so this one should not be trusted on its wording either. MEASURED the same day, by driving the real tool (a loopback stand-in answers turn one with a synthetic `tool_use`; the `tool_result` is read out of the next request body; unbilled; CLI 2.1.239): with `path=docs`, `glob="../../outside/*"` returns "No files found", an ABSOLUTE `glob` returns "No files found", and a plain `Grep` under the same `path` returns the two files that are there — so the filters narrow what is already enumerated under `path` and cannot re-root. **No hole; the residue is closed as measured rather than left open.** `Grep`'s `path` itself does escape and is validated already. Recorded here rather than deleted because the dismissing sentence in `docs/HOOKS.md` had stood unmeasured across the change that first put `Grep` in a leaf's hands, and the next person to widen the leaf's tool set should see what it took to retire it.
- **The `Glob` pattern check now DEPENDS on a measured vendor behaviour, and nothing re-measures it** (2026-08-25, medium — a deliberate narrowing, recorded so the premise is re-checkable rather than forgotten). Issue #71's allowlist hands a leaf `Glob`, whose `pattern` can name a place outside the tool's `path`. The check refuses a pattern beginning with `/` or with `~` — `/` because that is the one shape MEASURED to reach outside — driven against the real tool (a loopback stand-in answers turn one with a synthetic `tool_use`; the `tool_result` is read out of the next request body; unbilled; CLI 2.1.239) in a saturated fixture whose every candidate ancestor holds a marked file — and whose `HOME` is the fixture base, so the `~` rows are saturated too: `<base>/secret/*`, `/<base>/secret/*`, `<base>/{secret,outside}/*`, `<base>/secret/../secret/*` and `<base>/[s]ecret/*` read what they name, while every relative spelling (`../`, `../../`, `../../../`, `*/../../`, `sub/../../`, a brace with an absolute alternative in either order, a symlinked directory and file, `~/…`, `$HOME/…`, a leading space, a leading tab) returns nothing. **If a future CLI resolves `..`, follows a symlink, or expands a brace alternative before testing `isAbsolute`, the relative escape reopens and nothing in this repository notices** — the preflight roster check measures WHICH tools a leaf gets, not what one of them can reach, and `test_a_relative_pattern_is_not_refused_however_it_is_spelled` will still pass, because it asserts the current, deliberate non-refusal. The earlier design judged every spelling at its landing place and did not depend on the vendor; it was withdrawn because it defended reads that cannot happen and was where most of issue #71's eleven review rounds found defects (an escape it missed, an over-refusal it caused, an infinite loop in its brace expander, and four claims about the tool that were measurements of something else). To re-measure: **`python3 .claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change/scripts/measure_claude_tool.py`**, which is committed for exactly this purpose. Each row DECLARES whether it must read or must be inert, the script compares and exits non-zero on any disagreement, and `tools/tests/test_measure_claude_tool.py` pins the case list's coverage, the fixture's saturation, the result detector (both directions) and the verdict. An INERT row that reads is the end of the premise; a CONTROL row that reads nothing means the measurement broke and nothing above it can be believed. (This entry used to point at a scratch path under `/tmp`, which `AGENTS.md` §Canonical record placement says is not a record at all — and three other places asserted that TODO carried the harness while it carried a pointer to a file that had never been in this repository.) Bare `ripgrep` and Python's `glob` have each produced a wrong answer to this question; the harness drives the tool.
- **Issue #71's narrowed `Glob` pattern check keeps three accepted residues, none of them an escape** (2026-08-25, low — recorded here because this repository's convention puts residue in TODO, and until now these lived only in code comments and commit messages). (1) **An absolute brace pattern spanning two granted roots is refused although it reads**: `<repo>/{docs,sub}/*` has the literal prefix `<repo>/{docs,sub}` — `{` is not in `_GLOB_META_RE`, deliberately, because the Bash extractor that shares it resolves against the filesystem where a brace is not a wildcard — so it is under neither grant. Closing it needs the brace expander round 12 deleted, whose four guards were where three review rounds found defects. Absent from the corpus (no `SKILL` or prompt template directs a leaf to an absolute brace `Glob`), and the refusal names the working alternative: pass the directory as `path`. (2) **A `~` pattern is refused although it is inert** — measured, the tool reads nothing through it, since it asks `isAbsolute`, which `~` is not. Kept because `_glob_literal_prefix` already returns the expanded location, so the condition costs one character; the refusal text says so rather than calling it absolute. (3) ~~**The `.strip()` deletion generalises past its measurement**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-25 as never-open.** The entry said a TAB was allowed "without having been sampled", and the harness committed in the SAME commit already carried `"\t/etc/hostname"` in its default list; re-run under the rewritten harness (a saturated `"\t<base>/secret/*"`), a leading tab reads nothing, exactly like the leading space. The residue was sampled before it was filed as unsampled — a claim about my own commit's contents that reading `git show --stat` would have settled. What remains true and is worth keeping: the SPACE and the TAB are the only two whitespace prefixes anyone has asked about, and `tools/tests/test_measure_claude_tool.py` now pins both into the default case list so neither can be dropped from the re-check. All three are refusals or non-refusals of shapes that read nothing; none admits a read the manifest does not grant.
- **A reviewer measured a different built-in roster than this branch records, on the same CLI version and host, and the disagreement is unexplained** (2026-08-25, low — measured, unreconciled). `tools/orchestration_runtime.py`'s `CLAUDE_LEAF_TOOLS` block records 23 built-ins unfiltered and 19 under the old denylist argv, with fifteen of those judged by no `PreToolUse` matcher and named. A round-13 reviewer measured 27 and 23 on CLI 2.1.239, the extra four being `TaskCreate`, `TaskGet`, `TaskList`, `TaskUpdate`; an earlier reviewer measured 24/20 with `Skill`. Re-taken under the LEAF's argv three times since: 23/19, no `Task*` beyond `TaskOutput`/`TaskStop`, no `Skill`. A round-15 reviewer re-took it independently with the production capture server over three argvs and got the same 23/19/6, so the recorded number is the reproducible one and the outliers are the unexplained side. Every request the CLI makes to the loopback stand-in was logged — two POSTs, no GET — so it is not a startup fetch that the stand-in refuses. **Operational consequence**: a `claude_leaf_tool_roster_classified` failure naming an unfamiliar built-in may be roster nondeterminism on one CLI version rather than a change to classify, and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §0-2's remedy does not admit that possibility. Re-measure with `.claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change/scripts/measure_claude_tool.py`-style capture before treating such a failure as a vendor change. The enumerated list is exhaustive only for the argv it names, which is what keeps it true.

- **The orchestration read audit (`access_logs/<agent_run_id>.jsonl`) is observational, not tamper-proof, and no rework is scheduled** (operator-confirmed 2026-06-26, low — future direction, not started). `docs/HOOKS.md` already states the threat model — the log is "best-effort observability, never an authorization step" and "every logging channel here is leaf-writable and forgeable" — and `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` §"Enforcement surface" repeats that hook read decisions are appended "best-effort, not an exhaustive record". What neither doc records is that this was a deliberate accept-for-now: (1) a leaf reading through a bwrap ro-bind mount hits `run-gate --gate orchestration_read` only when the path is outside the mount, so most reads never reach the gate that would log them; (2) the audit line itself is appended by the leaf's own `run-gate` subprocess inside the sandbox, over a path the per-file write grant makes leaf-writable, so a compromised leaf can omit or rewrite its own audit trail. Authorization is unaffected (`log_orchestration_read`'s allow/deny check runs independently and first), so this is an integrity-of-the-record gap, not an access-control one. Two directions were named and neither is started: (a) move the audit append out of the sandbox, host-side, which needs a gate-to-host round-trip channel that does not exist today; (b) remove the ro-bind direct-read path entirely and force every read through the gate (large, separate change). No issue tracks either.

- **Issue #112 landed the gate's finding-attribution rule; three things it does NOT close are recorded here** (2026-08-28). A `Generate.gate` finding whose subject is a file this repository authors now terminalizes on the first attempt instead of warm-resuming `generate.generate`. The INPUT SURFACE the fix rests on, enumerated so the next reviewer has something to break rather than a claim to trust: (a) the origin oracle is `Conductor._host_rendered_src_names`, which asks `_conductor_authors_runner` / `_conductor_authors_makefile` and adds no fourth spelling of a predicate that has already drifted once between the conductor and the validator; (b) the lint attribution's channel is the linter's own verdict over two isolated copies of `src/`, and the partition is TOTAL OVER THE TREE (every file under `src/`, at any depth, lands in exactly one probe directory, subdirectories reproduced), so no file-extension knowledge enters the neutral core and the union of the probes is the directory the full run scanned. **The first version was total over the FLAT LISTING only** and this entry described that version for one round: the linter recurses, so a finding below the top level was in the full run and in neither probe, and an ordinary leaf-repairable finding came back terminal as `lint_finding_unattributed`. Host membership is anchored to depth 1, because the host writes its two files at the top of `src/`; (c) the static attribution's channel is a violation SUBCLASS plus a dedicated exit code (5), constructed POSITIONALLY — the runner gates are handed nothing but the runner to talk about — and nothing anywhere parses a violation string to decide it; (d) both probe kinds pass no `command_log_path`, so the node's canonical command log stays the record of the gate proper.
  - **MEASURED, 2026-08-28**, that the lint partition preserves findings: fortitude 0.8.0, the declared invocation, over the real `src_20260827_002` tree of issue #110's node (`workspace/pipelines/component__dynamics_advection_diffusion_boundary_1d_periodic_copy__0.1.0/dynamics-advection-diffusion-boundary-1d-periodic-copy_20260827_001/`) — full = 3 findings, leaf-only = 2, host-only = 1, and the host probe, whose module imports are all absent from its directory, produced nothing of its own. **That premise is the whole basis of the lint arm**; a linter that resolved across files would break it, and nothing re-measures it automatically.
  - **RESIDUE 1 — the syntax checker cannot decide an unattributable finding, deliberately.** Its new leaf-only probe records `checkers.syntax.attribution` and routes nothing. The host-rendered runner `use`s the leaf-authored checks module, so a diagnostic printed at a runner line can be the LEAF having missed the fixed checks ABI — real, common, warm-repairable — and terminalizing it would convert a fixable defect into a permanent `fail_closed`. So issue #112's "an unattributable finding fails closed" is applied to lint, which can partition files, and not here. The residue is a finding caused by the runner ALONE; it is held at the source by the language backend's renderer test, which now compiles AND lints the rendered runner under this gate's exact flags and rule set. What the probe adds is that the residue is now MEASURED on every failure rather than assumed — the `unattributed_interaction` count is what a later change should decide on.
  - **RESIDUE 2 — the Makefile arm is not implemented.** `src/Makefile` is in the origin oracle (so the lint arm covers it), but the STATIC arm wraps only the runner gates. The Makefile gates (`_validate_fortran_makefile_src_dir`, `_validate_makefile_test_no_relink`, `_validate_makefile_test_invokes_cases`) still emit unwrapped violations, so a post_generate finding against the host-authored control file still warm-retries. It was left out because the predicate differs: `_conductor_authors_makefile` is strictly WIDER than `_conductor_authors_runner` (true for an `infrastructure` node and for a node with no infra dep), so the validator's `_execution_m3c_language` mirror is the WRONG oracle for it and would under-attribute — a fail-open. Closing it means extracting the control-file capability loop out of `_ir_m3c_language` into its own named predicate and giving it a row in the conductor-validator parity test. Small, but it is a predicate change, and keeping it out of the acceptance-test commit was deliberate.
  - **RESIDUE 3 — `lineage.json` is a fourth host-authored surface at post_generate and is not covered.** `_validate_pipeline_lineage_presence` flags a malformed `pipeline_id` / `node_key` binding in a file the conductor writes, on EVERY node rather than only an M3c one, so its attribution helper is a different one again. Same defect class, same warm-retry burn, no wrap today.
  - **RESIDUE 4 — the lint attribution probe's composite-preset branch is unwitnessed, and unreachable today.** `_attribute_lint_findings`'s `_probe` reads `out.get("runs") or [out]`, mirroring the main run's single-vs-composite normalization. Under a composite preset the probe's excerpt would come back empty, so a `lint_findings` warm retry would carry no diagnostics and `_read_repair_findings` would fall back to the full prompt. It cannot happen now: `registry.unsupported_reason("language", "mixed")` is non-None, so a mixed node is rejected at `Compile.static` before any of this runs (verified against the registry by two reviewers independently). Recorded rather than fixed, and recorded HERE rather than only in a commit message — `AGENTS.md` §Canonical record placement is explicit that a permanently-needed fact is not recorded until it lands in the repository, and an earlier version of the commit that closed this round claimed it was "recorded" when it was not.
  - **Decisions of the new machinery that are deliberately NOT pinned**, from a witness census run over every predicate, constant, table entry and branch the change adds. Each is a real decision whose mutation the suite does not notice; none decides a verdict, and all of them shape what an operator READS: the 50-line tails on the probe excerpts, the stdout+stderr concatenation, the whole-directory quote in the unattributed arm, the error-report fallback chain in the no-exit-status message, and the literal text of `HOST_AUTHORED_ARTIFACT_MARKER` (its PRESENCE in the message is pinned; the string is not, and by design it carries no decision). Marked rather than deleted, because deleting them would remove behaviour rather than close the gap. The census's fourth class — "killed only by the token ratchet" — came back EMPTY: no decision of this change has the ratchet as its only witness.
  - **Blast radius, measured, so it is not discovered later.** Sampling 45 of the archived source trees under `workspace*/` and re-running the declared lint invocation over the same partition the gate builds: 42 of the 45 have a FAILING host probe, because runners rendered before issue #111 carry an `! allow(C003)` directive the current renderer no longer emits. That is the intended semantics — those are real findings in a file no leaf can fix, and terminalizing beats burning the retry budget — but it means a future renderer lint regression terminalizes EVERY node on its first gate failure, and the only upstream guard is `DeclaredLintRuleHoldTest`, which says of itself that it is a sample over this module's IR fixtures and not a pin.
  - **Not verified: `ruff`.** This repository's review procedure asks for a ruff diff against `origin/main`; ruff is not installed on the machine this landed on (`python3 -m ruff` → no module), so the change went out on the suite and `compileall` alone. Stated rather than skipped silently.

- **`docs/workflow/phases/phase_04_validate.md` contradicts itself on how many `--stage pre_judge` exit codes are answered before violations are classified.** Line 20 says "Three dedicated exit codes (3 `static_frontend_unavailable`, 4 `stale_dependency_ir`, 5 `host_authored_artifact_violation`)"; line 81 of the same file says "**Two** exit codes are answered before the bullets are read at all" and lists only 3 and 4. `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md`, `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §Validate and `_post_judge_inproc`'s `terminal_category` map all say three, so line 81 is the likely stale one — but this was not established, only observed. Found by a review round on #114 (2026-08-28); pre-existing, untouched by that branch, and deliberately not fixed there because deciding which sentence is right is a separate reading of the gate.

- **The operator-private root is spelled four times and `operator_secret_root()` is only one of them** (2026-09-01, issue #132, split out of issue #127 — pre-existing, put out of scope by the approved rename plan, recorded here because until now it lived only in K3's commit message and a reviewer found it independently). `tools/hooks/common.py:1590` `operator_secret_root()` is the spelling the READ GUARD has. Three writers build the same path from `Path.home()` instead: `tools/orchestration_runtime.py:19496` (`init_orchestration`) and `:10037` (`dismiss_violation`) for `operator_tokens/`, and `tools/run_workflow.py:1580` (`_claim_lock_path`) for `start_claims/`. `tools/hooks/common.py:1593-1596` states the rule for the SIBLING constant and says why — the side that CREATES and the side that FORBIDS reading must resolve the location identically — and the token and claim paths do not follow it. Nothing couples the four, so moving the root is four coordinated edits and missing any one splits a writer from the guard silently. **That is not hypothetical: the rename produced exactly that split**, which the migration item below records — the guard moved to `~/.atmofab` at K3 while the data was still under `~/.met-dsl`, deliberately and for one migration step's duration. **That window is CLOSED** (step 1 done, 2026-09-02): the four spellings agree on disk again, so what remains is the coupling, not a live gap. The demonstration is what makes this worth fixing rather than evidence of a hole open now. **Severity, stated rather than assumed**: a leaf gains nothing from either half being wrong — it cannot edit the tree and the operator owns the machine — so this is a maintenance hazard on the operator's side, not a `leaf shortcut`. The fix is to import `operator_secret_root` at the three writers and add the test that the four agree; it touches the capability gate's token path, so it is its own change under `.claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change`.
  - **DONE (PR #140, 2026-09-02), together with the item below.** `tools/hooks/common.py` now owns `operator_tokens_root()` beside `operator_secret_root()` and `workflow_homes_root()`, and the three writers IMPORT them: `init_orchestration` and `dismiss_violation` go through `_operator_token_path()` (a thin alias, in the shape `_workflow_homes_root` already had), and `_claim_lock_path` through `_start_claims_root()`, whose default is `operator_secret_root() / "start_claims"`. One line number this item cites had drifted and is corrected here: `init_orchestration`'s spelling was at `:19499`, not `:19496` (measured on `origin/main`). The other three were RIGHT — `tools/hooks/common.py:1590`, `tools/orchestration_runtime.py:10037` and `tools/run_workflow.py:1580` are each the exact line of the `".atmofab"` spelling. An earlier version of this DONE bullet claimed `:1590` had drifted too and offered `:1586-1590` as the repair; that was wrong in both halves — the function body runs 1588-1590 and the item was citing the spelling, not the definition. Found by a round-1 reviewer. **Evidence, both RED on `e0bae3d`**: `test_the_three_writers_and_the_guard_resolve_one_root` drives the real `init_orchestration` / `dismiss_violation` / `_claim_lock_path` / `protected_host_read_roots` under one patched `_home_dir` — at `e0bae3d` the token landed in the operator's real store, the claims root resolved to the operator's real `~/.atmofab/start_claims`, and the guard had no token-store entry; and `test_the_dot_atmofab_constant_is_spelled_once` requires the bare `".atmofab"` constant to appear exactly ONCE across `tools/` (minus `tools/tests`) and `mcp_servers/`, and in `tools/hooks/common.py` — the file set and the count, not the function name, so hoisting the literal to a module constant in that file is allowed and a second spelling anywhere, INCLUDING inside the same function, is not. (Two earlier versions of this sentence said "in exactly one function"; the first matched an instrument that pinned the function name and refused that hoist, the second matched one that counted scopes and could not see a second spelling in one scope. Both were found in review and both are fixed.) At `e0bae3d` the census finds four: `operator_secret_root`, `init_orchestration`, `dismiss_violation`, `_claim_lock_path`. Both in `tools/tests/test_operator_private_root.py`. The second is a BOUND ON GROWTH and not a detector — an f-string, the path inside a longer string, and a rename of the constant are all out of its reach, and its docstring says so. The token store gained `ATMOFAB_OPERATOR_TOKENS_ROOT` in the process; the claims root stays out of `protected_host_read_roots` deliberately (rule 1-e: a 0-byte advisory flock gets a leaf no closer to reporting a substep done).

- **Left open by PR #140, filed rather than fixed: the private-root resolvers are not TOTAL for a `~account` override.** All THREE call `Path(override).expanduser()`, which raises `RuntimeError: Could not determine home directory` when the override is spelled `~someone` and no such account exists: `tools/hooks/common.py::operator_tokens_root`, `workflow_homes_root`, and `tools/run_workflow.py::_start_claims_root`. **The third is CLOSED at its caller and left open at the resolver**: `_exclusive_claim` catches `(OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError)` around `_claim_lock_path` so the claim degrades to "proceed" as its contract says (PR #140 round 4 — the `expanduser()` was added on that branch and the escape was a regression against a sentence the same branch wrote). So the note below about the hook layer converting a raise into a BLOCK, and the candidate fix in `tools/hooks/cli.py`, apply to the FIRST TWO ONLY. Whoever picks this up must not read it as two sites. "Closed at its caller" means the driver no longer crashes; it does NOT mean the operator is told — the degradation is silent, like the `fcntl` and unwritable-home arms beside it, so a mistyped claim root costs the cold-start serialisation with no signal. That is `_exclusive_claim`'s standing contract rather than something PR #140 introduced, and it is written down here because "closed" would otherwise read as more than it is. "Closed at its caller" means the driver no longer crashes; it does NOT mean the operator is told — the degradation is silent, like the `fcntl` and unwritable-home arms beside it, so a mistyped claim root costs the cold-start serialisation with no signal. That is `_exclusive_claim`'s standing contract rather than something PR #140 introduced, and it is written down here because "closed" would otherwise read as more than it is. Measured 2026-09-02 at PR #140's HEAD, and the same is true of `workflow_homes_root` at `e0bae3d` — the branch adds a second copy of the shape, not the shape. What follows is FAIL-CLOSED: `tools/hooks/cli.py` turns a raising hook into `_decision_error` and BLOCKS, so **a leaf gains nothing** (rule 1-e) and this is an operator-facing wart, not a hole — every Bash call in that shell is refused with `hook entrypoint failure: Could not determine home directory` and no mention of which variable caused it. Not fixed because the fix is a DECISION, not a `try`: making the resolver total means saying what an unresolvable override should resolve TO, and every answer either guards a path nobody writes to or silently guards a different tree from the one the writer refuses. The creation-side refusal cannot help — it never runs, because the resolver raises first. Candidate shape if this is ever picked up: catch in `tools/hooks/cli.py`'s entrypoint and name the offending `ATMOFAB_*` variable in the refusal, leaving the resolvers alone. That shape covers the two hooks resolvers; the claims one already has its answer at its own caller, and a fix that changes `expanduser` itself would have to keep `_exclusive_claim`'s degradation working. Found by a round-1 reviewer on PR #140.

- **The test suite writes operator tokens into the REAL operator-private root, and the guard that was built to stop exactly that covers only half of it** (2026-09-01, issue #133, split out of issue #127 — found while doing the rename, pre-existing, deliberately not fixed there). `tools/tests/conftest.py::_forbid_isolated_homes_in_operator_secret_root` fails any test about to resolve the isolated-homes root to the real root, and `ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_HOMES_ROOT` gives `homes/` a redirect to resolve to. `operator_tokens/` has neither: `tools/orchestration_runtime.py:19496` (`init_orchestration`) and `:10037` (`dismiss_violation`) each build `Path.home() / ".atmofab" / "operator_tokens"` directly, with no environment override and nothing in `conftest.py` watching, so a `pytest` run over `tools/tests/` creates the root if it is absent and leaves one file per fixture orchestration id in it. **And the redirect `homes/` does have is not the whole story either** — the guard is a `pytest` fixture wrapping `runtime._workflow_homes_root`, so it sees nothing driven OUTSIDE the runner, which is exactly what a review round does when it drives a function in a scratch tree. Measured 2026-09-01: `~/.met-dsl/homes/` holds THREE entries, and the third is `rg1` (`owner.json#repo_root = /tmp/tmp4ymg3k6z`, 2026-08-27 18:46, with a full `claude/` home) — the fixture id at `tools/tests/test_orchestration_runtime.py:16331`, landed after the guard did. So "covers only half" is the shape of the gap and not its extent; both halves leak, by different routes. MEASURED 2026-09-01 by set arithmetic over the three roots that now exist. `~/.met-forge/operator_tokens` and `~/.atmofab/operator_tokens` — neither of which any workflow ever wrote, only this rename's suite runs — hold the same 249 filenames, and that set is a SUBSET of `~/.met-dsl/operator_tokens`, which holds 256. So 249 of the live root's 256 are suite residue. The remaining 7 are 6 genuine run tokens plus `o.txt`, written by the guard measurement K9 records. The real `orch_<YYYYMMDD>T<hhmmss>Z_<hex>` shape does NOT separate the two groups: 7 filenames have it, and one of those 7 (`orch_20260812T010203Z_ab12cd34.txt`) is inside the 249 — a fixture that spells itself like a live id, which is why the shape is a hint and the set difference is the measurement. **What it is not**: it is not a `leaf shortcut` and not a route to a wrong certification. A fixture id is not a live orchestration, `dismiss_violation` compares the token it reads against the one the operator holds, and the writer is the operator's own `pytest`, not a leaf — under `AGENTS.md` §Development premises the leaf gains nothing here. **What it is**: the suite mutating a directory it declares off-limits, growing without bound, and — as step 1 of the migration item below records — re-creating `~/.atmofab` behind the operator's back between the `mv` and the next run. The fix is to give `operator_tokens/` the redirect `homes/` already has and to widen `_forbid_isolated_homes_in_operator_secret_root` to the whole root rather than one subdirectory; that touches the capability gate's token path, so it is its own change with its own review (`.claude/skills/atmofab-enforcement-change`), not a rider on a rename. **The three roots this item measures over no longer exist as three** — `~/.met-dsl` and `~/.met-forge` were removed by the migration item's steps 1 and 7 (2026-09-02), so the set arithmetic above is a dated record and not re-takeable; the defect it measured is unchanged, and one `pytest` run still re-creates `~/.atmofab` if it is absent.

When the running orchestration OWNS that home the last two stay BLOCK, because `protected_host_read_roots` still resolves its own entry through `orchestration_meta.json#claude_workflow_home`, which still names the old root. Stating it unconditionally overstates the gap in the direction that argues for this step, so it is stated with its condition. The row in the defended set is the last: a PAST run's leaf transcript — a different `<oid>`, hence always ALLOW — whose reading `docs/workflow/WORKFLOW_CORE.md` forbids because it shortens a leaf's route to reporting its task done, and which `tools/hooks/common.py:1698` says would leave no trace in any artifact. **This is a reduction from two layers to one, not an open hole** — bwrap is mandatory (`_probe_bwrap_sandbox`: a host failing the probe is unsupported and the run fails closed) and `render_bwrap_command` is `--clearenv` plus explicit binds with no `--ro-bind / /`, so `~/.met-dsl` is not in a leaf's mount namespace; MEASURED as read of the bind construction, not by driving a real launch. The hook layer's own docstring nevertheless calls itself the closure of "the only other route", and this step is what restores it.

MEASURED again at 23:01, after ONE `python3 -m pytest tools/tests/` in this checkout: it existed, mode 755, `operator_tokens/` with 249 fixture files — the suite created it, which is the defect recorded above, and it will do so again. So the two records agree and neither is an instruction: **stat the target immediately before acting.** `mv ~/.met-dsl ~/.atmofab` is correct ONLY on a target that does not exist; on one that does, `mv` neither fails nor merges — it NESTS, exit 0, leaving `~/.atmofab/.met-dsl/` with `homes/`, `operator_tokens/` and `start_claims/` one level too deep, `operator_secret_root()` and `workflow_homes_root()` reading an empty new root, and every absolute path steps 5 and 6 repoint pointing at nothing. (Verified in a scratch directory, not reasoned about.) So: if the target does not exist, `mv ~/.met-dsl ~/.atmofab`; if it does, move `homes/` and `start_claims/` across, copy only the token files absent from the target — the target's copy wins on every collision, because `dismiss_violation` reads `~/.atmofab/operator_tokens/<oid>.txt` for the RUNNING orchestration and an old-root file overwriting a live one breaks that gate — then remove the old root once it is empty. Either way finish with `chmod 700 ~/.atmofab`: `~/.met-dsl` is mode 755 and its three subdirectories are 700, so the outer directory is the one still to close. Do it with `pgrep -af run_workflow.py` empty. TWO mechanisms re-create the root, not one. `tools/orchestration_runtime.py:19496` builds `Path.home() / ".atmofab" / "operator_tokens"` and `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`s it with NO environment override — unlike the homes root, which `ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_HOMES_ROOT` redirects and `tools/tests/conftest.py::_forbid_isolated_homes_in_operator_secret_root` guards [closed by #132/#133, PR #140: `ATMOFAB_OPERATOR_TOKENS_ROOT` exists now, and the guard — renamed `_forbid_anything_in_operator_secret_root` — covers all three resolvers; the measurement above stands as the record of what was true then] — and that is the one the suite reaches. `tools/run_workflow.py:1580` builds `~/.atmofab/start_claims` the same way and `:1604` `mkdir`s it, so a cold real run re-creates the root too; that one the `pgrep` above does cover, and `ATMOFAB_START_CLAIM_ROOT` relocates it. **And the `chmod 700` does not survive either of them**: `:19501` chmods `operator_tokens/` and `tools/run_workflow.py:1606` chmods `start_claims/`, while `_require_secure_home_ancestor`'s ancestor list records `~/.atmofab — mode not forced` (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py:16562`), so a re-created root comes back at the umask default — measured 2026-09-01, all three private roots on this machine are 755 with 700 subdirectories. Re-run the `chmod` after any deletion. `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §"The operator-private root" is canonical for the contents.
  2. `gh repo rename atmofab -R seiya/met-dsl`, then `git remote set-url origin git@github.com:seiya/atmofab && git fetch`. GitHub redirects the old URL, so a second machine only needs a fresh `clone` of the new one. This step is also what makes `docs/design/codex_backend_support_plan.md:6` resolve: it links `https://github.com/seiya/atmofab/issues/13`, which is dead until the rename happens.
  3. `~/.claude.json`: copy the `projects["/home/seiya/met-dsl"]` entry to a `"/home/seiya/atmofab"` key keeping `hasTrustDialogAccepted: true`, and point `githubRepoPaths` at `"seiya/atmofab": ["/home/seiya/atmofab"]`. An untrusted checkout silently drops the project layer's `permissions.allow` (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` §Hook trust), so the symptom of skipping this is a session that merely asks more often, not one that fails.

With every session closed, `mv /home/seiya/met-dsl /home/seiya/atmofab`, then `git worktree repair` in the moved checkout — the move invalidates every linked worktree's `gitdir` pointer, and the symptom is "not a git repository" with no recorded cause. Optionally rename `~/.claude/projects/-home-seiya-met-dsl` to `-home-seiya-atmofab` to carry session history across (`/resume` shows whether it took); `~/.cache/claude-cli-nodejs/-home-seiya-met-dsl/` can be deleted. **Rename any `METDSL_*` export in a shell rc to `ATMOFAB_*` rather than leaving it.** An earlier version of this item called the old exports "inert", which understates what inert means here: the knob's VALUE is lost and the setting silently reverts to its default, with nothing reporting it. One of them feeds a gate — `ATMOFAB_SYNTAX_COMPILERS` decides which compilers the `Generate.gate` syntax stage runs (`mcp_servers/build_runtime_server.py:1373`) — and `ATMOFAB_WORKFLOW_HOMES_ROOT` relocates the durable homes root that the read guard's protected set is derived from. `ATMOFAB_REQUIRE_CODEX_HOOKS_FEATURE` defaults to `"1"`, so an operator who had opted out gets the requirement back, which is the fail-closed direction. MEASURED 2026-09-01: this operator's `~/.zshrc`, `~/.zshenv`, `~/.zprofile`, `~/.bashrc` and `~/.profile` export none of them, so the step is a no-op here today; it is written down because this item is the migration record and the failure is silent.

Measured 2026-09-01: two orchestrations under `workspace/orchestrations/` record `/home/seiya/.met-dsl/homes/<oid>/claude`, which step 1 moves out from under them. Rewrite both to `/home/seiya/.atmofab/...`. (`workspace/` is untracked, so this is operator-local.) **The third points the other way and step 4 is what breaks it**: each durable home's own `~/.atmofab/homes/<oid>/owner.json#repo_root` records the CHECKOUT, and measured 2026-09-01 both real homes hold `/home/seiya/met-dsl`. Rewrite both to `/home/seiya/atmofab` after step 4. **Do not sweep `workspace/` for the old path**: measured 2026-09-01 it appears in 41 files there (~700 occurrences) — `sandbox_profiles/*.json`, `launches/*.response.json`, `agents/*/dialogs/child.response.json` — and all but the two `orchestration_meta.json` values are inert RECORDS of what a past run did, which is what they are for. Rewriting them would falsify the record without repairing anything; the resolution route the audit reads is `orchestration_meta.json#claude_workflow_home` alone (`skills/workflow-audit-claude/SKILL.md`). A THIRD `homes/` entry, `rg1`, is suite residue with `repo_root = /tmp/tmp4ymg3k6z` (see the item above); step 1 carries it across and nothing can repair it, so `prune_workflow_homes.py` will report it `refused:unverifiable_owner` for ever — delete it rather than repointing it, and do that BEFORE step 1 so it is not moved at all. Driven with `tools/prune_workflow_homes.py::inspect_entry` in a scratch tree, an entry that reads `deletable / pass` before the checkout moves reads `refused:unverifiable_owner` after — fail-closed, so nothing is lost silently, but the remedy `docs/RUNBOOK.md` offers is `--allow-unverifiable`, the one override that skips the terminal-status re-verification entirely, and it would be aimed at exactly the two homes holding the pre-rename leaf transcripts. One `sed` here is what keeps the operator off it.

Measured 2026-09-01: `~/.met-dsl/homes/orch_20260827T105454Z_99f63b7e/claude/projects/-home-seiya-met-dsl` and the same under `orch_20260827T165705Z_af3800b5`. Step 4 mentions renaming the operator's OWN `~/.claude/projects/-home-seiya-met-dsl` and says nothing about these; both are needed.

It survives only where this rename talks ABOUT itself: this step, the suite-residue item above, and the messages of K10, K11 and K12 — which is a record, not a leak, and is why `~/.met-forge` needs a step of its own rather than an explanation. Its test suite nevertheless created the real root — measured 2026-09-01: `~/.met-forge` (mode 755, mtime 19:29:04) holds `operator_tokens/` and nothing else, 249 files, and that set is a subset of `~/.met-dsl/operator_tokens` — including the one file whose name has the real `orch_<YYYYMMDD>T<hhmmss>Z_<hex>` shape (`orch_20260812T010203Z_ab12cd34.txt`), which is what makes that one a fixture rather than a live token: the directory was written by nothing but the suite. So it holds nothing unique and `rm -r ~/.met-forge` loses nothing. It is the same defect as step 1's, seen from the other side.
  8. **Update any operator script that parses `run_workflow.py`'s stdout envelope.** K5 renamed two keys of it, `metdsl_workflow_mode` -> `atmofab_workflow_mode` and `metdsl_workflow_exec_mode` -> `atmofab_workflow_exec_mode` (`tools/run_workflow.py:3953-3954`). Nothing in the tree reads them — no test, no document, no other module — so nothing here would have caught either the rename or a failure to rename, and a script outside the repository breaks silently. Both are also redundant with fields already in the same dict (`atmofab_workflow_mode` is unconditionally `"1"`; `atmofab_workflow_exec_mode` always equals the sibling `workflow_mode`), so deleting them is the better answer than pinning them — a separate change, recorded below.

What it reaches that the suite does not is the preflight roster probe's `atmofab-roster-home-` temp prefix (`tools/orchestration_runtime.py:17489`), whose only other occurrence in the tree is this sentence. An earlier version of this item paired it with `ATMOFAB_HOOK_REPO_ROOT`, which is wrong: that name IS covered, by `tools/tests/test_hooks_cli.py:60` driving `python3 -m tools.hooks.cli` as a real subprocess with it set, and by `tools/tests/test_suite_environment_isolation.py:380` anchoring it to `tools/hooks/cli.py`.
  - **Accepted loss, not a defect**: an `orchestration` started before the rename cannot be `--resume`d. It CAN still be audited — steps 5 and 6 exist to keep exactly that, and step 6's own headline says the audit trail is what is lost without it — so this bullet is not a licence to skip them; the accepted loss is `--resume` alone. `workspace/orchestrations/*` records the old `repo_root`, and `orchestration_meta.json#claude_workflow_home` the old home; renaming the checkout would break `--resume` on its own, so the namespace change costs nothing further. `tools/prune_workflow_homes.py` classifies such a home `refused:unverifiable_owner`.
